Archiving 기본 프로젝트 is 403 `protected_project`; `archived: false` on it is a no-op. Write guards on an archived project (refusing new sessions) are not built. `DELETE /api/projects/{name}` answers 204 and pushes `project_deleted` `{name}`; it deletes only a project with no sessions, channels, schedules or teams, and its computer assignments and browser egress row go with it in one transaction. A project that still holds records is 409 `project_not_empty` with the counts `{sessions, channels, schedules, teams}`, 기본 프로젝트 is 403 `protected_project`, an unknown name 404 `not_found`. | **FR-L27.8** | *[provisional, 2026-11-13, #487: the audit asks that the client use the transfer API]* Outside demo mode the 파일 screen's `TransferService` is the server's transfer API (`/api/transfers*`) through `ember-client` (`ember/client/src/transfers.rs`, `ember/app/src/ui/files_service.rs`). It shows the server computer's offers, transfers, received files, history and received-files folder, and this device's own sends. A send reads each file's size and SHA-256, offers them, and streams them in chunks without holding a file in memory; after a dropped connection it continues from the receiver's verified offset (`FR-F5`). Pause, resume and cancel go to the receiver and the stream follows its state (`FR-F4`); an offer nobody answers is withdrawn after five minutes (`FR-F3`). Every failure is a fixed Korean sentence in 합니다체, never the server's text. A receiver is the server computer (`/api/transfers`) or an online registered computer reached over the transport, through the server's relay to its node (`TransferTarget::Computer`, `FR-F14`); the app reads that computer's list (`transfers_at`) for its rows' progress, sends pause, resume and cancel there, and names the receiving computer in the history ("받는 곳"), because the server's history holds only its own computer's transfers. Phones and other devices that are not registered computers, computers registered by plain HTTP, and offline computers are refused with their reason before anything is offered. The server's `InsecureTarget` and `NoSpace` answers are shown as fixed sentences, with the reason in the log. *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, #773: the app side of the relay)* The app accepts its own offer to the server computer (the sender is the owner who would answer it); a computer's node accepts the owner's offer by itself (`FR-F15`), so the app never accepts for a computer. Offers from other devices still ask. "종단 간 암호화" is shown only when the server is reached over the peer-to-peer transport, not over plain HTTP. Progress is read by polling (1 s while something moves, 5 s otherwise) until the server pushes transfer events. When the server stops answering, the screen keeps the lists it last read and only sending is blocked. A transfer row follows the mockup: the subtitle is the direction ("받는 중", "보내는 중") in every state, and the meta line says the state (a paused row: "일시 정지 · 1.7 MB 남음"). Notices are the toolkit's `Message`, which has no placement yet, so they show at the top end and not at the mockup's bottom centre (TODO(compose-rust#577)). A history row names the other side as in the mockup ("보낸 곳" for a received file, "받는 곳" for a sent one) and is grouped under "최근" (the last 24 hours) or "이전", because the app has no local time zone to name a calendar day with. The server's own computer is shown as "이 서버" (`NFR-L4`). | Client integration tests against the real server router (`ember/client/tests/fr_f_transfer_client.rs`): an offer waits for the answer, then streams and matches its hash; a connection cut at half resumes past the cut point; an upload that gave up continues when called again; cancel stops it within 2 s and leaves no partial file; pause holds every byte; decline, expiry, the size limit and receiving off each end with their reason. Unit tests `fr_l27_8_*` in `ember/app/src/ui/files_service.rs` map a snapshot to the screen lists, refuse a send with its reason, and plan each action only where the state allows; for a computer they choose the target, refuse phones, plain HTTP and offline computers, show `InsecureTarget` and `NoSpace` as fixed sentences, follow the node's list without taking the server's same-numbered transfer, and name the receiving computer in the history. Not yet verified on a real display or between two real computers. |

| **FR-L31** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D25, D32 and D38, 2026-10-25; separate window: user, 2026-11-05; function and design: user, 2026-11-06: "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Ember 에디터 screen**: IDE 열기, Ember 에디터 opens, for the current project, a separate IDE window whose **functions equal Code-OSS (OSE)** (row by row in `docs/design/EDITOR-PARITY.md`) and whose **design is the mockup** `.scratch/mockup-v8/editor.html` with `editor.css`: the island layout of D25 (rounded island panels for the code area, tabs and tool windows) in the light and dark tokens of the mockup. It is drawn by `dioxus-compose` (`INTENT.md` D9a; D23 decides only how the chrome is drawn, with the vendored Code-OSS CSS where the mockup uses the same widget, and not how it looks), with the editing area a `compose-rust` `CodeEditor` widget driven by `ember/editor`. including 열린 파일 찾기 to expand the active file's ancestor directories and blue `.mod` highlighting when `git/status` reports the file modified), file tabs (an unsaved dot, close, and blue `.vc-mod` highlighting when `git/status` reports the file modified), breadcrumbs under the tabs, the code area (and when no file is open, the `.ed-empty` state showing the active keymap's find and project tool-window shortcut hints), a bottom tool window with 터미널, 실행, 문제 (marker rows with caret jump on click, capped at 200 visible items with a load-more button, #448) and 출력 (extension output channels via `MainThreadOutputService`, workbench log capped at 500 lines, channel selector and clear button, #448), extension notifications via `MainThreadMessageService` (`$showMessage`), and a status bar (agent state, clickable "N행 M열" button opening 줄로 이동, LF, encoding, "{tab_width}칸", branch). **Accessibility and keyboard navigation (#302)**: the explorer tree (`role="tree"`) and the tab strip (`role="tablist"`) use roving `tabindex` with no duplicate tab stop between the container and its active child (`tree_roving_tabindex`, `tab_strip_roving_tabindex`, `tabindex="-1"` on per-tab close buttons); pressing `Delete` or `Backspace` on a tab closes a clean tab and, on a dirty tab (`TabKeyOutcome::RefuseDirty`), asks the unsaved-changes question of `FR-L31.5` instead of discarding unsaved changes; tool-window stripe buttons carry `aria-pressed`; problem rows (`role="listitem"`) are focusable (`tabindex="0"`) and activate on `Enter` or `Space`; output and terminal log areas carry `role="log"` and accessible names; and every icon button, input, and action row in the Search, Commit, Git, Diff, and Conflict panels carries an explicit `aria-label` or `aria-pressed` attribute. | **FR-L31.6** | *[provisional, #451; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and `INTENT.md` D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Save states, external changes, lost connection and auto-save in the Ember 에디터 window**, as Code-OSS shows them. (1) *Save failure.* A failed write keeps the edits, posts a notice and shows a red bar over the code area of that document naming the file and a Korean reason (`file_failure_reason`, never the server's own text) with 다시 시도 and 닫기; the bar stays until a save succeeds, the document is reverted, or 닫기. (2) *Change on disk.* A clean buffer whose file changed on disk takes the new text without asking. A buffer with unsaved edits keeps them and shows an orange bar with 비교 (the disk version against the buffer, changed lines with three lines around, read through the session's file channel), 덮어쓰기 (writes the buffer over the disk version) and 디스크 버전으로 되돌리기 (reloads the disk version and drops the edits); a manual save (Cmd/Ctrl+S) does not write while the choice is open and says why, as Code-OSS's save conflict does. (3) *Deleted on disk.* The tab of a file deleted on disk is struck through and reads "<name> (삭제됨)"; the buffer stays open and a save writes the file again. (4) *Lost connection.* The window keeps its tabs and buffers and shows a banner, "서버와의 연결이 끊어졌습니다. 저장하지 않은 변경 사항은 이 창에 남아 있습니다", with 다시 연결 (the session's reason is the tooltip and the log); a save while lost is refused with a notice. 다시 연결 starts a new session (the protocol-level `reconnect_loop` does not fit: `EditorSession` drops its connection state with the socket) and puts every tab back in order with the buffer the window holds (`EditorSession::restore`: dirty unless it equals the disk, with the file's own line ending and BOM), then focuses the current tab; a failed reconnect keeps the banner with the new reason. (5) *Auto-save* (Code-OSS `files.autoSave`), chosen in the ⋯ menu under 자동 저장 and stored per device with the keymap (`Prefs::auto_save`, `off` when absent): 끄기 (`off`, the default), 입력을 멈추고 1초 뒤 (`afterDelay`, `files.autoSaveDelay` 1000 ms after the last edit) and 다른 탭으로 옮길 때 (`onFocusChange`, when another tab becomes current; leaving the window does not save yet). Auto-save saves only dirty documents and skips one in conflict or deleted on disk. (6) *Hot exit* is deferred (`INTENT.md` D43, #744). Every string is Korean in 합니다체 (`NFR-L4`); the bars and the banner follow the island rules of the window (rounded, the mockup's soft colour tokens), since the mockup draws none. | **FR-L31.7** | *[provisional, #453; basis: `INTENT.md` D38 and the owner rule of FR-L31 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Line ending, encoding and language mode in the Ember 에디터 status bar**, as Code-OSS's `editorStatus.ts`; the items sit where the mockup draws LF and UTF-8 (`.ed-sbi` buttons, hidden at the `hide-md` width like the mockup), with the language mode after the tab width. (1) *EOL.* Detected per document as Monaco does: the first line break decides (`\r\n` is CRLF, else LF), so a file with mixed endings counts as its first one; a file with no line break is LF on every OS (Code-OSS takes `files.eol` there, whose `auto` follows the OS; Ember keeps one rule for all OSes). The status bar shows `LF` or `CRLF`; a click opens the palette sheet as a picker (줄 끝 시퀀스 선택, LF and CRLF, the current one highlighted). Choosing the other one changes the document's line endings as Code-OSS's `pushEOL`: the extension host gets `ExtHostDocuments.$acceptModelChanged` with no content changes, `isEolChange` and the new `eol`, one version later, and the document is dirty; the widget's `\n` text is unchanged, and save writes the new endings. Undo of the EOL change is not offered (the widget's undo stack does not hold it). (2) *Encoding.* Detected by the byte order mark as Code-OSS does without `files.autoGuessEncoding`: `EF BB BF` UTF-8 with BOM, `FF FE` UTF-16 LE, `FE FF` UTF-16 BE, else UTF-8 when the bytes are UTF-8. The label is Code-OSS's (`UTF-8`, `UTF-8 with BOM`, `UTF-16 LE`, `UTF-16 BE`); an encoding Ember does not know is "알 수 없음", and such a file is still refused on open (no guessing; the open error says why). A click opens 작업 선택 with 인코딩으로 다시 열기 and 인코딩으로 저장, then the four encodings (id beside each, the current one highlighted). 저장 takes the encoding and saves (Code-OSS `EncodingMode.Encode`); the extension host gets `$acceptEncodingChanged`. 다시 열기 in the current encoding does nothing (Code-OSS `isNewEncoding`); in another one it reads the file again and decodes it exactly in that encoding, and the document is re-announced (remove + add with `IModelAddedData.encoding`). Bytes that do not fit the encoding leave the document as it was and a Korean notice says so. When the document has unsaved edits the picker first asks 저장하지 않은 변경 사항을 버리고 다른 인코딩으로 다시 여시겠습니까? with 변경 사항을 버리고 다시 열기 and 취소 (취소 highlighted), as Code-OSS asks before it reverts. (3) *Language mode.* The status bar shows the language of the document the session already has, by the first `aliases` entry of the extensions' `contributes.languages` (`Plain Text` for `plaintext`), else the id. A click opens 언어 모드 선택: 자동 감지 (the language the path gives), then every known language by name, `(id)` beside it and `(id) - 현재` beside the current one, sorted by name as Code-OSS's `getSortedRegisteredLanguageNames`. Every picker opens with the current EOL, encoding or language highlighted, tagged 현재 and in view, as Code-OSS reveals a quick pick's active item; the toolkit has no scroll-into-view, so a picker's list is drawn from the page of rows that holds the highlighted one, with a 위에 N개 더 있습니다 row that leads back up and counts inside the sheet's row budget, and one row of context below the highlighted one (unless it is the last). Until the person moves the highlight or types, it follows the current entry, so a language list that arrives after the sheet opened still opens on the current language. Choosing one puts the document under that id: `$activateByEvent("onLanguage:<id>")`, then `ExtHostDocuments.$acceptModelLanguageChanged(uri, id)` (extHost.protocol.ts L2400 at `PINNED_COMMIT`; the extension host treats it as remove + add), and the colours and CodeLenses follow the new language. An extension's `languages.setTextDocumentLanguage` (`MainThreadLanguages.$changeLanguage`) does the same and is refused for an id no extension contributes. (4) *Out of scope.* Breadcrumb symbols need document symbols (`$provideDocumentSymbols`, #442) and stay path only; Code-OSS's 파일 연결 구성, language settings, marketplace search and guessed-encoding rows are not offered. | **FR-L31.8** | *[provisional, #747; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Git blame of the cursor line in the Ember 에디터 window.** Code-OSS 1.139's git extension shows who last changed the cursor line (`git.blame.statusBarItem.enabled`, template `${authorName} (${authorDateAgo})`, with a hover) and an end-of-line hint (`git.blame.editorDecoration.enabled`). (1) *Status bar.* An item before the cursor position shows the author and the relative time, "김민지 (3일 전)", in Code-OSS's `fromNow` steps in Korean (지금 under 30 seconds, then N초, N분, N시간, N일, N주 under 30 days, N개월 under 365 days, N년, with 전, or 후 for a time ahead of the clock); its hover (`title`) holds the author and mail, the relative time, the commit summary and the short sha. A line no commit holds (an edit, saved or not, a new or untracked file, a repository without a commit) shows 아직 커밋하지 않음. Nothing shows for a file outside the folder, a project without a repository, or a failed call. (2) *Data.* A new op `blame` on the git relay of `FR-G4` (`/api/projects/{name}/git/blame`, node `POST /git/blame`, body `{path, line, contents?}`), because no existing op answers who changed a line (`log` has no path or line, `diff` only the change): `git blame --porcelain -L <line>,<line> -- <path>` in the work tree root, with `--contents -` and the buffer's text while the buffer has unsaved edits, so the line numbers are the buffer's. The buffer arrives as the widget holds it, with `\n` breaks; when it has no `\r` and the saved file in the work tree is CRLF (more CRLF than bare LF, Monaco's rule), the node converts it to CRLF first, so git compares like with like and only edited lines come back not committed (the document's EOL does not reach the window's bridge on develop; the saved file is read through the path policy). Limits: `path` is relative to the work tree root with plain components only (400 otherwise), `line` is at least 1 and inside the file (400), `contents` is at most the git output cap (8 MiB, 413), the output is capped like every git op, and git is stopped after 10 s (`EMBER_GIT_BLAME_TIMEOUT_SECS`, 504), because a blame walks history. A zero sha, a path HEAD does not have, or no HEAD answers `commit: null`. (3) *Refresh.* A cursor line change waits 250 ms before asking (a newer line supersedes it); answers are kept per path, line and unsaved buffer version (at most 512), and every status read of the window drops them: after a save, a commit, a stage or unstage, a discard, a branch switch and 새로 고침. (4) *Gap.* The end-of-line hint is not drawn: the compose-rust `CodeEditor` (v0.0.6) has four decoration kinds (underline, code lens, hover anchor, ghost text), and ghost text is a suggestion that Tab inserts into the document, so drawing blame with it would let a keystroke write the blame into the file. It waits for an after-line text decoration in compose-rust. App tests (`ember/app/src/ui/editor_blame.rs`, `ember/app/src/demo.rs`, `fr_l31_8_*`): the Korean relative time at each step, the status, hover and accessible texts, 아직 커밋하지 않음, the answer's decoding, the folder-relative path, the debounce state (asked once, superseded, a late answer kept without a redraw, dropped on a save or commit, unsaved versions apart, the last answer bridging the wait, the cache bound), and the demo answer. Not verified: the rendered window, a real remote node. |
| **FR-L31.9** | *[provisional, #748; basis: `INTENT.md` D38 and the owner rule of FR-L31 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Source control for a folder below the repository root.** As Code-OSS does for a workspace folder inside a repository (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`), the Ember 에디터 window shows the changes of the enclosing repository. The node answers `POST /git/toplevel` (relayed as `git/toplevel`, read-only, `FR-G4`) with the work tree root as git prints it and the directory's path below it (`--show-prefix`); the root must lie inside an allowed root, as for every repository path. Paths stay relative to the repository root on every call: `git/status` gives them so, and the node runs `diff`, `stage`, `unstage` and `apply` at the work tree root, because from a subdirectory git reads a pathspec relative to that subdirectory and `git apply --cached` skips every file outside it while still exiting 0. The window reads `git/toplevel` once with the first status (again on the next refresh if it failed; until it answers the folder is the root) and takes the folder's prefix from the two paths, or from git's prefix when the folder is spelled differently (a symlink). With it, a repository path maps to the folder by stripping the prefix and back by joining it; the diff gutter looks an open file up by its path below the repository root; the explorer and tab highlight, 파일 열기 and the discard use the folder path for a file inside the folder and the absolute path (repository root plus repository path) for one outside it. The 커밋 window lists every change of the repository, those outside the folder included, each by its path from the repository root, as Code-OSS lists them; staging patches keep the repository paths `git/diff` gave. Paths are `/` separated; a Windows folder is read with either separator and the drive letter without case. Draft. | Unit tests (`ember/app/src/ui/editor_scm_repo.rs`, `fr_l31_9_*`) cover the prefix from the paths and from git (root equal to the folder, a symlinked folder, a sibling with the same start, Windows separators and drive case), the mapping both ways (inside, outside, root equal to the folder, Windows separators, a round trip), the absolute path of a change inside and outside, the gutter lookup, the editor path matching the explorer's join, a staging patch of a file outside the folder keeping its repository path, and the `git/toplevel` answer. Node tests (`ember/node/tests/git_subfolder.rs`) run against real repositories: `toplevel` from the root, a subfolder and a deeper one, 404 for a plain directory; status, diff (inside, outside, untracked, the whole tree), stage, unstage and apply from a subfolder for files inside and outside it. The relay test (`ember/server/tests/git_relay.rs`) covers `toplevel`. Not verified: the rendered window on a real subfolder, a remote node. |
| **FR-L31.10** | *[provisional, #742; basis: `INTENT.md` D38 and the owner rule of FR-L31 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **The working-tree side of a diff is an editor.** As in Code-OSS's diff editor, the right side of a side-by-side (`나란히 보기`) working-tree diff in the Ember 에디터 window is the file's own document in a `CodeEditor`, bound through the same widget bridge as the file's tab (`ember/app/src/ui/editor_scm.rs` `diff_editor_view`): typing there edits the one document the session holds, so the edit shows in the tab too, the tab and the diff header show the unsaved mark, and Cmd/Ctrl+S in the diff saves that document (`save_target`). When the file has no tab, the diff opens its document in the session once (`diff_document_needs_open`) and lets it go when the diff closes or moves to another file: closed when nothing holds it and it has no unsaved edits, given a tab when it has some (the current tab stays), kept when a tab holds it (`release_diff_document`); a file edited in a diff without a tab counts as unsaved when the window closes or a folder is renamed. The left side is the base, read-only, one row per working-tree line so it lines up with the editor line by line (`align_working_rows`: the base line beside a changed line in red, an empty row beside an added line, removed base lines named on the row below them with a red rule and a hover list), and it follows the editor's first visible line (`on_visible_range_change`). The base is rebuilt once from the `git/diff` answer and the clean document text (`reconstruct_base_text`), kept while edits are unsaved and rebuilt from the next answer after a save; on every change the diff is recomputed against it, the gutter marks with `compute_line_diffs` and the rows and the `+n −m` summary with `align_working_rows`, from the same edit script. Reading the same diff again after a save keeps the editor open until the answer. Only the side-by-side working-tree view is editable: the staged diff is the index and stays read-only (as in Code-OSS), and the unified view stays read-only rows because an inline editable diff needs a widget the toolkit does not have; line and hunk staging stay in the unified view. Toolkit gaps (compose-rust v0.0.6 `CodeEditor`), so the diff is drawn with what it has rather than worked around: no whole-line background decoration (compose-rust#542: the editable side shows the change as `DiffAdded`, `DiffModified`, `DiffRemoved` gutter bars, not as tinted lines), no view zones or spacer lines (compose-rust#543: removed base lines cannot take blank rows on the editable side, so they are not rows on the base side either), no scroll position finer than a line, no reported line height and no way to set another editor's scroll (compose-rust#544: the base side follows the first visible line, and its 20 px rows assume the editor's line height). Each workaround carries a `TODO(compose-rust#N)` in the code. Draft. | Unit tests (`ember/app/src/ui/editor_scm.rs`, `fr_l31_10_*`) cover the base rows against the working tree (changed, added, removed above, removed at the end, two lines replaced by one, one row per working-tree line, the same edit script as the gutter), the hover text of removed lines, that only the side-by-side working-tree diff is editable, that reading the same diff again keeps it editable, the live recompute after an edit against the kept base and its rebuild after a save, that the tab and the diff edit one bridge document with one unsaved mark, opening and letting go of the diff's document, and that Cmd/Ctrl+S in the diff saves the working-tree document. Not verified: typing and saving in the diff with a real keyboard on screen. |
| **FR-L31.12** | *[provisional, #440; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Editing core of the code area in the Ember 에디터 window.** (1) *Find and replace in the file* (#628 landed the bar: 대소문자 구분, 단어 단위, counter, previous and next, 바꾸기, 모두 바꾸기, the 10,000-match cap). This row adds Code-OSS's other find widget options: **정규식 사용** (`.*`), with the Oniguruma engine search across files uses (`FR-E7`), so a pattern means the same in both; a pattern that does not compile matches nothing and the counter says 잘못된 정규식; with 정규식 a replacement expands `$&`, `$0`, `$1` to `$99`, `$$`, `\n`, `\t` and `\\` against that match's groups. **선택 영역에서 찾기** (선택): the scope is the selection at the moment the option is turned on, as in Code-OSS (ends in order; a selection over several lines that ends at the start of a line ends at the end of the line before; an empty selection searches the whole file), and only matches wholly inside it are counted, visited and replaced. **대소문자 보존** (`AB`, beside the replacement field): each replacement takes the case of the text it replaces by Code-OSS's `buildReplaceStringWithCasePreserved` (`-` or `_` segments each, then all upper, all lower, capital first, small first). A match never spans lines (Code-OSS's multi-line regex is not built). The options take Code-OSS's chords while a find bar field has focus (⌥⌘R, ⌥⌘L, ⌥⌘P on macOS, Alt+R, Alt+L, Alt+P elsewhere, the same in both keymaps; `keymap::FindOption`, matched by the physical key), shown in each button's tooltip. (2) *Toolkit gaps*, not built: the compose-rust v0.0.6 `CodeEditor` (`compose_rust::code`, dioxus-compose `CodeEditor`) has no soft wrap (its rows are fixed height and "code does not wrap"; the `SoftWrap` property is a `Text` one), so 보기 > 자동 줄 바꿈 (⌥Z / Alt+Z) is not offered (compose-rust#548); one selection only (`SelectionChange` and `CodeEditorHandle::select` carry one anchor and one caret), so no multi-cursor (compose-rust#549); no minimap (compose-rust#550); no decoration that paints a range's background or box (the kinds are underline, code lens, hover anchor and ghost text), so neither 모두 강조 of find matches nor bracket-pair highlighting can be drawn (compose-rust#542); and an edit batch is not one undo step (compose-rust#496), so 모두 바꾸기 undoes match by match. Draft. | Unit tests named `fr_l31_12_*` (`ember/app/tests/fr_l31_12_find.rs`): regex matching with case and whole word, UTF-16 columns, an invalid pattern and its message, navigation past the kept matches, group expansion and literal replacement left unexpanded, the preserve-case rules and their use on every replacement, the option chords on both OSes (`fr_l31_12_find_option_chords_follow_code_oss`), the scope from a selection and matches limited to it. Not verified: the rendered window. |
| **FR-L31.13** | *[provisional, #442; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Language features in the Ember 에디터 window**, beyond diagnostics, hover data, CodeLens and inline completions. (1) *Providers.* The session decodes the extension host's registrations `$registerCompletionsProvider`, `$registerDefinitionSupport`, `$registerReferenceSupport`, `$registerRenameSupport`, `$registerCodeActionSupport`, `$registerDocumentFormattingSupport`, `$registerRangeFormattingSupport`, `$registerSignatureHelpProvider` and `$registerDocumentSymbolProvider` (`ember/editor-conn/src/language.rs`) and keeps them with their selectors; a feature asks the providers that match the document, best score first. (2) *Completion list* (Ctrl+Space): `$provideCompletionItems` on every matching provider, one merged list filtered by what is typed (prefix, then substring, then letters in order; ties by sort text); accepting resolves the item first when the provider resolves details (`$resolveCompletionItem`), inserts its text over the typed word, applies its additional edits, runs its command, and releases the lists (`$releaseCompletionItems`). A snippet is inserted as plain text (placeholders kept, tab stops not linked). (3) *Go to definition* (F12): `$provideDefinition`; one result opens the file with the name selected, several are listed. (4) *Format document* (the keymap's 코드 정리 chord): `$provideDocumentFormattingEdits`, or the range formatter over the whole document when the language has only that; the edits go to the editor as its own edits, so one undo history holds them. (5) *Rename* (F2): `$resolveRenameLocation` when the provider supports it, else the word at the caret, fills a box with the current name; `$provideRenameEdits` answers a workspace edit that is applied to open documents in their buffer and to other files on disk; a rejection shows the provider's reason. (6) *References* (Shift+F12): `$provideReferences` with the declaration included, listed by file and line. (7) *Code actions* (Ctrl+. or Cmd+.): `$provideCodeActions` at the caret, the preferred fix first, a disabled action shown with its reason; choosing one resolves it when it came without its edit (`$resolveCodeAction`), applies the edit, runs the command and releases the lists (`$releaseCodeActions`). An extension's `workspace.applyEdit` (`MainThreadBulkEdits.$tryApplyWorkspaceEdit`) is applied the same way. (8) *Parameter hint* (Ctrl+Shift+Space or Cmd+Shift+Space): `$provideSignatureHelp` from the first provider that answers, the active parameter set off; the hint it replaces is released (`$releaseSignatureHelp`). (9) *Breadcrumb symbols*: `$provideDocumentSymbols` when a file becomes active and after a save; the symbols around the caret follow the path. (10) *Where it is drawn.* Lists and the rename box are a sheet over the window in the mockup's tokens (the mockup draws none), opened by their chord or the editor menu and filtered in their own field; the parameter hint is a strip at the bottom of the editor area. They are not at the caret and do not open while typing, because the `CodeEditor` widget reports no caret rectangle (compose-rust#553) and no key before it handles it (compose-rust#554, related compose-rust#100); the editor menu carries every command for the same reason. Cmd/Ctrl+click waits for a pointer press with modifiers (compose-rust#555, also compose-rust#417) and linked snippet tab stops for a snippet session (compose-rust#556). Not built: completion and parameter hints while typing, Cmd/Ctrl+click, peek views, snippet tab stops, file create, rename and delete inside a workspace edit, on-type and on-save formatting, workspace symbols. Draft. | Unit tests `fr_l31_13_*`: every registration and call shape (`ember/editor-conn/src/language.rs`), the DTO decoding, the list filter, snippet text, text and workspace edits (`ember/editor/src/language/`), the session flows with cancellation and release (`ember/editor/tests/fr_l31_13_language_features.rs`), and the window's sheet rows, messages and chords (`ember/app/src/ui/editor_language.rs`). Not yet verified against a real extension host or on a display. |
| **FR-L31.14** | *[provisional, #446; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Extensions panel in the Ember 에디터 window.** (1) *Where.* A left-stripe island tool window 확장 after Git (the mockup draws none, so it follows the project window's island rules and the `style.css` tokens). The VS Code keymap opens it with ⇧⌘X / Ctrl+Shift+X as Code-OSS does; the JetBrains keymap binds no chord (its Plugins page has none by default). The command palette lists it. (2) *Installed list.* The rows come from the scan the session reads on the management connection (`remoteExtensionsScanner.scanExtensions`, `ember_editor::extensions::parse_installed`): display name (the manifest name when `displayName` is absent or an unresolved `%key%`), publisher, version, description, and whether it is enabled. Two groups, 설치됨 (user extensions) and 기본 제공 (built-in, `isBuiltin`), each sorted by display name without regard to case. The list is read again after an install, an uninstall and on 새로 고침. (3) *Search.* Text in the panel's field searches Open VSX (debounced 300 ms, Enter at once); an empty field shows the installed list. **The search goes through the server, never from the client:** `GET /api/ide/extensions/search?q=<text>&offset=<n>` asks `https://open-vsx.org/api/-/search` (`query`, `size` 30, `offset`, `sortBy=relevance`, `sortOrder=desc`). Security reasoning: the host is a constant in the server and the client supplies only `q` (1 to 200 characters, no control characters) and `offset` (at most 10,000), so the route cannot be pointed at another host; the request carries no Ember credential (its own HTTP client without the server token), follows no redirect, times out after 10 s and reads at most 1 MiB; the answer is rebuilt into fixed fields (`id`, `name`, `namespace`, `display_name`, `description`, `version`, `downloads`, `verified`, `deprecated`) with every text capped (description 400 characters), and an entry whose `namespace.name` fails the install id pattern is dropped, so nothing the gallery sends can become an install argument that the install route would refuse anyway; URL fields (icon, download) are dropped, so the client never fetches a third-party URL and a device's address does not reach the gallery. The machine that downloads at install time is the server's OSE, so search and install see the same registry from the same network, and a device with no route to open-vsx.org still searches. Cost: the server's address reaches open-vsx.org on a search, as it already does on an install. Rejected: searching from the client (a second egress point, the device's address exposed, results the server's network may not be able to install). Not configurable: a mirror would have to change the OSE `product.json` gallery too. The route is in the token-protected router; the guest door does not serve it. Answers: 200 `{offset,total,extensions}`, 400 (bad `q` or `offset`), 502 (the gallery failed, timed out, redirected or sent something that is not the search JSON). (4) *Install and uninstall.* Install is `POST /api/computers/{id}/ide/extensions` (`FR-W4`, #459) with the id only, so the OSE picks the newest compatible version as Code-OSS does. Uninstall is `DELETE /api/computers/{id}/ide/extensions/{publisher.name}`: `dpx-ose-server --uninstall-extension <id>` (the flag is in Code-OSS 1.139.1 `serverEnvironmentService.ts`) with the same rules as install (id pattern, no shell, the computer's data dir, one extension command per computer at a time or 409, 120 s, cleared environment, 16 KiB output; 502 with the output when the command fails). A built-in extension has no uninstall button. One install or uninstall runs at a time in the panel; the row says 설치 중 or 제거 중. (5) *Enable and disable.* Code-OSS keeps the disabled ids in the workbench's own profile storage (`extensionsIdentifiers/disabled`, `extensionEnablementService.ts`) and leaves them out of what it hands the extension host; the OSE server is never told and has no channel for it. Ember is the workbench here, so it does the same: the disabled ids are per-device prefs keyed by computer id (`Prefs.disabled_extensions`), matched without regard to case, and `SessionConfig.disabled_extensions` leaves them out of the extension host's init data, the contributed commands, the languages and the grammars at connect. Built-in extensions can be disabled too. Gaps: no per-workspace disable, and the VS Code Web fallback keeps its own list. (6) *Restart notice.* Ember does not send `$deltaExtensions`, so an install, uninstall, enable or disable takes effect when the extension host starts again, which today means opening the editor window again (no in-window restart: gap). While the enabled set (id and version) differs from the one the extension host started with, the panel shows `변경 사항은 에디터 창을 다시 열면 적용됩니다.`; enabling again what was disabled removes the notice. After an install or an uninstall succeeds the notice stays until the window closes (the next scan is not relied on to show the change). (7) *States* in Korean (`NFR-L4`): no computer id (install and uninstall are off), not connected, searching, no results, more results (`더 보기`), the server's own message on a failure, the command output on a failed install or uninstall. Not built: extension detail page, icons, ratings, update of an installed extension, install of a specific version or a VSIX, recommendations, extension packs shown as packs, sync. A debug build reads `EMBER_CR_EDITOR_EXTENSIONS=open|installed|search|busy|restart` to start with the window open in that state for captures (sample rows, no request sent); a release build ignores it. Draft. | `ember/editor/tests/fr_l31_14_extensions.rs` (parsing a scan, the `%key%` fallback, grouping and order, the enabled filter without regard to case); `ember/server/tests/fr_l31_14_extensions.rs` (search against a loopback fake gallery: the upstream query, the rebuilt fields, caps, dropped ids, 400s, 502 on failure, redirect and oversize, no `Authorization` sent upstream, token protection; uninstall argv, bad ids, 409, 502, non-local computer); unit tests in `ember/app/src/ui/editor_extensions.rs` (`fr_l31_14_*`: the rows and groups, the restart notice rule, stale answers dropped, busy rules, state texts) and `ember/app/src/prefs.rs` (the disabled list round trip). `ember/editor/tests/fr_l31_14_live_extensions.rs` reads the scan again on a real OSE (ignored without `EMBER_OSE_SERVER`). Not verified: on screen; against a real OSE (`--uninstall-extension`, and whether a running OSE's scan shows an extension the CLI installed a moment ago). |

A modal message (`options.modal`) is a dialog with `options.detail`, the extension's non-cancel buttons and a cancel button (the close-affordance one, else 취소 answering `undefined`); it never enters the center. | **FR-L31.17** | *[provisional, #449, `INTENT.md` D47; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Settings, settings.json and keybindings.json in the Ember 에디터 window.** (1) *Scopes.* Default (every extension's `contributes.configuration` plus `ember_settings`), user (`<data folder>/editor/User/settings.json` on the client), remote (the OSE server's `Machine/settings.json`, read only) and workspace (`<folder>/.vscode/settings.json`), merged in that order; a language block wins over every plain key and object values merge key by key (`SettingsScopes::value`). The files are JSON with comments and trailing commas (`parse_jsonc`). (2) *Extension host.* All scopes go out in `$initializeConfiguration` as separate models, and every change of a scope sends `$acceptConfigurationChanged` with the new data and the changed keys (`SettingsScopes::change_from`, `exthost::accept_configuration_changed`). (3) *Documents.* `settings.json` and `keybindings.json` open as editable documents in a tab; saving one applies it at once. The session reads and writes only the user's two files on the client and refuses any other `vscode-userdata:` URI. A file that does not parse keeps the last good settings and the view says why. (4) *Settings view.* ⌘, / Ctrl+, (VS Code keymap), ⌘, / Ctrl+Alt+S (JetBrains keymap), the ⋯ menu and the palette open a 설정 tab: a search field over key, description and source, a 사용자 / 작업 영역 switch for where edits go, one row per setting with a control by type (switch, choices, number or text field committed with Enter, "settings.json에서 편집" for objects and arrays), 기본값으로 on a key the scope sets, and a note when a stronger scope overrides the row. An edit rewrites only that key of the file and keeps comments, order and line endings (`set_setting`); it is refused while the file is open with unsaved edits. (5) *Honoured by Ember.* `editor.tabSize` and `editor.insertSpaces` per language for documents opened afterwards; `files.exclude` hides explorer rows (Code-OSS glob syntax, a hidden folder hides what is below it). `editor.wordWrap`, `editor.formatOnSave`, `files.autoSave` and `files.autoSaveDelay` are registered and resolved (`EditorSettings`) and are not acted on yet (#451, #442). (6) *Keybindings.* `keybindings.json` is read on top of the keymap of `FR-L30`: a key bound to a Code-OSS command id of an Ember action runs it, any other command goes to the extension host, `-command` removes a default (`resolve_key`). Entries with `when` and two-stroke keys are not applied. Draft. | Unit tests `fr_l31_17_*` in `ember/editor/tests/fr_l31_17_settings.rs` (JSONC, language blocks, scope merge, change keys, the configuration payload per scope, `EditorSettings`, tab size per language through `SessionCore`, `files.exclude` globs, `set_setting`, the settings list and search, `keybindings.json`, `vscode-userdata:` paths), `ember/editor-conn/src/exthost.rs` (`$acceptConfigurationChanged` call shape, override models) and `ember/app/tests/fr_l31_17_settings.rs` (user files on disk, which file a save belongs to, row values, typed values, explorer rows, key resolution, shortcuts and palette rows). | **FR-L31.18** | *[provisional, #450; basis: `INTENT.md` D46 and the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Workspace trust in the Ember 에디터 window.** (1) *Prompt.* On the first open of a folder with no remembered answer the window opens in restricted mode and shows a modal with no close action: "이 폴더에 있는 파일의 작성자를 신뢰합니까?", the detail that extensions may run the folder's files automatically, the scope sentence of D45 (agents and terminals keep full authority), and two choices, 예, 작성자를 신뢰합니다 and 아니요, 작성자를 신뢰하지 않습니다, drawn as the window's dialog buttons with trust as the primary one (`prompt_buttons`, the card of the unsaved-changes sheet of FR-L31.5, #801) (Code-OSS `workspace.contribution.ts` startup prompt). (2) *Memory.* The answer is saved per device in the prefs file (`Prefs::workspace_trust`, beside the keymap) under the computer id and the folder; the longest remembered folder that is or contains the opened folder decides, as `doGetUriTrustInfo` does. (3) *Restricted mode.* The extension host starts with `$initializeWorkspace(workspace, false)`; `myExtensions` and `activationEvents` of the init data leave out every extension whose `getExtensionUntrustedWorkspaceSupportType` is `false` (it has a `main` and `capabilities.untrustedWorkspaces.supported` is not `true` or `"limited"`) and every extension that depends on one (`allExtensions` stays whole). `MainThreadWorkspace.$isResourceTrusted` answers the folder's trust; `$requestWorkspaceTrust` and `$requestResourceTrust` answer `true` when trusted, else wait, show the prompt again and answer `true` on 신뢰 or `undefined` on 제한 모드. 실행 (and debug and tasks when they exist) says "제한 모드에서는 실행을(를) 사용할 수 없습니다. ..." instead of running. A banner above the tabs says "제한 모드는 안전하게 코드를 둘러보기 위한 모드입니다. 모든 기능을 사용하려면 이 폴더를 신뢰하십시오." with 신뢰 and a close button (closed until the window opens again). (4) *Grant.* 신뢰 sends `ExtHostExtensionService.$deltaExtensions` with the withheld extensions in `myToAdd` and their activation events, then `ExtHostWorkspace.$onDidGrantWorkspaceTrust()`; trust is granted once per window and leaving it takes effect at the next open. Draft. | Unit tests named `fr_l31_18_*`: the support rule and dependants (`ember/editor-conn/src/trust.rs`), the init data rewrite and the grant calls, the session core's answers and waiting requests (`ember/editor/src/trust.rs`, `ember/editor/tests/fr_l31_18_workspace_trust.rs`), the per-folder memory with parent folders and computers, the prompt and banner states, the blocked-feature text (`ember/app/src/ui/workspace_trust.rs`). Not verified: a real OSE extension host taking `$deltaExtensions` and activating an added extension, the rendered prompt and banner. |
| **FR-L31.19** | *[provisional, #333; source: the owner rule of FR-L31 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업" and the token rule of FR-S11]* **TLS for the editor terminal relay.** The Ember 에디터 terminal (and the session screen's terminal view) reaches the server relay (`/api/computers/{id}/terms`, its `attach` WebSocket) over `https` and `wss` when the server URL is `https`, with the certificate verified against the webpki roots (rustls, the stack `ember/node` and `ember/server` already use); there is no option to accept an unverified certificate, because the HTTP server has no fingerprint pinning of its own. Plain `http` and `ws` are used only for a loopback host (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `localhost`) or a host listed with an explicit `http://host:port` entry in `EMBER_TOKEN_HOSTS`, the same rule as the token, so keystrokes and shell output do not cross a network in clear by accident; any other address is refused before connecting, and before the tab strip's list, new and kill requests (#796), so a withheld token never turns into a 401 first; the panel shows the fixed sentence `RELAY_REFUSED` and the English reason goes to the log. | Unit tests (`ember/app/src/terminal_stream.rs`, `fr_l31_19_*`) and `ember/app/tests/fr_l31_19_terminal_errors.rs` cover the ws or wss scheme per server scheme, https always allowed, plain http allowed to loopback and to an explicit http entry only, and other schemes refused. Not verified: a real https server with a public certificate. |
| **FR-L31.20** | *[provisional, #334; source: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **The editor terminal PTY follows its panel.** As Code-OSS does with its xterm fit addon, the terminal panel of the Ember 에디터 window gives the PTY its columns and rows from the panel size and the cell size: the panel's border box less its padding (`.ed-term`, 16 px left and right, 12 px top and bottom), divided by the cell (`--code-fs` 13 px at 0.6 em advance, `--code-lh` 22 px), rounded down, at least 2 columns and 1 row. The first size goes out when the panel opens; after that a size goes out once it has held for 100 ms, through the node terminal API's resize message on the attach socket (`{"type":"resize","size":{"rows":R,"cols":C}}`, `TermInput::Resize`, relayed by the server unchanged). A hidden panel sends nothing, so it never shrinks the PTY; a terminal started again after 다시 연결 gets the current size. The panel reads its size on each render, because the toolkit sends no resize event (dioxus-compose#207); the 0.6 em cell width is a fixed stand-in, because the toolkit can drop the font family and measures no text (dioxus-compose#196). Draft. | Unit tests `fr_l31_20_*` (`ember/app/src/ui/term_fit.rs`) cover columns and rows from the panel and cell size, the minimum and the hidden panel, the first size sent at once, the debounce while a size changes and when it returns, the wake for a held size, and the resize message shape. Not verified: the rendered window, a real PTY, and a window resize with no other render. |

| **FR-L31.22** | *[provisional, #336; source: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Editor terminal reconnect and visible protocol errors.** When the terminal's attach stream drops without the process exiting, the panel keeps the last screen, shows `터미널 연결이 끊어졌습니다. N초 후 다시 연결합니다. (n/6)` and reattaches with backoff (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, then 30 seconds; six attempts, reset once a stream attaches). The reattach sends the same session key, so the server re-adopts the PTY the node kept alive (FR-P6), and the hello asks for a snapshot, which redraws the screen; keys typed while down are dropped, not replayed. After the last attempt the panel shows a fixed sentence and the 다시 연결 button. Attach and relay failures show `터미널 연결에 문제가 생겼습니다.`, never the transport's text (that goes to the log), and an unreadable relay frame is skipped and named once on the panel. The tab strip's list, new and kill failures show fixed sentences too (#796): the relay refusal, `서버가 터미널 요청을 거절했습니다. …` for a 401 or 403, otherwise `터미널 목록을 불러오지 못했습니다.`, `새 터미널을 만들지 못했습니다.` or `터미널을 종료하지 못했습니다.` with `잠시 뒤 다시 시도하세요.`; the status and URL go to the log (`terminal_list::panel_error`). A process exit never reconnects. This state is the terminal's own, separate from the editor window's lost-connection banner (#451). Pure policy in `ember/app/src/terminal_reconnect.rs`, loop in `terminal_stream::start`; tests `fr_l31_22_*`. |

| **FR-L31.24** | *[provisional, #789; basis: `INTENT.md` D38 and the owner rule of FR-L31 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"; E4 keeps the OSE unpatched]* **File watching of the workspace folder, on any local disk.** (1) *Cause.* Code-OSS 1.139.1 refuses `fs.watch()` on macOS for every path below `/Volumes/` (`src/vs/platform/files/node/watcher/nodejs/nodejsWatcherLib.ts` L207-215, "possibly being a network share"), a guess by path prefix that also catches a local external disk. The session watched each open file with a non-recursive request, which the OSE serves with `fs.watch()`, so a project on `/Volumes/<disk>` never reported an external change. (2) *Watch.* At connect, before any file is opened, the session asks for one recursive watch on the workspace folder with the default of Code-OSS's `files.watcherExclude` (`ember/editor/src/watch.rs`), as the workbench does (`workspaceWatcher.ts`). The OSE serves it with its recursive watcher (FSEvents on macOS, inotify on Linux), which has no path-prefix rule and reports every file below the folder. A file below the folder that the excludes do not cover therefore gets no request of its own (`covered_by_workspace_watch`): the server's file channel serves a one-file request with a separate `fs.watch()` watcher that cannot reuse the recursive one (`src/vs/platform/files/common/diskFileSystemProvider.ts` `watchNonRecursive`). If the recursive request fails, files are watched one by one as before. (3) *Network share by filesystem type.* The server classifies the project folder by the filesystem it is on, never by its path (`ember/server/src/fs_kind.rs`): macOS `statfs` `f_fstypename` (smbfs, nfs, afpfs, webdav, ...), Linux `statfs` `f_type` (NFS, SMB, CIFS, SMB2, ...), Windows `GetDriveTypeW` equal to `DRIVE_REMOTE`; anything unknown is local. Only for a network share the OSE is started with `--file-watcher-polling 5000` (Code-OSS's own polling default), because native events do not report what another machine changes on a share. A local disk gets no flag wherever it is mounted. **Rejected:** patching the `/Volumes/` check out of the OSE build (E4: the only change to the upstream tree is `product.json`); a node-side watcher (#460), which duplicates the OSE's watcher for the one case the recursive request already covers. **Not covered:** a file outside the workspace folder that is on `/Volumes/` is still watched with `fs.watch()` and refused, as in Code-OSS itself; the Windows branch is not compiled or run locally. | Unit: `fr_l31_24_workspace_watch_is_recursive_with_the_code_oss_excludes`, `fr_l31_24_workspace_watch_is_uncorrelated`, `fr_l31_24_files_below_the_workspace_need_no_watch_of_their_own`, `fr_l31_24_files_outside_or_in_an_excluded_store_keep_their_own_watch` (`ember/editor/src/watch.rs`); `fr_l31_24_*` in `ember/server/src/fs_kind.rs` (apfs, hfs and exfat are local whatever the mount point; smbfs, nfs, afpfs, webdav and cifs are shares; the Linux magics; `DRIVE_REMOTE`; the Windows volume root; a mounted local volume below `/Volumes` is local) and `fr_l31_24_only_a_network_share_makes_the_ose_poll` (`ember/server/src/ose.rs`). Wiring: `pinned_commit_connects_lists_workspace_opens_edits_shows_diagnostics_and_saves` checks the one recursive watch at connect and no second watch after a workspace file is opened (`ember/editor/tests/connect_and_fallback.rs`). Live (ignored, needs an OSE build): `live_fr_l31_24_external_change_under_ws_root` with `EMBER_LIVE_WS_ROOT` below `/Volumes/<disk>` expects the reload and no "possibly being a network share" line for a workspace path in the server logs. Run on 2026-11-13 against OSE 1.139.1 darwin-arm64 with the workspace on `/Volumes/macMini` (APFS): failed before the change (the refusal line for the opened file, no reload in 30 s), passes after it. Not verified: a real network share (polling), Linux and Windows hosts, the editor window on a display. |
