---

## §S: Sessions and transcripts (main server: E3)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-S1** | A session belongs to a project and has a mutable *current computer* attribute (`INTENT.md` D1). | Changing the current computer does not change the session's identity, history, account or A2A address. |
| **FR-S2** | Every session's transcript is stored on the main server in one normalised representation, regardless of which agent produced it. Each agent's own native session state (e.g. Claude Code's session file, Codex's thread) is also kept on the main server, so the agent's own resume works. | Restarting the main server loses no completed turn. Resuming a session after a restart uses the agent's native resume (`--resume <id>` for Claude Code, thread resume for Codex). |
| **FR-S3** | A session survives every client disconnecting, and any number of clients can attach to it at once. | Close all clients mid-turn; the turn completes; a later client sees the full result. Two clients attached at once both see live output. |
| **FR-S4** | Full-text search across all sessions' messages. | Indexed search (not a linear scan); results link to the matching message. |
| **FR-S5** | A session can be forked from any completed turn where the agent supports it. | The fork records its parent and turn; agents that cannot fork have the action disabled, not failing. |
| **FR-S6** | Idle sessions release their agent process and reconnect transparently on the next message; a session being viewed is kept alive. | Measured: an idle session's agent process exits after the idle timeout; sending a message restores it via native resume. A session open in a client is not reclaimed. |
| **FR-S7** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` Q4]* When a session's current computer changes, the agent is told it moved ("now working on X (moved)") and re-reads files before editing. *[user, `INTENT.md` D20]* The notice is not worded as "invalid". | v0: a system notice tells the agent it has moved to the new computer and prior file observations must be re-read before editing. The same notice is sent on every connection state change (`FR-X3`): moved to the main server, and moved back. Per-file comparison *[provisional]*: at a switch the server takes the files in the session's Read, Edit, Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit tool calls (absolute paths, the latest 100), hashes each (SHA-256 of the whole file) on the computer left and on the one entered, and the notice names only files whose hash differs, is missing on one side, or could not be read; identical files need no re-read. Without file calls, or with an undelivered earlier notice, the v0 wording applies. The "observation" is the file as it is on the old computer at switch time, not at the moment it was read. |
| **FR-S8** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D17]* Agents outlive the main server. Each session's agent runs under a per-session shim that holds its stdio; the server reattaches after a crash, restart or update. An update hands live sessions to the new server without stopping them. If a shim also dies, the session resumes through the agent's native resume (`FR-S2`). Each session has a memory limit. | Kill the server mid-turn: the agent keeps running and the restarted server reattaches and shows the rest of the turn. Update the server with 3 sessions running: none stops and none loses a turn. Kill a shim: the session resumes natively. One agent exceeding its limit ends only its own session; the others continue. Replaces the `kill_on_drop` coupling. Partly built (#128): the shim (`ember-shim`, `ember_server::session_shim`), reattach with offset replay, native-resume fallback and the per-session memory limit (polled RSS of the agent's process tree, kills only that session) exist with tests `fr_s8_*` in `tests/session_shim.rs`. Wiring (`tests/fr_s8_wiring.rs`): all long-lived agent spawns go through `agents::proc::spawn_agent`; with a session id (`Sessions::ensure_live` adds it) and the `ember-shim` binary present they run under a shim, otherwise (or with `EMBER_SESSION_SHIM=off`) as direct children with `kill_on_drop`. The stdout bridge hands the adapter one line per read and stores and acknowledges the end of the previous line when the adapter asks for the next (table `shim_offsets`, last migration), so a crash repeats at most the line in flight and loses none. At startup `Sessions::rejoin_shims` reattaches every live shim of an agent that can be rejoined as it runs (Claude Code and Antigravity) from its stored offset; for Codex and ACP agents a surviving shim is stopped and the session resumes natively on its next message, because their `initialize` handshake needs a fresh process. A graceful server exit leaves the shims, and so the agents, running; the next server's `rejoin_shims` is the live handoff (no separate coordination). Acknowledgement is transactional (#128): a session's event pump acknowledges shim output only after the events it produced are stored, so a crash between reading a line and storing it replays that line. A permission prompt pending when the server died is rebuilt from the store when Claude Code is rejoined (`AgentRun::restore_pending_approvals`) and can be answered. For Codex a turn in flight is drained from the surviving app-server into the store (ended as failed if it cannot finish, e.g. it waits on an approval), then the app-server is stopped and the session resumes natively; there is no cross-turn app-server keep-alive. `rejoin_shims` attaches all sessions concurrently; tests `fr_s8_*` in `tests/fr_s8_wiring.rs` cover the replay, the approval, the Codex drain and the 3-session handoff. Antigravity runs each turn under a shim and reattaches its output after a restart (#284). Its hook fails closed if a request was interrupted by the restart. ACP agents are drained like Codex (#284): a turn in flight is read from the surviving agent (`session/update` notifications through the normal mapper, the `session/prompt` response as the turn end with usage and stop reason), stored, and the agent is stopped; the session resumes natively through `session/resume` or `session/load` on its next message. They are not rejoined as they run, because ACP v1 has no method that attaches a new client to an in-flight `session/prompt` (`session/load` and `session/resume` open a session on a fresh connection after `initialize`). A permission, `fs/*` or `terminal/*` request still open when the server died cannot be answered, so that turn ends as failed; a tool call announced before the restart may appear again when its update is drained. Tests: `fr_s8_acp_mid_turn_output_drained_and_persisted_after_server_crash` (`tests/fr_s8_wiring.rs`, fixture `tests/fixtures/acp/fake-acp-agent.sh`) and the `fr_s8_drain*` unit tests in `src/agents/acp/tests.rs`. The per-session memory limit is carried on `StartRequest::mem_limit_mb` and `AgentCommand::mem_limit_mb` into the shim spec (`proc::mem_limit_bytes`; the environment value `EMBER_SESSION_MEM_LIMIT_MB` is the fallback, `EMBER_SESSION_MEM_POLL_MS` sets the poll). `Sessions::set_mem_limit_mb(agent, limit)` sets it per agent kind for sessions started afterwards, and `ensure_live` fills the request (a start-config hook may override it); all four adapters pass it on. A running shim keeps the limit it started with, and a rejoined one is not changed. Tests: `fr_s8_per_session_memory_limit_from_the_start_request_ends_only_that_session`, `fr_s8_start_request_carries_the_sessions_memory_limit`, `fr_s8_sessions_keep_a_memory_limit_per_agent_kind`. *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]* The limit is set per harness through `PUT /api/agents/{agent}/settings`, stored in `harness_settings` and loaded at startup, so it survives a server restart; it applies to agents started after the change (details, range and tests in `FR-L20`). Not yet done: the app does not call the API yet (it still keeps `mem_limit_mb` on the device); the lower bound 256 MiB is unmeasured. Without a stored value the limit comes from `EMBER_SESSION_MEM_LIMIT_MB` or code. ACP drain is verified only against a scripted fake agent, not against a real ACP agent such as OMP. Real-server smoke harness (`ember/server/scripts/real-server-smoke.sh check` and `server-update`, `docs/design/REAL-SERVER-RUN.md`, #128) covers stopping and updating/restarting the server while three `claude-code` sessions run under `ember-shim`, verifying that all three agent processes stay alive while the server is down and that the restarted server rejoins all three shims and finishes their turns without loss. *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* The limit is a 1 s RSS poll (not `RLIMIT_AS`) and the shim waits up to 1 hour for the final ack after the agent exits; both are configurable. |
| **FR-S9** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D37, 2026-11-06, #338]* Local token. The server keeps a random token in a file in the data folder (`server-token`, mode 0600), creates it on first start when it is missing, and never logs it. Every HTTP route requires `Authorization: Bearer <token>`; the comparison is constant time; a missing or wrong token gets 401 with the JSON error body of the other routes. Only `GET /api/health` is exempt *[provisional]*, so a launcher can tell whether a server is up. WebSocket routes take the same header on the upgrade; browsers cannot set headers on a WebSocket, so a browser client uses a `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` entry `ember.token.<token>` (offered next to `ember.v1`, which the server selects so the token is not echoed), never a query string *[provisional]*. | An unauthenticated request is refused with 401 on each route family (sessions, projects, accounts, devices, hub, git, terminals, files, WebSocket upgrades); a wrong token is refused with 401; the right token is accepted; the token file has mode 0600 and is created on first start; two comparisons of different tokens take the same code path (constant-time compare). Tests named `fr_s9_*`. **Rotation** *[provisional, 2026-11-13, #369]*: after revoking a device that could have read the token (D37), the owner rotates it with `ember-server rotate-token` (writes a fresh token to the file and exits) or `POST /api/server-token/rotate` (token-protected, local listener only; answers `{rotated, token_file}` and never the token, so only a caller on this computer learns it). The file is replaced through a temporary file and a rename (mode 0600). A running server follows the file: a presented token that differs from the one in force makes it re-read the file once if its modification time changed, so a CLI rotation needs no restart. From then on the old token gets 401. A client made from the environment or the file (`Api::new`, `Api::with_token_file`) re-reads the token on a 401 and retries once; clones share the new token; a token given by the caller is never re-read. Push sockets opened with the old token stay open until they close (not cut at rotation). The push task reads its socket too, so it ends and drops its broadcast receiver as soon as the connection closes, not at the next push. Tests `fr_s9_rotate_*`, `fr_s9_cli_rotation_*`, `fr_s9_push_task_ends_when_the_socket_closes` (server), `fr_s9_client_rereads_*`, `fr_s9_a_*` in `ember/client/tests/fr_s9_token_reread.rs`; built in #791, local tests pass. |
| **FR-S10** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D37, 2026-11-06, #338]* Origin and Host check on all routes. Loopback is not trusted by itself: a request whose `Origin` is present and not on the allowed list, or whose `Host` is not the listen address or a configured name, is refused, on HTTP as on WebSocket. Builds on `api/origin.rs` (#339), which checks the WebSocket routes. | A cross-origin request is refused on each route family and a request with a foreign `Host` is refused; a request with no `Origin` (a native client) passes this check and still needs the token (`FR-S9`). Tests named `fr_s10_*`. *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, `INTENT.md` D37)* Listen guard: the server refuses to start when `EMBER_LISTEN` is not a loopback address (`0.0.0.0`, `::`, a LAN address, an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address) unless `EMBER_ALLOW_NON_LOOPBACK_LISTEN=1` is set, and logs a startup warning when it is; `localhost:<port>` is read as `127.0.0.1:<port>`. Decision in `auth::check_listen`, tests `fr_s10_non_loopback_listen_*`. |
| **FR-S11** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D37, 2026-11-06, #338]* The client and tools send the token. `ember/client`'s `ServerApi` reads the token from `EMBER_SERVER_TOKEN`, else from the token file in the data folder, and sends it as `Authorization: Bearer` on every HTTP call and WebSocket upgrade (the terminal stream included). The app and the tools on the same computer use the same path. | With the right token the client works end to end against a server that enforces `FR-S9`; with no token the client reports an authentication error and does not retry in a loop. Tests named `fr_s11_*`. A token file is read only if it is a regular file (not a symlink) with no group or other bits (mode 0600), and the token is sent only to a loopback host or one in `EMBER_TOKEN_HOSTS`; the environment variable skips the file checks. **`EMBER_TOKEN_HOSTS` is https-only by default (#429):** a bare entry (`dev.example.com`) matches that host on any port over `https` only; plain `http` needs an entry with the scheme and port (`http://dev.example.com:8740`); an entry with a scheme matches only that scheme, host and port (a missing port is the scheme's default); a non-loopback IPv6 host is written in the scheme form (`https://[fd00::1]:8740`). When the token is withheld from a bare listed host only because the target is `http`, the client logs one warning per process (`withheld_by_scheme`, test `fr_s11_a_bare_host_over_http_is_reported_as_withheld_by_scheme`). **The token file open (#430):** the kind is checked on the path, then the file is opened with `O_NONBLOCK`, so a FIFO swapped in between returns at once and is refused by the handle's kind (`fr_s11_a_fifo_swapped_in_before_the_open_is_refused_without_blocking`). `O_NONBLOCK` is spelled per OS because `ember-client` has no `libc` dependency; a unix target without a known value fails to compile, and `fr_s11_o_nonblock_matches_libc` checks the value against `libc` (a unix dev-dependency only). Not yet compiled or run. |
| **FR-S13** | *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, `INTENT.md` D37, #784)* **Keys in the OS keystore.** `secret.key`, `transport.key` (server and node) and device keys are held in the OS keystore where one answers (macOS Keychain, Windows Credential Manager, Linux Secret Service; the `keyring` crate, service `dev.darkpyonix.ember`, account `<file name>@<canonical dir>`), with the 0600 file as the fallback. `EMBER_KEYSTORE=file\|os\|auto` (default `auto`: the keystore when a probe answers, else the file; `os` refuses to start without one). Migration: an existing file is copied into the keystore, read back and compared, then deleted with a `<file>.migrated` marker; a failed write or a mismatched read-back keeps the file. Only the binaries install the OS store, so library callers and tests use the file unless they pass a store. Residual risk in the install guide (`#keystore`). | Tests `fr_s13_*` (`ember/node/tests/fr_s13_keystore.rs`, `ember/server/src/accounts/secrets.rs`) with the in-memory store: mode parsing, auto picks the file when the store is unavailable, file fallback, migration with marker, read-back mismatch and write failure keep the file, `secret.key` ciphertext still opens after migration. Not verified: a real Keychain, Credential Manager or Secret Service; app-held device keys (`ember/app`, follow-up). |

### §S status: server APIs behind the launcher (FR-L4, FR-L9, FR-S4, FR-S5)

- **Projects** are a table (`projects`, store migration 8), filled from existing sessions on
  migration and on every session creation. **Computer assignment** (FR-L4) is many-to-many
  (`project_computers`): `GET/POST /api/projects`, `PUT`/`DELETE
  /api/projects/{name}/computers/{computer_id}` (`local` = the main server). Removing a
  computer removes its assignments.
- **Session metadata** (FR-L9): `pinned` and `archived` columns (migration 9) and the existing
  title, changed with `PATCH /api/sessions/{id}` `{title?, pinned?, archived?}`. Not activity:
  `updated_at` is unchanged.
- **Push** gains `session_updated` and `project_updated` (additive; clients skip unknown types), so a second client sees renames, pins, archives and assignments
  without reloading.
- **Search** (FR-S4): an FTS5 index (`messages_fts`, `unicode61` tokenizer) of user and
  assistant messages, kept current by a trigger on `events` and backfilled on migration.
  `GET /api/search?q=&limit=` answers `{session_id, seq, kind, snippet, title, project,
  archived}`, best match first; each word matches as a prefix (Korean `오류` finds `오류가`).
  The bundled SQLite (`libsqlite3-sys` with rusqlite's `bundled` feature) is compiled with
  `SQLITE_ENABLE_FTS5`.
- **Export** (FR-L9): `GET /api/sessions/{id}/export`, `format: "ember-transcript"`, the
  record and every stored event.
- **Paths**: the routes above are `/api/...` with no version (D15, implemented in #63 and #85).
- **Fork** (FR-S5): `POST /sessions/{id}/fork {turn_seq?, title?}` creates a new session after a
  completed turn (default: the last). The parent's events up to that `turn_ended` are copied
  (native ids are not), and `session_forks` (migration 15) records the parent and turn;
  `GET /sessions/{id}` reports `fork_of`, `fork_turns` and `can_fork` (true when a completed turn
  exists). No wrapped agent forks from an arbitrary turn headlessly, so the fork starts a fresh
  agent session and its first message carries the copied conversation as a system notice. A
  session without a completed turn answers 409 and `can_fork` is false. **[user, 2026-10-25]**
  "에이전트의 자체 포크를 사용하고 fallback으로 대화 복사를 해": a fork uses the agent's own fork
  when the agent has one, and the conversation copy only as the fallback.
  - **Native fork.** An adapter declares `native_fork()`: `Latest` (forks the whole native
    session), `AtTurn` (forks through a chosen turn) or `None`. When the parent has a native id
    and the adapter can fork from the chosen turn, `session_forks` records a pending native fork
    (migration 16: parent native id, turns to keep) and the copied-conversation notice is not sent. The fork
    starts lazily, at its first message: the adapter forks instead of resuming, and the new native
    id is stored through the usual `NativeSession` event. `Latest` is used only when the chosen turn
    is the parent's last (no user message after it); an earlier turn falls back to the copy.
  - **Fallback.** No capability, no native id, a turn the agent cannot fork from, or a native
    fork that fails at start: the fresh session is seeded with the copied conversation as before,
    and a notice says why.
  - **Per agent** (verified 2026-10-25 against the installed CLIs, see `docs/design/NATIVE-FORK.md`):
    Codex `thread/fork {threadId, lastTurnId}` is `AtTurn`; Claude Code `--resume <id>
    --fork-session` is `Latest` (`--resume-session-at <message uuid>` is a hidden print-mode flag
    that would need the message uuid from the session file, not used); ACP `session/fork
    {sessionId, cwd, mcpServers}` is `Latest` and only when the agent advertises
    `sessionCapabilities.fork` (unstable in the ACP schema); Antigravity (`agy`) has no fork
    (flags: `--continue`, `--conversation <id>`), so it always uses the copy. The client action
    (fork from a turn, or from the last turn in the menu) is written, not yet compiled.

---

## §A: Agent wrapping (E2: native behaviour preserved)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-A1** | Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity and OMP (oh-my-pi) run on the main server as their own unmodified CLIs. [user] | Each agent's version is the vendor's release; no agent binary or package is patched. |
| **FR-A2** | Each agent is driven through its own non-interactive protocol, chosen per agent: Claude Code via `--print --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json` with `--permission-prompt-tool stdio`; Codex via `codex app-server` JSON-RPC; Antigravity via its stream-json print mode, one process per turn; other agents (including OMP) via the Agent Client Protocol (ACP). *[provisional]* ACP is also a candidate for Claude Code, Codex and Gemini through their ACP adapters; the per-agent choice compares native-behaviour preservation (E2) between the vendor's own headless protocol and its ACP adapter (`INTENT.md` Q6). | An integration test per agent runs a turn that reads a file, edits it and runs a command, and verifies the normalised events (`FR-A3`). |
| **FR-A3** | All agents' output is normalised into one event model (message, tool call, tool result, approval request, usage, turn end) driving one session state machine. | Adding an agent requires an adapter only; the UI, storage and A2A need no change. Matches `ember/proxy/dpx/agents/`'s adapter rule. |
| **FR-A4** | The agent's own settings, models, modes, session IDs and resume keep working as they do natively. | A session started in Ember can be resumed with the agent's own CLI on the main server, and vice versa where the agent supports it. |
| **FR-A5** | Tool approvals: allow once, always allow (for this session and tool kind), deny; a list of pending approvals; an opt-in mode that approves everything. | For agents with no headless approval channel (Antigravity), approval is obtained through the agent's own hook mechanism (a pre-tool-use hook calling back to the main server), not by patching the agent. **Questions and the tool policy.** *[provisional, 2026-11-07, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]* What the automatic modes may do with a tool is one function, `approval_policy(tool, needs_person)` in `ember/server/src/events.rs`, and one table (see "FR-A5 tool policy" below). An agent's question to the person (Claude Code's `AskUserQuestion`) is its own event, `QuestionAsked {question_id, questions }`, answered by `QuestionAnswered`; it is never an approval, is never answered automatically, and is answered or skipped through `POST /api/sessions/{id}/questions/{question_id}/answer` (`{"answers":[{"question_id":"q0","values":["blue"]}]}` or `{"decline":true}`; 404 unknown session or question not pending, 400 invalid answers, 422 unknown field). Only the human owner answers a question: the route needs the server token and is not exempt in `auth.rs`, so a team leader or an A2A runtime token may not (supervisor decision 2026-11-07; escalate to the leader if that is ever to change). A question that arrives in a shape Ember cannot read stays an approval with `needs_person: true`, so it still waits for a person. Answers are checked against the stored question (every question once; 1 to 8 values each; each value 1 to 1000 characters after trimming; one value for a question that is not multi-select); the stored `QuestionAnswered` replaces each value of a `secret` question by `***`. A free-text answer to an `AskUserQuestion` has `secret = false` (Claude Code exposes no secret flag), so it is stored as typed in the session events and shown in the transcript; only a question marked `secret` (Codex `isSecret`, not supported yet) is stored as `***`. An answer is sent to the agent first and recorded as `QuestionAnswered` only after the agent accepted it; a second answer is refused while the first is in flight, and any failure frees the question for a retry or a skip (follow-up, not yet compiled). When Claude Code withdraws a prompt with `control_cancel_request`, the adapter emits `PromptWithdrawn { prompt_id }` (stored as `kind: prompt_withdrawn`), which removes that approval or question from the pending lists and the feed and returns the session status to running, as a resolution does (follow-up, not yet compiled). A question waits for a person without end in Claude Code: nothing on Ember's side times it out; only the turn's interrupt (or `control_cancel_request`) withdraws it. The Antigravity adapter is different for approvals: an approval nobody answers is denied after 3480 s (`APPROVAL_TIMEOUT`, or 20 s when agy reports another hook timeout), with the notice "Approval for <tool> timed out; retry." (agy sends no question frame; none is built for it). The notices for the two automatic cases are fixed Korean sentences in 합니다체 (NFR-L4): "질문에는 자동으로 답하지 않습니다. 직접 답해 주세요." and "계획을 자동 승인했습니다."; the other server-written notices stay English by the repository's convention. Measured on Claude Code 2.1.319 (2026-11-07; recordings `ember/server/tests/fixtures/claude/question_answered_2.1.319.jsonl` and `question_unanswered_2.1.319.jsonl`, plan mode in `.scratch`): the `can_use_tool` frame of `AskUserQuestion` carries `requires_user_interaction: true` and `input.questions[{question, header, options[{label, description}], multiSelect}]`; the reply is `allow` with `updatedInput` = the original input plus `answers` keyed by the question text (a multi-select question joins its values with ", ", not yet measured); an `allow` without `answers` gives the model the tool result "The user did not answer the questions."; the flag was measured only on `AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode`, and an ordinary tool has no such key. Codex (`item/tool/requestUserInput`, schema known, NOT measured), ACP agents and Antigravity questions are NOT supported and stay refused. Tests (merged (#667, #675), CI compiled and ran its tests): `fr_a5_approval_policy_table`, `fr_a5_question_notice_texts_are_fixed`, `fr_a5_an_old_approval_row_without_needs_person_still_parses_as_false`, `fr_a5_question_events_round_trip_and_set_the_status`, `fr_a5_secret_answers_are_stored_as_stars_and_others_as_given`, `fr_a5_answers_must_fit_their_questions`, `fr_a5_user_question_tool_is_exactly_ask_user_question` (`events.rs`); `fr_a5_recorded_ask_user_question_becomes_a_question`, `fr_a5_question_reply_equals_the_recorded_answer_frame`, `fr_a5_declining_a_question_equals_the_recorded_unanswered_frame`, `fr_a5_question_reply_validates_and_joins_multi_select`, `fr_a5_unparsable_question_input_stays_an_approval_that_needs_a_person`, `fr_a5_needs_person_is_the_frames_flag_and_absent_means_false`, `fr_a5_restored_question_input_has_the_recorded_shape` (`agents/claude_code.rs`); `fr_a5_a_question_is_a_question_event_and_allow_always_does_not_apply_to_it`, `fr_a5_a_recorded_question_is_answered_with_the_measured_reply`, `fr_a5_a_declined_question_replies_allow_without_answers`, `fr_a5_allow_always_on_a_plan_is_not_remembered` (`tests/claude_code.rs`, fixtures `fake-claude-question.sh`, `fake-claude-question-reply.sh`, `fake-claude-plan.sh`); `fr_a5_approve_all_does_not_answer_a_question_but_answers_a_normal_approval`, `fr_a5_switching_approve_all_on_skips_a_pending_question`, `fr_a5_approve_all_allows_a_plan_with_a_notice_and_never_a_flagged_unknown_tool`, `fr_a5_switching_approve_all_on_allows_a_pending_plan_and_skips_a_flagged_tool`, `fr_a5_a_question_is_answered_recorded_and_its_secret_is_not_stored`, `fr_a5_declining_a_question_is_recorded_without_answers`, `fr_a5_a_turn_end_withdraws_a_pending_question`, `fr_a5_an_agent_without_questions_refuses_to_answer_one`, `fr_a5_question_route_statuses`, `fr_a5_question_route_declines` (`tests/session_flow.rs`); `fr_a5_the_question_route_is_not_reachable_with_a_runtime_token` (`tests/fr_s9_token_auth.rs`) and `fr_a5_the_question_answer_route_is_not_token_exempt` (`auth.rs`). Follow-up (written test first, **not yet compiled or run**): `fr_a5_prompt_withdrawn_round_trips_and_returns_to_running` (`events.rs`), `fr_a5_a_cancel_request_withdraws_the_approval_or_question_it_names` (`agents/claude_code.rs`), `fr_a5_an_adapter_failure_leaves_no_answer_event_and_the_retry_succeeds`, `fr_a5_a_failed_decline_can_be_followed_by_an_answer`, `fr_a5_a_double_answer_is_refused_while_the_first_is_in_flight`, `fr_a5_a_turn_end_releases_the_keys_of_its_session`, `fr_a5_session_end_releases_the_keys_of_its_session`, `fr_a5_a_withdrawn_question_leaves_the_pending_list_and_the_feed_while_the_turn_runs`, `fr_a5_a_withdrawn_approval_leaves_the_feed_while_the_turn_runs` (`tests/session_flow.rs`). |
| **FR-A6** | Supported agents are detected automatically on the main server, with their installed versions. | Detection runs at startup and on demand; a missing agent is shown as not installed, not as an error. |
| **FR-A6.1** | *[provisional]* Detection is per computer and never invents a version: `POST /api/computers/{id}/harnesses/detect` re-runs detection now (no cache) and answers `[{harness, installed, version, path, detected_at}]`. `harness` is the agent kind string of `GET /api/agents` (`claude-code`, `codex`, `antigravity`, and each configured ACP name such as `omp` or a Gemini ACP entry; Gemini is the `gemini` ACP preset of `FR-A10`, offered when `gemini` is listed in `EMBER_ACP_AGENTS`). `version` is `null` when the CLI is absent or its `--version` output cannot be parsed (a version must start with a digit); `path` is the resolved executable or `null`; `detected_at` is epoch milliseconds. A registered computer other than the local one answers 501 `not_implemented` with a message, and an unknown computer id 404. `GET /api/agents` keeps its shape and gains `path` and `detected_at` additively. | Tests `fr_a6_1_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_a6_1_harness_detect.rs`: a fake CLI printing `9.9.9 (Claude Code)` gives installed, `9.9.9`, its path and a fresh `detected_at`; an absent CLI gives `installed:false`, `version:null`, `path:null` (never `1.0.0`); garbage output gives installed with `version:null`; replacing the CLI changes the answer on the next call; 404 and 501; `GET /api/agents` keeps `kind`, `installed`, `version`; no token is 401 and the path is in the guest-door owner corpus. **Implemented for the local computer, not compiled or run (CI builds).** **Open: the node probe.** A node has no harness detection (`ember/node` `envinfo` lists no agent CLI), so another computer is 501 until a probe is added to the node; it needs a second real node to verify. The app still shows `1.0.0` when the version is missing (`ember/app/src/ui/cr/agents.rs`); the hand-off is in `docs/design/` (Harness detect API). |
| **FR-A7** | The main server manages MCP servers centrally and injects them into each agent session at creation. | An MCP server added once is visible to every agent that supports MCP. *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]* The `ember-channels` entry for Claude Code is skipped when the user's own Claude Code configuration already names that server, failing closed when that configuration cannot be read; stale `ember-mcp-*.json` private config files from a crashed run are removed at server start. The cleanup decides by modification time alone: after a server restart a surviving session, or a second server on the same TMPDIR, can lose a file more than an hour old and then fail to reconnect its MCP servers; accepted. The name check does not read plugin-provided servers, `enabledMcpjsonServers` or settings files, or claude.ai connector servers, and on Windows the user config is looked up through `HOME` only (`USERPROFILE` is ignored); a name taken through those is not detected and which entry wins is not measured. Details and tests: FR-T12. |
| **FR-A8** | Scheduled tasks: cron expressions (with time zone), fixed intervals, and one-off runs; each run either continues an existing session or starts a new one. Agents may create schedules from within a conversation. *[provisional, #759]* An **event routine** (`kind: {type: event, on: ci_failed, repository: owner/name, branch?}`, `FR-L23`) has no planned trigger; the server polls each project's GitHub CI through the project's computer `gh` (`POST /git/github` with `failed_runs: true`, the `FR-G13` path; every 120 s, `EMBER_CI_POLL_SECS`) and starts the routine once per failed workflow run of that repository (and branch, when given) created at or after the routine. Polling, not webhooks, because Ember assumes no public endpoint and stores no GitHub token. | Missed triggers while the server was down are detected and reported, not silently dropped. A failed run starts each matching unpaused event routine exactly once: the (routine, run id) pair is stored before the run, so a later poll or a restart does not start it again, and the run is recorded in `schedule_runs` like a scheduled one with the run's creation time as `scheduled_at`; failures from before the routine, on another branch or another repository start nothing (tests `ember/server/tests/fr_l23_ci_routines.rs`, `ember/client/tests/schedules_flow.rs` `fr_l23_event_routine_round_trips`). |
| **FR-A9** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D18; **changed by D30, 2026-10-25**: in the client a member takes only the harness, and the account choice and proxy come from the harness's profile (`FR-L17`); the member API below stays until the profile store replaces it]* Creating a member (`FR-L8`) takes the harness and the account choice (fixed account or switching pipeline, `FR-U1`), and optionally a model proxy path (`FR-U6`). | A member is created and run from those choices alone; neither or both account choices are rejected. Built, awaiting CI (#129): `members.rs`, tests `fr_a9_*`. *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* `team spawn` with neither choice keeps defaulting to the pipeline; the exactly-one check stays in the member API. |
| **FR-A10** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D40, 2026-11-06, option "(a) Gemini CLI 어댑터 추가" narrowed by option "(c)" the same day; details provisional]* The Gemini CLI runs as an ACP agent named `gemini` with a built-in preset: command `gemini`, args `["--acp"]` (see "Gemini CLI facts" for the flag), version args `--version`. The user can override the preset with `EMBER_ACP_AGENTS`. **Opt-in like `omp`**: the agent is offered (and shows in `GET /api/agents` and in detection) only when `gemini` is listed in `EMBER_ACP_AGENTS`; `EMBER_GEMINI_BIN` picks the binary. **Flag fallback** (decided with the open docs conflict below): if a start with the preset's own `--acp` fails within 2 s and a one-off run of the same command exits non-zero with an unknown or unrecognised option, flag or argument message, the start is retried once with `--experimental-acp`; an overridden `args` list is never retried; which flag the real CLI accepts is UNVERIFIED. | Tests `fr_a10_*` in `ember/server/src/agents/acp/tests.rs` (preset fields, args, opt-in and override, the rejection decision on sample outputs) and `ember/server/tests/fr_a10_gemini.rs` (a fake `gemini` script: a turn through the session layer, the retry with `--experimental-acp`). The ACP read/edit/permission mapping is the generic adapter's (tests of `omp`); it is not repeated for Gemini. Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. **Known consequence of the typed events:** a server rollback below this version cannot decode stored `TurnError` events (the client wire maps unknown events to `Unknown`, the server store does not). **Upstream text is redacted** (see `FR-A10b`, `FR-A10c`), and the `gemini` child gets an allow-listed environment, not the server's: only `HOME`, `PATH`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`, `USER`, `TERM`, the `XDG_*` directories, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_*`, `GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_VERTEXAI`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, the proxy variables (both cases) and `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` (`GEMINI_ENV_ALLOW` in `acp.rs`, `EnvPolicy::Allowlist`; `omp` and configured agents keep `EnvPolicy::Inherit`). Test `fr_a10_env_*`: a fake script dumping its environment lacks `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` (a stand-in for a server secret) and has `PATH` and `HOME`. The flag-rejection decision needs both the flag (`--acp` or the word `acp`) and an unknown, unrecognized, invalid or bad option, flag or argument; "invalid API key option" alone does not retry (`fr_a10_flag_rejection_needs_the_flag_*`). The probe child is killed and reaped after its 2 s (`fr_a10_probe_*`). **The probe has four outcomes** (`ProbeOutcome` in `acp.rs`): `Exited{ok,text}` (the text feeds the rejection decision), `StillRunning` (after 2 s), `SpawnFailed(kind, redacted text)` and `WaitFailed(redacted text)`. Only `StillRunning` means the flag was accepted; `SpawnFailed` and `WaitFailed` say nothing about the flag, so there is no retry and a `warn` names the outcome and the io error kind. A spawn that fails with ETXTBSY ("text file busy", a script written a moment ago while another thread forks) is retried up to 3 times, 20 ms apart (#546). **The agent's stderr file is redacted (#544, `INTENT.md` D45 [provisional]).** Under the session shim (`FR-S8`) the agent's stderr goes to `<shim dir>/<id>.log` line by line through `redact_upstream_uncapped` (the same rules as `redact_upstream`, without the 300-character cap, so a debugging line stays whole; a line over 64 KiB is split). The file is created 0600 and the shim dir is made 0700 (an existing one is tightened). A log whose shim is gone (no `<id>.json`) is removed after 7 days (`LOG_RETENTION`) when the next shim starts. The redaction is a heuristic: a project id that looks like a word passes. Tests `fr_s8_shim_stderr_log_is_redacted_and_private` and `fr_s8_shim_logs_older_than_the_retention_are_swept` in `ember/server/tests/session_shim.rs`, not yet compiled or run. **UNVERIFIED, the allow-list may be too tight for a real install:** variables a real Gemini CLI install may need and `GEMINI_ENV_ALLOW` does not carry include `GEMINI_MODEL`, `GEMINI_CLI_HOME`, `NODE_OPTIONS` and the nvm/npm/volta/asdf variables that put `node` on `PATH` (an `npx` or nvm install may fail to start under the cleared environment). Nothing was run against the real CLI. |
| **FR-A10a** | Detection (`FR-A6.1`) reports `gemini` installed with the parsed version and path, or `installed:false`, `version:null`. A version is never invented. | Fake `gemini` printing `0.99.1` gives `0.99.1`; absent gives `null`, never `1.0.0`; garbage gives installed with `null`. Tests `fr_a10a_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_a10_gemini.rs`. Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. |
| **FR-A10b** | Authentication is the user's own: a Gemini API key (`GEMINI_API_KEY`) or a Gemini Code Assist (Enterprise) licence; Vertex AI is UNVERIFIED after the 2026-06-18 change. "Login with Google" for individual Google AI Pro, Ultra and free accounts is not supported, because the Gemini CLI no longer serves them (`INTENT.md` D40, sources in the decision): for such an account the session shows "Gemini CLI no longer serves individual accounts, use Antigravity" and the card says so. Ember never stores, reads or copies Gemini credentials (`~/.gemini/`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`); it starts the CLI as the user, with the user's environment. When the CLI reports that authentication is missing or failed, the session shows "sign in needed" with the instruction to set the key. How the CLI signals it over ACP (`authenticate` error, JSON-RPC error code or message) is UNVERIFIED. **Typed state:** a new event `turn_error` with `code: "auth_required"` and the message `sign in needed: set GEMINI_API_KEY (individual Google accounts are no longer served by the Gemini CLI, use Antigravity)`, followed by a failed `turn_ended`. The classifier is a heuristic (JSON-RPC code `-32000`, or `authenticat`, `unauthorized`, `api key`, `sign in`, `login`, `credentials`, `401` in the message or data). A `session/new` that fails this way does not fail the start: each turn retries it (so a key set later works at the next start of the process), and if the `initialize` answer lists the method id `gemini-api-key` Ember sends `authenticate` with that id (the id only, never a credential; the id is UNVERIFIED). | Fake agent answering `session/new` or `session/prompt` with an authentication error gives the typed state `auth_required`, not a crash (`fr_a10b_*` in `acp/tests.rs`, and a fake `gemini` script in `fr_a10_gemini.rs`). No test reads `~/.gemini`. The app shows the card as API key and Enterprise only. Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. **Redaction and one behaviour change for all ACP agents:** an authentication error from `session/new` no longer fails the start for any ACP agent, including `omp`; it becomes `auth_required` with the generic text `sign in needed: the <agent> agent reported that authentication is missing or failed` (no `GEMINI_API_KEY` mention), and the next prompt retries `session/new`. Tests `fr_a10b_an_omp_authentication_error_on_session_new_*` and `fr_a10b_a_normal_omp_turn_is_unchanged`. The `session/new` warning, the flag-fallback context and every error text built from `RpcError` pass through `redact_upstream` (below); the raw text is logged only at `debug`, which is not for sharing. |
| **FR-A10c** | Usage limits and quota errors come from Gemini and are never invented. A quota or rate-limit error ends the turn with a typed error (`quota_exceeded`, with the CLI's message), and the session stays usable for a later turn. The documented limits are in "Gemini CLI facts"; Ember shows them only as documentation text, not as a live count. | Fake agent returning a quota error ends the turn with `quota_exceeded`; the next turn runs. The wording of the real error is UNVERIFIED. **Typed state:** `turn_error` with `code: "quota_exceeded"` and the agent's own message, followed by a failed `turn_ended`. The classifier is a heuristic on the message and data (`resource_exhausted`, `quota`, `rate limit`, `too many requests`, or the number `429` as a whole word); it wins over the auth match. Tests `fr_a10c_*`. **Redaction (`redact_upstream` in `acp.rs`, tests `fr_a10_redact_*`):** the message of `quota_exceeded` and every error text from the agent is whitespace and control characters collapsed to single spaces; Google API keys (`AIza` + 35 characters), `Bearer` and `Authorization` values, `sk-`, `ghp_`, `ya29.` style tokens, e-mail addresses and any run of 32 or more `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` with a letter and a digit become `[redacted]`; the result is at most 300 characters, ending in `...` when cut. Known gaps: a secret that looks like an ordinary word, a 32+ run of letters or digits only, a secret split by spaces, a long hyphenated identifier or UUID is redacted by mistake. A fake agent whose quota error carries a key and an e-mail yields a `TurnError` with neither, and a default-level `tracing` capture shows neither (`tests/fr_a10_gemini.rs`). Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. |
| **FR-A10d** | The Home usage chip for Gemini shows "not measured" (`FR-U7` "unknown") unless the CLI reports usage in `session/update`; remaining allowance (`FR-U4`) is "unknown". | Fake agent without usage gives "unknown"; with usage, the counts match the report. The adapter reads usage only from the prompt answer's `usage` (as for `omp`); with none, no `usage` event is stored and `GET /api/sessions/{id}/usage` answers `reported:false`. Tests `fr_a10d_*` and `fr_a10_a_turn_runs_through_the_preset_and_fr_a10d_reports_no_usage`. Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. |
| **FR-A10e** | Model selection uses the preset's model template: the model id is passed with `-m <model>` at start, or `unstable_setSessionModel` where the agent advertises it. The model list is what the CLI reports; Ember hard-codes none. Which of the two the real CLI supports over ACP is UNVERIFIED. The preset uses `-m {model}` (`fr_a10_gemini_process_args_carry_the_model_and_no_instructions_flag`); `unstable_setSessionModel` is not called. | Fake agent: the chosen model reaches the process args or the set-model call. **Implemented for the process args.** Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. |

Compiled and run by CI at 68ad53c (green); the real CLI stays UNVERIFIED. |

Never run. The card stays "preparing" until then. |

> The 09-22 transcript parsers in `ember/proxy/dpx/agents/` (Claude Code, Codex) satisfy part of
> `FR-A3` for reading existing history and are kept (`INTENT.md` D13).

> **ACP adapter (`FR-A2`, #53).** `ember/server/src/agents/acp.rs` is one generic ACP client (protocol
> version 1, schema `schema-v1.24.1`): `initialize`, `session/new`, native resume through
> `session/resume` (or `session/load` with the replay dropped), `session/prompt`, `session/cancel`;
> `session/update` mapped to `FR-A3` events; `session/request_permission` mapped to `FR-A5`
> approvals by option kind (`allow_once` / `allow_always` / `reject_once`); the client methods
> `fs/read_text_file`, `fs/write_text_file` and `terminal/*` served on the main server, or on the
> session's computer through its node API (`FR-X1`). ACP agents are configured, not compiled in
> (`EMBER_ACP_AGENTS`, preset `omp` = `omp acp`), and appear as agent kinds by their configured
> name. *[provisional]* Covered by tests against an in-process fake ACP agent; the `FR-A2`
> acceptance test against real OMP (`ember/server/tests/acp_omp.rs`, `EMBER_E2E_OMP=1`) has not been
> run (OMP is not installed on the main server). Gaps: no account isolation (`FR-U2`) or
> transcript import for ACP agents; instructions reach agents without an instructions flag in
> front of the first prompt of each process.

> **Gemini CLI facts (`FR-A10`, read 2026-11-06, nothing run: the CLI is not installed on this machine).**
> Each fact is *verified-from-docs* (read on the page named) or UNVERIFIED.
>
> - **ACP flag.** `gemini --acp` (verified-from-docs, ACP mode page, https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/blob/main/docs/cli/acp-mode.md). The CLI reference (https://geminicli.com/docs/cli/cli-reference/) still lists only `--experimental-acp` ("Experimental feature") and not `--acp`, so the docs disagree. The version that renamed it is UNVERIFIED. The preset uses `--acp`; if detection finds a CLI that rejects it, the fallback is `--experimental-acp` (to be settled by `FR-A10g`). Earlier Ember docs said `--experimental-acp`.
> - **Install.** `npm install -g @google/gemini-cli`, `brew install gemini-cli`, or `npx @google/gemini-cli` (verified-from-docs, https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli). The Node version is UNVERIFIED. Channels: `latest` (weekly), `preview`, `nightly`.
> - **Version output.** `--version` / `-v` "Show CLI version number and exit" (verified-from-docs, CLI reference). The exact output format is UNVERIFIED; `FR-A6.1` parses a leading digit.
> - **Authentication** (verified-from-docs, https://geminicli.com/docs/get-started/authentication/): Sign in with Google (cached locally; the file name is not on the page, `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json` is UNVERIFIED); `GEMINI_API_KEY` (AI Studio key); Vertex AI by Application Default Credentials (`gcloud auth application-default login`), a service account JSON via `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, or `GOOGLE_API_KEY`, with `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` and `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION`. Environment variables can also live in `.gemini/.env` (searched upward from the working directory, then `~/.gemini/.env`; first file found only). Headless mode uses cached credentials, or needs an API key or Vertex variables if never signed in. `~/.gemini/settings.json` holding credentials is UNVERIFIED.
> - **Availability change.** Since 2026-06-18 the Gemini CLI "stops serving requests for Google AI Pro, Google AI Ultra, and free tier individual accounts" in favour of the Antigravity CLI; Enterprise Code Assist licences and API-key authentication are stated as unaffected; Vertex AI is not addressed (verified-from-docs, https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/discussions/28017; also https://9to5google.com/2026/06/17/gemini-cli-code-assist-shutting-down/). The quota page and CLI reference still describe the older limits and carry no notice of this. Whether Vertex AI still works is UNVERIFIED.
> - **Documented limits** (https://geminicli.com/docs/resources/quota-and-pricing/, may predate the change above): Login with Google free, 1000 model requests per user per day; Gemini API key free, 250 per user per day (Flash only); Google AI Pro 1,500 and Ultra 2,000 per day; pay-as-you-go API keys "varies by pricing tier"; Vertex "dynamic shared quota" or provisioned throughput. Per-minute limits: "limited per user per minute", no number given. How a quota error appears is not documented (UNVERIFIED). `/stats model` shows usage inside the CLI.
> - **ACP surface** (verified-from-docs, ACP mode page): `initialize`, `authenticate`, `newSession`, `loadSession`, `prompt`, `cancel`, `setSessionMode`, `unstable_setSessionModel`, a file system proxy, and `session/request_permission` for MCP tool permission. The page does not mention fork, `terminal/create` or resume (`session/resume`); the permission option kinds and whether the agent advertises `loadSession` in `initialize` are UNVERIFIED. The adapter already uses `session/load` where `session/resume` is absent.
> - **Sessions.** Stored in `~/.gemini/tmp/<project_hash>/chats/`, hash from the project root; resume with `--resume` / `-r` (`latest`, index or UUID) (verified-from-docs, https://geminicli.com/docs/cli/session-management/). The file format is UNVERIFIED, so no transcript import (`FR-A3` history) is specified.
> - **Model and modes.** `-m` / `--model` (default `auto`); `--approval-mode` `default`, `auto_edit`, `yolo`, `plan`; `--yolo` is deprecated; `--include-directories` adds workspace directories (verified-from-docs, CLI reference).
> - **Instructions.** `GEMINI.md` is loaded from `~/.gemini/GEMINI.md`, the workspace and parents, and just-in-time from touched directories; `context.fileName` in `settings.json` renames it (verified-from-docs, https://geminicli.com/docs/cli/gemini-md/).

### FR-A5 tool policy

*[provisional, 2026-11-07, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]* One table, read by the event pump, `Sessions::set_approve_all` and the adapters' "allow always" memory (`approval_policy` in `ember/server/src/events.rs`). Tool names match exactly and case-sensitively; a prefixed MCP tool such as `mcp__x__AskUserQuestion` is an ordinary tool.

| 도구 | 자동 승인 모드 | AllowAlways | 사람 응답 필요 |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| `AskUserQuestion` | 자동 승인하지 않음 | 기억하지 않음 | 필요: 답을 고르거나 건너뜀 |
| `ExitPlanMode` | 자동 승인하고 '계획을 자동 승인했습니다.'를 기록 | 기억하지 않음 | 승인 모드 밖에서는 필요 |
| `requires_user_interaction` 표시가 있는 그 밖의 도구 | 자동 승인하지 않음 | 기억하지 않음 | 필요 |
| 그 밖의 모든 도구 | 자동 승인 | 기억함 | 필요 없음, 단 승인 모드가 꺼져 있으면 사람이 허용 |

Status: written, not yet compiled. The mark `requires_user_interaction: true` was measured only on `AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode` in Claude Code 2.1.319, so the third row is a safety net for tools not yet seen, not a measured list. Only Claude Code sends the mark; the other adapters pass `needs_person: false`.

### §A Antigravity: the reinforced hook (FR-A5, `INTENT.md` D14) [user]

Antigravity (`agy`) has no headless approval channel and no ACP. Ember runs it with
`--dangerously-skip-permissions --sandbox` and makes its own `PreToolUse` hook (matcher `*`, in a
per-session folder passed with `--add-dir`) the only gate. **[user, 2026-10-03]** the "reinforced
hook" design, relayed by the darkpyonix leader. Implementation: `ember/server/src/agents/antigravity.rs`.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-A5a** | The hook config sets an explicit `timeout` (3600 s); Ember answers `deny` before it expires (route 3480 s < hook curl 3540 s < agy 3600 s) and shows "approval timed out; retry". If agy reports another timeout, Ember denies at 20 s. | Unit tests `unanswered_approval_times_out_as_deny_with_a_retry_notice`, `recorded_hooks_listing`; test 3 below. |
| **FR-A5b** | The hook prints exactly `allow` or a `deny` and exits 0 on any error (curl missing, unreachable server, non-2xx, unknown or revoked token, empty or unexpected body). It never answers `ask`. | `hook_script_*` unit tests, `a_hook_call_after_the_run_ended_is_denied_by_the_hook`; tests 4. |
| **FR-A5c** | A fresh random token per run, revoked when it ends. Ending, interrupting or shutting down a turn denies every pending approval and resolves its card; hook calls outside a turn are denied. | `broker_waits_for_the_answer`, `turn_end_denies_pending_cards_and_later_hooks`. |
| **FR-A5d** | Before **every** turn `agy -p /hooks --output-format json` must list Ember's hook from this session's folder, enabled, `PreToolUse` matcher `*`; otherwise the turn is refused. | `a_hook_that_was_not_loaded_fails_the_turn`, `recorded_hooks_listing`. |
| **FR-A5e** | *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* `--sandbox` is off by default (`EMBER_AGY_SANDBOX=0`) and the hook is the gate; `EMBER_AGY_SANDBOX=1` passes it (#65). The agy version is pinned (`PINNED_VERSION` = 1.2.16); another version gets a notice and runs read-only: no `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, only read-only tools inside the workspaces, everything else denied without asking. | `only_the_pinned_version_is_gated`, `read_only_policy_*`, `another_agy_version_runs_read_only`. |
| **FR-A5f** | No global agy configuration is written: the hook lives in the session folder, so the user's own agy sessions are unaffected. Tool calls naming the session folder are denied (it holds the token and the hook). Read-only tools are free only inside the workspaces and agy's `brain/`; elsewhere they ask. | `the_session_root_is_off_limits`, `reads_are_free_only_inside_the_workspaces`. |

**Hook tests on agy 1.2.16** (2026-10-03, macOS, model `gemini-3.6-flash-low`; standalone
`sh` hook logging its stdin; throwaway folders under `.scratch/agy-tests/`; harmless commands
only). Every run: `agy -p "<prompt>" --output-format stream-json --add-dir <folder>/root
--new-project --model gemini-3.6-flash-low --dangerously-skip-permissions [extra]` from
`<folder>/work`, `root/.agents/hooks.json` = one `PreToolUse` hook, matcher `*`. The prompt asked for
`touch marker` unless noted; "marker" = whether the file appeared anywhere in the folder.
Recordings: `ember/server/tests/fixtures/agy/*_1.2.16.*`.

| # | Test | Setup | Observed | Result |
| - | ---- | ----- | -------- | ------ |
| 0 | Hook loaded from an untrusted `--add-dir` folder | `agy -p /hooks --output-format json --add-dir root` | JSON `command.data.hooks[]` with `name`, `enabled`, `source` (the file), `actions[].event/matcher/timeout_seconds` (3600) | PASS |
| 1 | Hook runs under skip-permissions | hook answers `allow`, timeout 3600 | hook called once (payload logged), `touch` ran: marker created (in the session folder: the model chose it as `Cwd`) | PASS |
| 2 | `deny` blocks | hook `deny`; also with `--sandbox` | step `ERROR`, `tool call denied by pre-tool hook: …`; no marker, both runs | PASS |
| 3 | Long timeout holds the tool | (a) timeout 600, hook sleeps 90 s then `deny`; (b) no timeout (default 30), sleeps 45 s then `deny`; (c) timeout 600, sleeps 70 s then `allow` | (a) tool waited 90.3 s, denied, no marker; (b) hook killed at 31 s, `failed: command failed: signal: killed`, **no marker** (AionCore saw 1.1.9 run the tool here); (c) tool ran after 70.2 s | PASS |
| 4 | Hook failures | exit 1, no output; exit 2 with `allow` on stdout; `kill -9` itself; prints `this is not json {`; command `/nonexistent/…` | all: step `ERROR` (`exit status 1` / `exit status 2` / `signal: killed` / `failed to unmarshal result` / `exit status 127`), no marker | PASS |
| 4+ | Other outputs (extra probes) | exit 0 with: empty stdout (blank line, or zero bytes); `{}`; `{"decision":""}`; `{"decision":"maybe"}`; `{"decision":"ask"}` | **empty stdout → tool RAN**; **`ask` → tool RAN** (skip-permissions approves it); `{}`, `""`, `maybe` → denied | FAIL-OPEN, compensated: Ember's hook never prints empty or `ask` (FR-A5b) |
| 5 | Sub-agents, `call_mcp_tool`, browser | (s) hook denies only `run_command`, prompt delegates `touch` to `invoke_subagent`; (m) hook `deny`, stdio test MCP server in `root/.agents/mcp_config.json`; (b) hook `deny`, `open_browser_url file://…` | (s) the sub-agent's `run_command` reached the hook (its own `conversationId`), denied, no marker; (m) MCP server got `initialize`/`tools/list`, `call_mcp_tool {ServerName, ToolName}` reached the hook, denied, server never got `tools/call`; (b) model reported the browser tools unavailable (no browser connected), no tool call | PASS (s, m); browser **untestable** here |
| 6 | Several hooks: deny wins | `a-allow`+`b-deny`; `a-deny`+`b-allow`; `allow` and `deny` hooks in two `--add-dir` folders | denied in all three; hooks run in order, `allow` does not stop later hooks, `deny` does | PASS |
| 7 | Parallel tool calls | one response with three `run_command` (`touch m1/m2/m3`), hook sleeps 5 s then `deny` | three hook calls (steps 2, 3, 4), run one after another, all denied, no markers | PASS |

Other observations (1.2.16): the hook inherits agy's environment; the session folder is listed
first in `workspacePaths` even when the project is also passed with `--add-dir`, and the model
picked it as `Cwd` (FR-A5f denies that); `--sandbox` refused shell writes even in the project
(`touch: marker: Operation not permitted`) while `write_to_file` worked; the stream now carries
`tool_info.output`, `tool_info.error.message` and `text_delta`; `--new-project` left one project
file per run in `~/.gemini/config/projects/` (not used by the adapter; the test files were
removed). Not verified: browser tools; the read-only fallback against a real different agy
version; the end-to-end adapter test (`EMBER_E2E_AGY=1`) on 1.2.16. Open: shell writes under
`--sandbox` (#65).

**#65 findings (agy 1.4.6, 2026-10-25, macOS, project on `/Volumes`).** The failure reproduces on 1.4.6
and is not caused by the external disk, `/Volumes` vs `/private` paths, or untrusted folders. agy
writes a Seatbelt profile to `~/.gemini/sandbox/profiles/exebox-*.sb` per command (deleted after
use; captured by polling). It allows writes to `/` and then explicitly denies writes to the
working directory, its `.git` and the enclosing repository's `.git`, plus system paths. The allow
list holds only temp dirs, agy's own brain/scratch folders and a few toolchain caches. Running
the captured profile with `sandbox-exec -f` reproduces `Operation not permitted` for `touch` in
the cwd and a subfolder, while the parent folder stays writable. The same happens when the cwd
is exactly a `trustedWorkspaces` entry (`--mode accept-edits|auto`, `--project` change nothing),
and a project `.agents/settings.json` with `write_file(<cwd>/)` did not change the profile. The agy docs say workspace
writes in the sandbox come from `write_file` rules in `permissions` of
`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`; headless runs apparently get no such grant. Cause: agy,
not Ember's wrapper. Not verified: a project under the home directory; a global `write_file`
rule (it would edit the user's global agy config, which FR-A5f forbids). Decided 2026-10-25 [user]: `EMBER_AGY_SANDBOX=0` by default. Options were:
keep `--sandbox` and accept that shell commands cannot write (file edits via `write_to_file`
still work), or add a global `write_file` allow rule, or set `EMBER_AGY_SANDBOX=0` (the hook stays the gate).

**Outcome:** tests 1–7 pass on 1.2.16 (browser tools untestable); Antigravity sessions on 1.2.16
run gated, any other version read-only.

## §X: Execution on computers (D4)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-X1** | Each computer runs one **ember node** (execution daemon) that carries out tool actions for sessions (file read/write, directory listing, search, command execution with PTY) and streams results back to the main server. | A CLI on the main server, wrapped, performs a read-edit-run cycle whose effects appear on the target computer's disk and processes only. |
| **FR-X2** | The wrapping is transparent to the agent: paths, working directory and environment the agent sees are the target computer's. | The agent's own "print working directory" and environment queries report the target computer's values. |
| **FR-X3** | A session's current computer can be switched. *[user, `INTENT.md` D20]* The default is the main server; there is a list of available computers; the user designates one. If the designated computer becomes unreachable, the session falls back to the main server automatically and returns when the computer is detected again. Every connection state change is notified to the agent as "now working on X (moved)", not as invalid. The session knows its current computer. | After a switch, the next tool action runs on the new computer; the environment description given to the agent is replaced, not appended (`FR-S7`). Disconnect the designated computer: the session continues on the main server and the agent receives a "moved" notice; reconnect it: the session returns and the agent is notified again. Fallback and return are implemented (#131): `PUT /api/sessions/{id}/designated-computer` designates, a watcher probes every 10 s, and a move needs 2 probes in a row to agree (debounce). A session without a designation never moves. Tests `fr_x3_*` and `fr_s7_*` in `ember/server/tests/computers.rs`; written, not yet compiled or run (CI builds). *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* The detection default stays 2 probes x 10 s and is configurable (`EMBER_FALLBACK_PROBES`, `EMBER_FALLBACK_INTERVAL_SECS`). The designated and the current computer are exposed to clients as read-only fields (E5): `designated_computer_id` beside the current computer. |
| **FR-X4** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` Q5]* A background job started on one computer keeps running after the session switches away, and its completion is reported into the session. | Start a long build on A, switch to B, finish the build on A: the session receives the result. |
| **FR-X5** | ember node is the only Ember component required on a computer for agent work; it does not run agent CLIs or store transcripts. | Measured: daemon RSS stays bounded and independent of the number of sessions using that computer. *[provisional]* Budget: RSS grows by at most 64 MiB over 300 sessions after a 40-session warm-up. CI gate `ember/node/tests/memory.rs` (in-process, Linux `/proc` or macOS `ps`); the per-session memory and CPU recorder is its ignored test `record_per_session_cost`. |
| **FR-X6** | *[user, confirmed 2026-10-25, `INTENT.md` D19]* The agent CLI's own API traffic leaves through the session's current computer, so the model provider sees that computer's IP. Built as a loopback HTTP CONNECT proxy per session (own credential) in `ember/server/src/egress_proxy.rs`: a start hook sets `HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` (`NO_PROXY` keeps loopback direct; *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* CONNECT only, plain `http://` is not proxied), each CONNECT asks the session's current computer and is dialled by that node's `/egress` (or directly on the main server). `EMBER_AGENT_EGRESS=off` disables it. | A session on computer A and one on computer B reach an IP-echo endpoint through their CLI's proxy setting and see A's and B's addresses; after a switch the address changes. Tests `fr_x6_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_x6_egress.rs` (written, not yet run; no build in sub-agent). Not verified: real CLIs honouring the proxy, and the IP-echo check on two computers (#130). *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* A tunnel open at a switch stays on its old computer until it closes, so a long stream can keep the old IP; when the session ends, its proxy closes its listener and tears down its open tunnels. |
| **FR-X7** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D33, 2026-10-25]* ember node reports its computer's status for the monitor (`FR-L21`): CPU total and per core, GPU, memory (used, pressure, swap), disk (used, free), network (send, receive, interface), temperatures and fans where the OS exposes them, and the process list (name, PID, CPU, memory, power where available), with the agent processes of sessions on that computer identified. Node side built (#330): `GET /metrics?top=` on the node and `GET /api/computers/{id}/metrics` on the server return one sample (CPU total and per core, memory and swap, disks, network counters, temperatures where exposed, the top processes by CPU then memory, GPU empty with a reason). Not built: fan speed, process power, identifying agent processes (the client matches sessions), GPU values, sampling only while watched (the node keeps one small `sysinfo` state). The client reads it: `Api::metrics` (`ember/client/src/api.rs`, tests `fr_x7_*` in `metrics_tests`) and the monitor sheet (FR-L21). The 컴퓨터 추가 sheet of the Project screen is specified under `FR-C1` and `FR-C2` (D39), not here. | Values are sampled only while a client watches (about 1 s) and stop when none does, keeping `FR-X5`'s memory budget. A metric the OS does not expose is reported as unknown, never estimated. Tested on macOS and Linux nodes. Tests: `fr_x7_*` in `ember/node/src/metrics.rs`, `ember/node/tests/node_api.rs`, `ember/server/tests/fr_x7_metrics_relay.rs` (not yet run: not verified on Linux or Windows; GPU not verified). |
| **NFR-X1** | *[provisional]* Before and after moving agents to the main server, measure whether local slowdown came from agent runtimes and transcripts (memory) or from builds and tests (CPU). | If CPU dominates, add a concurrency limit per computer on the main server. |

## §T: Agent-to-agent messaging (D5)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-T1** | Any session can list other sessions it may message and send a message to one, regardless of agent vendor, computer or account. | A Claude Code session and a Codex session on different computers and accounts exchange a message and a reply. |
| **FR-T2** | Each agent reaches A2A through an adapter in its own native idiom (D5), chosen before its built-in means of talking to other agents by its name, description and loading alone: for example an `ember` MCP server loaded upfront with `list_agents`/`send_message` in the native argument shapes. Built-in means (Claude Code `ListAgents`, `SendMessage`, `Agent`, and their equivalents) stay enabled and are never blocked or intercepted [user, 2026-10-04: "send message도 그냥 거르지 말고 모델이 알아서 쓰도록 하는게 맞는거 같아"]. The `ember-a2a` CLI stays as the shared path for people and debugging. Draft per agent: `docs/design/A2A-NATIVE.md`. | No agent is modified; adapters use only the agent's supported surfaces (MCP, settings, hooks, instruction files). Behavioural evaluation per agent: natural-language prompts in English and Korean ("consult X", "ask another agent", "다른 에이전트에게 물어봐") and negative controls, N ≥ 30 per prompt per condition, on a fixture with live sessions and a recorder of A2A calls. The built-ins stay enabled during measurement. Measured: the rate at which Ember A2A is the first A2A call, native calls at any position (counted as failures, never blocked), delivery confirmed by the recorder, false positives on negatives. Targets [user, 2026-10-04: "초안대로 확정하면 되긴 할거같은데"]: ≥ 95% Ember-first with the built-ins enabled, ≤ 2% false positives, re-measured whenever an agent CLI version changes. Leakage handling [provisional]: a built-in-first call is a recorded failure; a prompt above 5% built-in-first is fixed by changing the Ember tool's name, description or server instructions, never by blocking; claimed sends the recorder lacks count as delivery failures; detail in `docs/design/A2A-NATIVE.md`. Not implemented (#102). |
| **FR-T2a** | The runtime token is not exposed in the agent's shell environment: it lives in the Ember-launched adapter process (or an equivalent the agent's tools cannot read), is bound to the session and scoped to A2A, and expires. | `env`/`printenv` from the agent's shell and the agent's file tools cannot reveal a token that authorizes A2A calls. Today the token is exported as `EMBER_RUNTIME_TOKEN` to the agent process; not implemented (#102). |
| **FR-T3** | A delivered message enters the target session through the same path as a user message, with a header naming the sender session, its project/computer and a reply reference. | The target agent can reply with one call using the reply reference. |
| **FR-T4** | Messages to a sleeping (idle-released) session are queued durably and wake it. | Restart the main server with a message queued; it is delivered after restart. |
| **FR-T5** | Loop protection: per-session send rate and per-pair rate limits within a time window. | Two agents instructed to reply to each other forever are stopped by the limit, with a visible notice in both sessions. |
| **FR-T6** | Users can mention another session from the composer, and can turn A2A off per user or per session. | Off means sends to and from that session are rejected with a clear reason. |
| **FR-T8** | Completion and idle reports reach the delegating session without polling [user, 2026-10-04: "AionUI는 리더한테 각자 작업이 끝났고 한거가 전부 다 보고가 들어가더라고 idle로 들어가면 리더가 바로 확인 없이 알게되는 방식인거 같던데 그런 인터렉션이 필요할거같아."]. When a session that received work through A2A, a team task or a spawn ends its turn and goes idle, fails, or stalls, Ember pushes a report to the session that delegated the work (the team leader, or the sender of the message being worked on). The report holds: the outcome (done, failed, needs input, stalled), a summary of the last response, files changed and PRs or commits created during the turn, the error for a failure, and a reference to the full transcript. Triggers: turn end with the session idle; an error that ends the turn; an approval or question waiting on a person; no progress for a configurable stall time. Models: Claude Code's `notify_when_idle` and subagent hand-back, AionUI's teammate reports. | The report arrives as one A2A message in the delegating session's own idiom (FR-T2), with no polling or status call by the receiver. One report per turn end (deduplicated by session and turn); a turn that continues within a short settle window (e.g. an automatic follow-up) reports once at its real end; a stall report is sent at most once per stall; the server builds the report itself from the turn (outcome and stop reason, last-response summary, files changed, PRs or commits), not from the model choosing to send one; wake policy: each done report wakes an idle receiver at once, with reports arriving within a 2 s window batched into one turn, and failed, interrupted, needs-input and undeliverable reports always wake at once (rejected: AionUI's wait until every teammate has settled, because the user asked to know without checking); delivery is stored first and acknowledged only after the receiving turn succeeds, so a failed turn gets it again; an idle receiver is woken by a report and acts on it in a new turn, as a Claude Code cross-session message or subagent hand-back wakes an idle session; a busy receiver gets reports at its next tool round, several batched into one; a report is marked as a report and never makes the receiver reply automatically (a reply is an ordinary A2A message the receiver chooses to send); reports do not beget reports (when a turn woken by a report ends, its FR-T8 report goes only to that session's own delegator, never back to the teammate whose report woke it); reports count toward loop protection (FR-T3); a session with no delegator sends nothing. Tests: done, failed, needs-input and stall reports each arrive exactly once with the listed fields; an idle leader is woken by a teammate's completion report within 5 s and starts a turn; reports arriving while the leader is busy are delivered together at its next tool round; no report-reply loop forms between a leader and a teammate over repeated turns; no report without a delegator; a burst of short turns yields one report per real turn end. Not implemented (#102). The app shows a question waiting on a person as a card with answer controls, and counts it with approvals in the "needs you" badge (FR-T11). |
| **FR-T7** | A leader session can spawn teammate sessions, assign tasks, and read a shared task list and mailbox. | Tasks and mailbox are stored on the main server; each teammate keeps its own approvals. Built and verified with scripted agents (`ember/server/src/a2a/team.rs`, `ember/server/tests/a2a_team.rs`, #55). Preparation runbook and checklist for the real two-computer, two-account run (marking the steps that need the owner's second computer and CLI logins): `docs/design/MULTI-COMPUTER-TEAM-RUN.md` (#82). Real two-computer, two-account run remains UNVERIFIED until run by the owner. |
| **FR-T9** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D21]* Separate from team messages, an agent with permission can read another session's live and past terminal output and send it input or an interrupt, as A2A tools (models: herdr `pane read`, `pane send-text`, `pane send-keys`). | A session that is not answering is read by another: the reader sees its live output and its earlier output, sends text and an interrupt, and the target receives them. Without the permission each call is rejected with a reason. The grant is per session and revocable. Implemented (#132, `ember/server/src/a2a/terminal.rs`): read (last N lines, since a seq, follow with `wait_ms`), send text and interrupt; deny by default, allowed by a per-session user grant (`PUT /api/sessions/{id}/terminal-grants`) or for a team leader over its active teammates; each use is logged as a notice in the target's transcript. The `ember-a2a terminal read`, `send-text` and `interrupt` commands (`ember/server/src/a2a/client.rs`) and agent instructions (`A2a::instructions` in `ember/server/src/a2a/mod.rs`) are implemented (#147, PR #277), and all 5 integration tests (`fr_t9_authorized_read_returns_recent_output`, `fr_t9_unauthorized_read_is_denied`, `fr_t9_send_text_reaches_the_target`, `fr_t9_interrupt_stops_a_running_turn`, `fr_t9_cli_terminal_commands_and_permissions`) in `ember/server/tests/a2a_terminal.rs` pass in CI (`Cargo test (server)`, run `42395402010`). The N ≥ 30 per prompt per condition behavioral evaluation of agent tool choice (`FR-T2`) requires live multi-agent model runs and remains `[OWNER ACTION REQUIRED]` (tracked under `FR-T2`). *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* The implicit grant is leader to its own teammates only; a teammate needs an explicit user grant for the leader's terminal. |
| **FR-T10** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22]* A channel belongs to exactly one project. Every project has one default channel (all of its agents) and can create more, each with a chosen subset of the project's agents. A channel has a name, a member list (agent sessions and people) and a durable message log. | A created channel keeps its chosen agents and none other. A channel cannot be attached to a second project. A new project has its default channel. The log survives a restart of the main server and is the same on every client (`FR-T16`). Status: server API and the client list, room, polling and send are built (#331). Not built: `FR-T11`, `FR-T12` (@ mention, member picker, "reaches agent" marker), `FR-T13` to `FR-T15`, `FR-T17`, unread counts, push. The create-channel sheet is built (#499, see FR-L12). |
| **FR-T16** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22: "메인 서버에 사람 전용 대화 jsonl 파일이 필요하려나"; file layout provisional]* Channel messages, human-only conversation included, are stored on the main server in a per-channel append-only JSONL file of human messages, separate from the agents' native session files. Clients on any machine read it from the main server. | Two clients on different computers see the same messages in the same order. A message without a mention is in this file and in no agent's transcript. The exact line schema and path are [provisional]. **Status (guests):** a guest's post (`guest_router_with_dir`) is also one line with `from` = `guest:<id>` and an extra `name` (display name at writing time; no other line has `name`), with the same insert-append-rollback (500 on a failed append); written test first (`fr_t16_*` in `fr_t15_attribution.rs`), compiled and run by CI, passing on develop (2026-11-06). Agent and system messages stay out of the file. Start-up repair details and limits [provisional, 2026-11-07]: a crash fragment (a last line without a newline) is cut in place with `set_len` and its bytes are NOT saved aside (every human message is also in SQLite and is re-appended); `recover` uses its own log instance and shares no lock with the routers, so it runs synchronously before any router is served (comment in `main.rs` and `JsonlLog::recover`); there is no test of the foreign-owner refusal or of a failing fsync. **Recovery and hardening** [provisional, 2026-11-07, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]: written test first, NOT yet compiled or run. Each append is followed by `sync_data` (a failed sync is an append failure and the post is rolled back); the `channels` directory is created with mode 0700 (an existing one is never changed), a new file gets 0600, the file is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` (a symlinked `<id>.jsonl` is refused) and the handle must be a regular file owned by the effective user. A file larger than 1 GiB takes no further lines: the post is not refused, `append` returns `Ok(false)`, the SQLite row stays and one warning per channel is logged. At start (`JsonlLog::recover`, called in `main.rs` before the routers serve) the last 1 MiB of each channel file is read: a final line without a newline is cut back to the last newline, then the human rows of SQLite with a `seq` above the last line's `seq` are appended in order and synced. A symlink, a non-regular file, a foreign owner, a file over the cap, a last line that is not a JSON object with an integer `seq`, or a last line longer than the window leaves the file untouched with a warning (channel id and fixed reason, no message text); recovery never stops the start. Limits: the repair is append-only, so a row missing from the middle of the file is not repaired; per-person read cursors are not built. Tests: `fr_t16_recovery_cuts_a_partial_last_line_and_re_appends_from_sqlite`, `fr_t16_recovery_appends_the_rows_missing_at_the_end`, `fr_t16_recovery_leaves_an_up_to_date_file_byte_for_byte`, `fr_t16_recovery_writes_everything_to_a_missing_or_empty_file`, `fr_t16_recovery_does_not_repair_a_gap_in_the_middle`, `fr_t16_a_symlinked_file_is_skipped_by_recovery_and_refused_by_append`, `fr_t16_a_directory_named_like_the_file_is_skipped`, `fr_t16_a_file_with_an_unparseable_last_line_is_skipped_untouched`, `fr_t16_a_fragment_longer_than_the_window_is_skipped_untouched`, `fr_t16_a_file_that_is_one_short_fragment_is_truncated_to_zero_and_refilled`, `fr_t16_recovery_never_writes_agent_or_system_messages`, `fr_t16_recovery_does_not_fail_when_the_channels_dir_is_unreadable`, `fr_t16_over_the_cap_append_returns_false_and_the_row_stays`, `fr_t16_a_new_file_is_mode_0600_and_a_new_directory_0700`, `fr_t16_an_existing_directory_keeps_its_mode` (`ember/server/tests/fr_t16_jsonl.rs`), and `fr_t16_recovery_writes_a_guest_line_with_name` (unit test in `channels/jsonl.rs`). Not checked: the foreign-owner refusal and a failing fsync (no test). |

The session then decides as on its own route: 404 not pending, 400 for invalid answers (the fixed messages) or a body that is both or neither, 409 `conflict` when no agent is running; 200 `{status: "answered"|"declined"}`; the session records `QuestionAnswered` (a skip has no answers) and the feed item clears with it.
