| **FR-T12** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22]* In a channel composer, `@<agent>` mentions a member agent and the message is delivered to it, Slack style. A message with no agent mention is human-to-human conversation and is **not** delivered to any agent. *[provisional, 2026-11-06]* In a channel the mention syntax is `@<agent>`; the session composer keeps `@@` (`FR-T6`). An agent replies into a channel through a dedicated tool named `channel_post`. | A message with a mention enters the agent's session through the A2A path (`FR-T3`, as `FR-T6`), once per mentioned agent. A message without one reaches no agent session, and no model token is spent. The composer marks which agents a message will reach before it is sent. The agent's reply is posted in the channel. *[provisional, 2026-11-06]* The reply is posted through the `channel_post` tool (channel and text), and appears in the log attributed to the agent; an agent that is not a member of the channel cannot post to it. A test calls `channel_post` and finds the message in the channel log. **Status:** parsing, the stored `mentions` and the composer preview (`POST /api/channels/{id}/mentions/preview`) are built (`ember/server/tests/fr_t12_mentions.rs`); delivery to the agent is built (`ember/server/tests/fr_t12_delivery.rs`): after the message is stored, logged and pushed, each mentioned agent gets one A2A message from the person, headed `[ember a2a] Message <id> from 나 (channel #<name>, id <channel id>)` with the line `This message is from a person in a channel, not from another agent.`, stored in `a2a_messages` with `from_session = channel:<id>`. A message authored by an agent never triggers delivery (only the person's message is delivered), so no loop can start here; **Mention-chain depth** *[provisional]*: the depth is 1. Only a person's message parses `@name` and delivers; a message an agent posts parses no mention and delivers to no one, so an agent cannot mention another agent and no chain or loop can form through a channel. If agent mentions are ever allowed, `FR-T5` limits must apply to them and a depth limit be set then. **Reply (written, not yet compiled or run):** in v1 `channel_post` is the CLI subcommand `ember-a2a channel post <channel id> "text"` (`channel_post` is accepted as an alias; `-` reads stdin), and the delivered header carries the line `To reply in the channel: ember-a2a channel post <channel id> "..."`; `A2a::instructions` has a Channels section. It calls `POST /api/channels/{id}/post` with the session's runtime token. `auth.rs` exempts exactly that path for POST (four segments); every other `/api/channels/*` route keeps the server token, and the handler checks the token (401). Rules: the session must be a member (403); a person must have mentioned it in this channel within the last 30 minutes (403; the window is read from `a2a_messages.origin_channel`, there is no separate thread state); at most 6 posts per minute per session and channel (429, in memory, lost at restart). The session's own A2A switch is not consulted, so an agent with A2A off may answer a mention it received. The post is stored, appended to the JSONL and pushed like a person's message, authored by the session id. Tests: `ember/server/tests/fr_t12_channel_post.rs`, `fr_t12_cli_channel_post_builds_request` in `a2a/client.rs`. **MCP tool `channel_post` *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]*** (#380, PR a, written, not yet compiled or run): `ember-a2a mcp` is a stdio MCP server (newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0, hand-written, no new dependency) with one tool, `channel_post` with the arguments `channel` (the id from the mention header; letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.`, at most 128 characters) and `text` (not empty after trimming, at most 8192 bytes), no others. It calls the same route as the CLI with the session's runtime token read at call time from `EMBER_URL` and `EMBER_RUNTIME_TOKEN`, so `initialize` and `tools/list` work without them and a call then fails as a tool error saying the tool only works inside an Ember agent session. A refusal (not a member, no recent mention, rate limit) is a tool result with `isError: true`, whose text is one fixed English sentence chosen by the server's machine `code` (`unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `not_a_member`, `disabled`, `rate_limited`, `quota_exceeded`, `not_found`, `bad_request`, `internal`) and, for a missing or unknown code, by the HTTP status class (400, 401/403, 404, 409, 429, 5xx, other); the server's `error` text and any other field are never forwarded *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]*; a wrong argument is a tool error, an unknown tool is `-32602`, any other request method is `-32601` and the server keeps running, malformed JSON is `-32700` and a frame over 1 MiB is discarded with `-32600`. Stdout carries protocol frames only; stderr never holds the token, a message text or the URL. The server sends the runtime token over plain HTTP to the host `EMBER_URL` names and does not refuse a non-loopback host; at startup it prints one stderr line naming only that host when it is not loopback (127.0.0.0/8, `::1`, `localhost`; names are not resolved), and the CLI `ember-a2a` has the same property and is unchanged *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]*. Facts measured on 2026-11-06 without a model turn, in an isolated HOME: Claude Code 2.1.319 sends `server/discover` first, then `initialize` with protocol 2025-11-25, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list`; Codex 0.164.0 starts the server at `thread/start`, sends `initialize` with 2025-06-18, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list`, and later `resources/templates/list` and `resources/list`; both go on after `-32601`. The server answers the client's protocol version when it is one of 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26, 2024-11-05 and otherwise the newest. Codex filters the child's environment, so the registration (PR b) must list both variables in the entry. **Registration for Claude Code only (PR b, *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]*, written, not yet compiled or run):** a Claude Code session on this computer, with the `ember-a2a` binary installed, replies through the MCP tool `mcp__ember-channels__channel_post` (server name `ember-channels`, constants `CHANNEL_MCP_SERVER` and `CHANNEL_POST_TOOL` in `a2a/mod.rs`). Measured on 2026-11-06 on Claude Code 2.1.319 (design note `.scratch/pz/issue-380-channel-post-mcp-design.md`, section 11): in `-p` mode the call is DENIED without a rule and passes with exactly `--allowedTools mcp__ember-channels__channel_post`. A start-config hook adds the `ember-channels` entry (`ember-a2a mcp`, 10 s start timeout, `EMBER_URL` and `EMBER_RUNTIME_TOKEN` in its `env`) to the start request, using the values `runtime_env` already put in the request (the same session token as the CLI, no second token is issued), and adds the one allow rule to `StartRequest::allowed_tools`; the Claude adapter writes the entry into the private (0600) `--mcp-config` file, so the token is never on the command line, and passes `--allowedTools <tool>` before the next flag. The adapter refuses any allowed tool that contains `*` or whitespace or does not start with `mcp__`, so the allow rule is one exact tool, never a wildcard. A session that runs on another computer keeps the CLI (the MCP child would need the binary there; detected from the `session_computer` row), as does a missing `ember-a2a` binary, an existing server named `ember-channels`, and a request without the runtime variables. The mention prompt (`A2a::instructions_for`) and both reply lines (the person's mention and the guest's, whose untrusted-input header and markers are unchanged) name the tool for such a session, with one fallback sentence `ember-a2a channel post <channel-id> "<text>"`, and keep every rule (answer only when addressed, 30 minutes, 6 posts a minute); one function, `channel_reply_by_tool`, decides for the hook and the prompts. For every other recipient the text is byte-for-byte the old one. **Codex, Antigravity and ACP agents keep `ember-a2a channel post`; their MCP behaviour (tool name, approval, environment of the child) is NOT MEASURED** (Codex credits were exhausted and the `agy` limit was reached; only the no-model facts above exist for Codex). **NOT MEASURED for Claude Code as well:** the interaction of the allow rule with `bypassPermissions` and `acceptEdits`, interactive mode, and giving the rule through the settings file instead of the flag. Tests: `fr_t12_*` in `ember/server/src/a2a/mod.rs` (`channel_tool_tests`: constants, hook adds the entry and the rule, same token and no second issue, local computer row, other computer, no binary, Codex/Antigravity/ACP/Scripted untouched, remote request, missing or empty variables, name clash, token in the private file and not in argv, Claude prompt, old prompt byte-for-byte, person footer, guest footer) and in `ember/server/src/agents/claude_code.rs` (`fr_t12_no_allowed_tools_means_no_flag`, `fr_t12_allowed_tools_is_the_flag_then_the_one_tool_then_a_flag_or_the_end`, `fr_t12_allowed_tools_rejects_wildcards_and_non_mcp_rules`). The CLI stays as the fallback. Tests: `fr_t12_mcp_*` in `ember/server/src/a2a/mcp.rs` (protocol over in-memory pipes with a fake poster) and `fr_t12_mcp_usage_names_the_subcommand` in `a2a/client.rs`; `ember/server/tests/fr_t12_mcp_channel_post.rs` (also `fr_t12_mcp_the_token_is_in_no_output_of_the_real_process`: the token is in neither stdout nor stderr, and a non-loopback `EMBER_URL` gives one host-only warning line) drives the real binary against an in-process server (post appears in the feed, non-member and bad token refused, the 7th post a minute refused, no environment, stdin closed exits 0). Not built: an open mention thread beyond the 30-minute window; a per-person rate limit. In a channel the token is `@<agent>` (exact name, any case; the session composer keeps `@@`) *[provisional]*. **Name fallback, host parser and temp files *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]* (written, not yet compiled or run):** (1) Before registering `ember-channels`, the start hook asks `channel_server_name_taken` whether the user's own Claude Code configuration already defines an MCP server of that name (ASCII case ignored). It reads `.mcp.json` in the session directory and every ancestor; `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/.claude.json` (the variable from the start request, else from the server's environment) and `$HOME/.claude.json`, keys `mcpServers` and `projects.<directory>.mcpServers` for the directory and each ancestor; and the managed files `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-mcp.json` and `/etc/claude-code/managed-mcp.json`. If the name is taken, the hook registers neither the server nor the allow rule, keeps the CLI text for that session (the prompt builders ask `channel_reply_by_tool`, which is false for a session in the in-memory fallback set; the start request's instructions are corrected too, because the instructions hook runs before the config hook), logs one `warn` with the session id, the kind of file and a fixed reason (never file contents) and records one notice, `The channel tool could not be registered because a MCP server named ember-channels is already configured; replies use the ember-a2a command.` The check fails closed and tells the two cases apart (`NameCheck::Taken` and `NameCheck::Unsure`): a file that exists but cannot be read, is not valid JSON, is over 4 MiB or is not a regular file (a directory, a FIFO, a socket, a device) is `Unsure` with the fixed reason `unreadable`, `not valid JSON`, `larger than 4 MiB` or `not a regular file`, the registration is skipped and the notice is `The channel tool was not registered because the Claude Code configuration (<kind>) could not be checked (<why>); replies use the ember-a2a command.`; a missing file is fine. The file is opened non-blocking and judged on the open handle (a FIFO swapped in cannot stall the start), and a symlink to a regular file is followed. The check does not read plugin-provided servers, `enabledMcpjsonServers` or settings files, or claude.ai connector servers, and on Windows it finds the user config through `HOME` only (`USERPROFILE` is ignored), so a name taken through those is not detected and which entry would win is not measured *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]*. Claude Code's own precedence between a project `.mcp.json` and a `--mcp-config` entry of the same name is NOT MEASURED, which is why the registration steps aside instead of racing it. A later start without the collision registers again and clears the set; after a server restart the set is empty, and a surviving agent is told "by tool" again (its prompt carries the CLI fallback sentence) until its next start. A session's entry is also dropped when its team membership ends (the end hook; the sessions API has no delete path). (2) `ember-server` removes stale `ember-mcp-<32 lowercase hex>.json` files from the temporary directory once at start (`cleanup_stale_mcp_files`): only a regular file (not a symlink), owned by the current user, mode exactly 0600, modified before this process started and at least one hour ago (an agent that survived the last run may still re-read a recent file); errors are ignored; the decision uses the modification time alone, so after a server restart a surviving session, or a second server on the same TMPDIR, can lose a file more than an hour old and then fail to reconnect its MCP servers, which is accepted *[provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]*. (3) `non_loopback_host` ends the authority at the first `/`, `\`, `?` or `#` before splitting userinfo at the last `@` (as WHATWG URL parsing does), so `http://evil.com\@127.0.0.1` names `evil.com`. `Client::new` (used by the CLI and by `ember-a2a mcp`) refuses with the fixed text `EMBER_URL is not a plain http://host[:port] address` (the URL is never in the message) any URL with `@` in the authority, a `\`, `?` or `#` before the path, an empty host, or a port that is not ASCII digits in 1..=65535, and accepts `http://host[:port]` with an optional trailing `/`, so a URL it accepts has the host the warning names. Tests: `fr_t12_collision_*` in `ember/server/src/a2a/mod.rs` (new: `fr_t12_collision_each_unsure_reason_and_a_missing_file_is_free`, `fr_t12_collision_a_fifo_is_not_a_regular_file_and_does_not_block`, `fr_t12_collision_a_symlink_to_a_regular_file_is_still_read`, `fr_t12_collision_unsure_notice_text`, `fr_t12_collision_hook_unsure_skips_with_its_own_notice_and_taken_keeps_the_old_text`, `fr_t12_the_end_hook_drops_the_fallback_entry_and_the_set_does_not_grow`), `parses_base_urls`, `accepts_plain_addresses_with_one_optional_trailing_slash` and `refuses_userinfo_separators_bad_ports_and_empty_hosts_without_echoing_the_url` in `a2a/client.rs`, `fr_t12_mcp_non_loopback_host_cases` in `a2a/mcp.rs`, `fr_t12_cleanup_*` in `ember/server/src/agents/claude_code.rs`. |
| **FR-T13** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22]* A mention is limited to the channel's member agents, and the existing switches and rules still apply: A2A off per user or session (`FR-T6`), the team messaging rule (`FR-T7`). | A mention of a non-member agent is refused with a reason, and nothing is delivered. A mention of a session with A2A off is rejected with the `FR-T6` reason, shown in the channel. **Status:** built. The member gate (400 `not_a_member`, nothing stored) and, at delivery, the server-wide and per-session switches (`FR-T6`) and the team rule (`FR-T7`): a refusal is posted in the channel as a message from `system` (`@<name> not reached: <reason>`; stored and pushed, not written to the JSONL) and the person's message stays posted. *[provisional, 2026-11-06]* `FR-T6` "off per user" is treated as the server-wide A2A switch, because no per-person switch exists yet (per-person allowances are `FR-T15`/`FR-T17`, not built). A channel has no team, so an active teammate is reachable only by its own team and a mention of one is refused. Loop protection's limits are not applied to a person's message (as for `FR-T6` mentions), but the row counts toward the window for later agent sends. |
| **FR-T14** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22: "그 채널만 볼 수 있어. 디스코드나 슬랙 생각하는게 편할거야."]* A channel's owner can invite an external account (another person) into it by an invite, as in Discord or Slack, and remove them. An invited person sees only the channels they are invited to, and nothing of the project's sessions, files or computers. | An invited account sees that channel's log and no other part of the project, and removal ends its access at once. *[provisional, 2026-11-06]* A guest (invited person) sees the message log only and has no right to answer approvals; an approval answer from a guest is refused (the refusal is inferred from "no right to answer approvals", not stated by the supervisor).  *[user, 2026-11-06, relayed by the supervisor: "(a) 채널 한정 초대 링크"]* **Authentication:** the owner creates a single-use, channel-scoped invite link carrying a token; the server stores only a hash of the token. Accepting the link yields a per-person Bearer credential scoped to that channel, stored hashed. Revoking the invite or removing the person invalidates the credential at once. A guest credential is valid only on that channel's message log (read, and the guest's own posts, *[provisional]*) and nowhere else. **Tests to write first:** a guest credential gets 401 or 403 on every non-channel route (projects, sessions, files, computers, other channels); 403 on the feed and on approval answers; a revoked credential gets 401 on its next request; a used link cannot be accepted a second time; the stores hold the hash of the token and of the credential and never the values (a test reads the table). The server token middleware (`INTENT.md` D37) must treat a guest credential as a different credential class, valid only on the channel guest routes and never as the server token. *[provisional, 2026-11-06, supervisor decisions on the invite design]* (1) Guests reach the server only through a separate optional listener `EMBER_GUEST_LISTEN`, off by default, serving only the guest router. (2) The server token is refused on guest routes. (3) An invite expires after 24 h by default, 1 to 168 h. (4) A guest may read member and agent names. (5) Editing or deleting own messages is out of v1. (6) A cost cap for an allowance is a later issue. (7) One credential per invite (one person per invite); the credential's expiry follows the invite's. (8) A guest answering approvals has no route: 404, pinned by a test. **Status:** the store and the owner routes are written test first and **not yet compiled or run** (tests `fr_t14_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_invites.rs`): tables `channel_invites` and `channel_guests` (SHA-256 hex hashes only; the token `dpi_...` is returned once), `POST /api/channels/{id}/invites {ttl_hours?}` (201 `{id, token, link: /join#<token>, expires_at}`), `GET` (no token or hash), `DELETE .../invites/{invite_id}` (204; also revokes the guest credential made from it). **Guest access (PR 2 of 5, written test first, **not yet compiled or run**, tests `fr_t14_*` and `fr_t15_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_access.rs`):** `Store::accept_invite` (unexpired, unused, unrevoked; marks `used_at`; the guest row holds only the credential hash and `expires_at` = the invite's; name trimmed, 1 to 64 characters; a second accept fails) and `Store::guest_by_credential` (hash lookup, none when expired, revoked or its invite revoked). `channels::guest::guest_router` in `ember/server/src/channels/guest.rs`: `POST /api/guest/accept {token, name}` (201 `{credential, channel_id, guest_id, expires_at}`, no credential needed, every failure of the token is the same 404), then behind `guest_from_headers` (`Authorization: Bearer dpg_...`, 401 `{error, code}` otherwise, the server token is refused): `GET /api/guest/channels/{id}` (names only), `GET .../messages?after=`, `POST .../messages {text}` (author forced to `guest:<id>`, `from` ignored, text 1 to 8192 bytes, a mention goes through the member gate and is stored, never delivered). Another channel is 404; no route for the feed, approvals, edit or delete (404 or 405). `auth.rs` does not exempt `/api/guest/*`: the guest router is never served under `auth::protect`, its door is `EMBER_GUEST_LISTEN` (PR 5), and a guest router merged under `auth::protect` by mistake answers 401 on `/api/guest/accept` without the server token (tested, `fr_t14_the_guest_router_merged_under_auth_protect_fails_loudly`); a guest credential on any owner path is 401. **Hardening after the review of #400 (written test first, tests in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_hardening.rs`, **not yet compiled or run**):** *[provisional]* (a) the invite token and the credential carry 244 random bits (two UUIDv4 from the OS CSPRNG); (b) a guest posts at most 10 times per minute (429 `rate_limited`, in memory per guest id) and has at most 500 messages per channel (429 `quota_exceeded`, counted from the log by author `guest:<id>`); (c) the display name is refused (400 `invalid_name`, checked before the invite is burned) when it equals, trimmed and lowercased, `user`, `나`, `system`, `agent` or the name of a member agent of the channel, when it contains a character of Unicode category Cc, Cf (zero-width and bidi controls), Zl or Zp, or when it is only whitespace; no Unicode normaliser is a dependency, so the comparison is plain lowercase; (d) a guest sees the channel's full history from before it joined (the Discord and Slack model); (e) in what a guest reads, another guest's id never appears: the author is `guest`, with `name` the display name and `you: true` on the caller's own messages (the log itself still stores `guest:<id>`); (f) the accept attempt limit per remote address belongs to PR 5, where the listener knows the address, and is not built. **Guest door (PR 5a, written test first, **not yet compiled or run**, tests `fr_t14_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_door.rs`):** *[provisional, leader decisions 2026-11-06]* `ember/server/src/guest_door.rs`. **Off by default:** with `EMBER_GUEST_LISTEN` unset or empty nothing is bound and the guest router is not constructed in `main.rs`; nothing is exposed unless the owner sets it. When set (`ip:port`) it is a second listener that serves the guest router (`GUEST_ROUTES` in `channels/guest.rs`: `POST /api/guest/accept`, `GET /api/guest/channels/{id}`, `GET` and `POST /api/guest/channels/{id}/messages`) and `GET /join`, never merged with the owner app and never under `auth::protect`; every other path is a bare 404, `OPTIONS` is 405, a request with `Upgrade` is 400 (no WebSocket for guests; guests poll). Startup errors: a value that is not `ip:port`, the same address as `EMBER_LISTEN`, **a non-loopback address without `EMBER_GUEST_LISTEN_ALLOW_REMOTE=1`** (`0.0.0.0`, `::`, `::ffff:127.0.0.1` and LAN addresses; PR 5b made the variable effective again: with `1` the door binds and `main.rs` logs a startup warning that it is reachable from that network over plain HTTP), an invalid entry in `EMBER_GUEST_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, a bind failure, an `EMBER_GUEST_PUBLIC_URL` with a path, query, fragment, userinfo or a scheme other than http(s). Host: only the listen IP (not an unspecified one), `localhost` on a loopback bind, the names in `EMBER_GUEST_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, and the host of `EMBER_GUEST_PUBLIC_URL`; there is no "any IP literal" rule; otherwise 403. Origin: absent is allowed; present only when equal to `Host` (case-insensitive) or an allowed non-loopback host; `null`, another site, and a loopback Origin different from `Host` are 403. `/join` is an embedded Korean the guest page (`ember/server/src/guest_door/join.html` with `join.js` inlined, **built and compiled by CI; browser behaviour UNVERIFIED until the owner checks it in a real browser**, tests `fr_t14_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_join_page.rs`) with `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'sha256-<hash of the inline script>'; style-src 'sha256-<hash of the style block>'; connect-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` (both hashes computed once at startup from the embedded text, so the header cannot drift; no `unsafe-inline`, no `unsafe-eval`; one inline script, so no asset route exists). The page reads the `dpi_` token from `location.hash` and removes it from the address bar with `history.replaceState` before anything else; the token goes only in the JSON body of `POST /api/guest/accept` (`credentials: 'omit'`); the credential is kept in `sessionStorage` only (gone when the tab closes, memory if storage is unavailable; `localStorage` is deliberately not used) and a 401 clears it and shows that the invitation ended; messages are rendered with `textContent` only and polled every 3 s (back-off on 429 by `Retry-After`, stop on 401 and 404 (both clear the stored credential); the log keeps the newest 500 messages, error back-off carries 0..25% jitter, and damaged items in a poll are skipped; the script and page are normalised to LF before hashing and `ember/server/src/guest_door/.gitattributes` pins LF, so the hash matches what a browser sees; **known limits:** reloading the name form loses the token (the fragment was removed from the address bar, so the guest must reopen the link), and the `#token` may remain in the browser's history entry of the original navigation), `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: no-store`; every door response carries `nosniff` and `no-store`. A route-table test probes the real router against `GUEST_ROUTES` and a corpus of owner paths answers 404 or 405 with every method. The invite response gains `link_warning` and `guest_door: {state: on|off, listen, public_url}`: door off, the link stays relative (`/join#<token>`); door on with a public URL, `<public url>/join#<token>`; door on without one, an absolute link from the listen address with `link_warning: true`, which the owner UI must show as a warning to check that the address is reachable from outside (client not built). **Door limits (review of #417, written test first, not yet compiled or run):** every door request body is at most 16 KiB (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`, 413 by `Content-Length` before any handler, and the extractors stop at the limit), a request (handler included) that takes more than 10 s (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT`) is 408; `HEAD`, `TRACE` and `CONNECT` are 405 on every path (also `HEAD /join`, so nothing leaks); an unspecified address (`0.0.0.0`, `::`) on either side with the same port as the other listener is a startup error. The route-table test proves the claim in two halves: without a credential a method outside the table is 401, 404 or 405 and never 2xx or 3xx (authentication runs before the method match, so a caller learns nothing about methods); with a valid guest credential it is 404 or 405 and every table row is neither. Not covered (needs hyper's builder or tower-http, no new dependency was added): a header-read timeout on the connection itself. The real `/join` page (a script with a CSP hash or nonce, a `fetch` POST of the token from the fragment) is built as above (5a served a placeholder with no script). The owner route `GET /api/guest-door` (owner router, behind the server token, never on the guest door) returns `{state, listen, public_url, link_warning}` from the same helper as the invite response (`fr_t14_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_door_status.rs`, not yet compiled or run). **Door limits (PR 5b, written test first, **not yet compiled or run**, tests `fr_t14_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_limits.rs`):** *[leader decision, 2026-11-06, adjustable; provisional]* `GuestDoor::wrap_limited` puts these limits outside the Host and Origin checks, so every request is bounded before anything else runs. (1) Per remote address (IPv4 exact, IPv6 reduced to its /64, an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address counted as its IPv4): `POST /api/guest/accept` 5 a minute and 20 an hour, counted for every attempt whatever the outcome (failures too; a refused attempt is not recorded), every other guest request 120 a minute; over the limit is 429 `{code: rate_limited}` with `Retry-After`. Sliding windows of timestamps, aged out after 1 hour, a sweep on every 256th call, at most 10 000 tracked addresses (a new one evicts the oldest-touched). (2) After the per-address check (a request it refuses spends no global budget, so one flooding address cannot make every guest see 503), a global token bucket of 50 requests a second (burst 100) answers 503 `{code: busy}` when empty, and at most 64 requests in flight (a semaphore with `try_acquire`, 503 at once, never queued). At most 256 TCP connections are open at once (`MAX_CONNECTIONS`, a semaphore taken at accept and held for the connection's life; the next connection is closed at once and counted in a debug log). (3) The remote address is `ConnectInfo<SocketAddr>`; a limited door with none refuses (500), it never lets a request through unidentified. `X-Forwarded-For` is read only when the TCP peer is in `EMBER_GUEST_TRUSTED_PROXIES` (comma list of IPs; an invalid entry is a startup error), walking the chain from the right, skipping trusted hops, and stopping at the first entry that is not a trusted proxy: that is the client, and everything to its left is ignored (a forged or unparsable leftmost entry changes nothing); if that entry does not parse the key is the peer; an entry is trimmed and an IPv4 `:port`, `[v6]` and `[v6]:port` become the bare address; from any other peer the header is ignored entirely, so a forged one neither changes the key nor dodges the limit. (4) The door is served by `GuestDoor::serve_with`, a hyper HTTP/1 server (`hyper` and `hyper-util`, both already in the tree through axum) with a header-read timeout of 10 s (`HEADER_READ_TIMEOUT`): a client that does not send a whole header in time is disconnected. Graceful shutdown and `ConnectInfo` are kept. (5) The non-loopback refusal is gone; **remote use is possible only with `EMBER_GUEST_LISTEN_ALLOW_REMOTE=1`, and putting TLS in front of the door (a reverse proxy or tunnel) is the owner's duty**: the door speaks plain HTTP, and the credential and the invite token travel in clear without it. Limiter memory is bounded (10 000 addresses, at most 140 timestamps each); a flood from many spoofable addresses can evict a real offender's history, which the global bucket still covers. **Behind a reverse proxy WITHOUT `EMBER_GUEST_TRUSTED_PROXIES` every guest is keyed to the proxy's address and shares one budget** (5 accepts a minute for all); the owner must list the proxy. Known gaps: `/join` shares the 64 in-flight slots with the API and a slow-body accept request holds one for up to 10 s; eviction of old addresses can flush an offender's history after about 10 000 distinct keys; whether hyper answers 408 or only closes on a header timeout is not verified (the test checks only the disconnect). Not built: an access log, a cap on connections per address (a slow body is covered only by the 10 s request timeout).  Not verified (needs a person or a second machine): a guest over a real LAN, TLS or a tunnel in front, the browser behaviour of the CSP and the fragment link. Not built also: delivery of the page to a real guest client, any client UI. **Owner UI, door state before the first invite (built, written test first, **not yet compiled or run**, tests `fr_t14_door_state_is_known_before_the_first_invite`, `fr_t14_a_failed_door_read_stays_unknown`, `fr_t14_the_invite_response_refreshes_the_door_state` in `ember/app/src/ui/channel_owner.rs`):** *[provisional]* when the channel owner sheet opens it reads `GET /api/guest-door` once and keeps the answer as the known door state, so the sheet shows the real state (off, on, or on with a reach warning) before any invite exists; the freshest answer wins, and the create-invite response refreshes it. While the read is pending, or when it fails, the state stays "아직 알 수 없습니다" and a failed read adds the fixed sentence "게스트 문 상태를 읽지 못했습니다", never the server's text. Review windows keep their sample door and do not call the server. **Characterization tests:** `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_door_characterization.rs` (`fr_t14_char_*`, written test first, not yet compiled or run) pins the current behaviour of the door's hardening code (Host and Origin guard, body and method rules, headers, 404 fallback, `Retry-After`, env parsing and `parse_public_url`, graceful shutdown, and the public item paths) so that moving it into a shared module can be proven to change nothing. The hardening code that is not specific to the guest now lives in `ember/server/src/door/` (moved without change, #560 pins it). `tests/fr_t14_guest_door_guard_matrix.rs` pins the Host and Origin guard on the wrong-method path of the guest door to a CI measurement (#594, 84 requests per run): a foreign Host or Origin is a 403 for every method on every path, registered or not; with a good Host a wrong method on a registered path is axum's default 405 with `Allow`, HEAD is the guard's 405 on every path, an unregistered path is 404. The enrol door answers a wrong method with a good Host differently, 404 with `Allow` (it sets `method_not_allowed_fallback(not_found)` so a wrong method looks like an unknown path; the guest paths are public API of an invite link, so its 405 reveals nothing new). The guard result, a 403 for a foreign Host or Origin, is the same on both doors. [provisional, 2026-11-06; the 405 versus 404 difference is as built, not decided: owner confirmation pending] Step C0 moves the mixed items (`serve_capped`, `LimitConfig`, `LimitKind`, `AddrLimiter`, `DoorLimits`, `limit_layer`, `guard_layer`, `parse_public_url`) into `door/` the same way, and first pins the layer order (limits before the Host and Origin guard, the guard before the declared-size check, the timeout around the route) in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_door_order.rs` and the three "guest door" log messages of `serve_capped` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t14_guest_door_logs.rs` (both written test first, not yet compiled or run). Step C1 adds six seams so the enrol door can reuse that code (a label for the `serve_capped` logs, a strict path on `DoorLimits`, a body limit on `Guard`, a variable name for `parse_public_url`, an environment `Spec` for `DoorEnv::parse` with `listen_clash`, and `harden::stack` for the layer order); the guest door passes the old literals and every test under `ember/server/tests` stays unedited, and the per-seam mutation check is in the C1 pull request text (the repository has no `docs/design` door topic file), not yet compiled or run. |
| **FR-T15** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22: "그래 좋아."]* A person's message is attributed to the signed-in account that wrote it, in the log and in what an agent receives (`FR-T3` header). By default an invited person's mention reaches no agent unless the channel owner allows it. | Each log entry names its author. By default an invited person's mention reaches no agent (their words cannot spend the owner's tokens or steer the owner's sessions). The owner's allow-setting is stored per channel and per person.  *[user, 2026-11-06, relayed by the supervisor: "(a) 채널 한정 초대 링크"]* The author of a guest's message is the person bound to the guest credential, so attribution does not depend on a name typed in the request. **Status (PR 2, PR 3):** the guest post route forces author `guest:<guest_id>` and ignores `from`, written test first (`fr_t15_*` in `fr_t14_guest_access.rs`), not yet compiled or run; the guest mention reaches no agent because the guest route runs no delivery. PR 3 adds `author_name` and `author_kind` (`person`, `agent`, `guest`, `system`) to every message of the owner views (`GET /api/channels/{id}`, `.../messages`) and of the `channel_message` push, derived at read time (guest names in one query per page), tests `fr_t15_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t15_attribution.rs`, **not yet compiled or run**. **PR 4 (allowance and delivery, written, not yet compiled or run):** with a real A2A, a guest's mention without an active allowance makes no `a2a_messages` row and posts one `system` note per message (`guest @<name>'s mention to @<agent> was not delivered (no active allowance)`; stored and pushed, not in the JSONL); tests `fr_t15_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t17_allowance.rs`. The guest router needs `guest_router_with_a2a`; without A2A it delivers nothing and posts no note. **Test:** a guest post is logged under the credential's person, and a body that names another author is ignored or refused. A guest's mention reaches no agent unless the owner has allowed it (this row and `FR-T17`); a test posts as a guest with a mention and finds no `a2a_messages` row. |
| **FR-T17** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22: "사람별로 앞으로 몇시간 동안 자동 허용 이런 기능이 필요할거 같아."]* The owner can allow one person's mentions automatically for the next N hours. The allowance expires on its own, can be revoked at any time, and is visible in the channel (who, until when). | After expiry, the person's next mention reaches no agent again, without any action by the owner. Revoking stops delivery at once. The channel shows the active allowance and its end time to its members. Mentions delivered under an allowance are marked as such in the agent's `FR-T3` header. *[provisional, 2026-11-06]* An allowance is in whole hours, capped at 168 h; a value of 0, a fraction or more than 168 is refused (the refusal cases are inferred from "whole hours, capped at 168 h").  *[user, 2026-11-06, relayed by the supervisor: "(a) 채널 한정 초대 링크"]* The allowance belongs to the person bound to the guest credential and to the channel. **Tests:** an allowance of 1 to 168 whole hours is accepted, and 0, a fraction or more than 168 is refused; revoking the invite or the person ends the allowance and the credential at once (the next request is 401 and delivery stops); after expiry a guest's mention reaches no agent again. Authentication of the guest is by the invite link and per-person Bearer credential of `FR-T14`. *[provisional, 2026-11-06, design note from the review of #400]* A guest's text is untrusted input, a prompt-injection surface. When delivery under an allowance lands (PR 4), it is presented to the agent in the `FR-T3` header as such: a clearly delimited block (for example `[guest message, untrusted]`) with the guest's display name and the allowance's end time, never merged into instructions or into the owner's text. The PR 4 tests pin that format and that a guest's text cannot close the block early. **Status (PR 4, written, not yet compiled or run; tests `fr_t17_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t17_allowance.rs`):** owner routes `GET /api/channels/{id}/guests`, `PUT .../guests/{guest_id}/allow` (`{hours}`, whole 1..=168, else 400 `invalid_hours`; `allow_until = now + hours h`, cut to the credential's own `expires_at` with `capped: true`), `DELETE .../allow` and `DELETE .../guests/{guest_id}`; the channel (`GET /api/channels`, `/api/channels/{id}`) carries `guests: [{id, name, allow_until}]` (additive; `allow_until` null when none is in force). Delivery is decided when the mention is delivered, from the database: guest not revoked and `allow_until > now`, so expiry needs no action and a revocation counts at once. Each mentioned member agent gets the text once through `A2a::send_from_guest`, which keeps the server-wide switch, the target session switch and the team rule and skips loop limits; a refusal is posted as `@<agent> not reached: <reason>`. The header is pinned by a test: first line `[ember a2a] Message <id> from a GUEST (<name>), channel #<channel> (id <cid>), allowed until <RFC 3339 UTC>.`, then the untrusted notice, then the text between `<<<guest message, untrusted>>>` and `<<<end guest message>>>`, then `To reply in the channel: ember-a2a channel post <cid> "..."`. Either marker inside the guest's text (and name) is replaced by `[marker removed]`. **Hardening (PR 4 review, written test first, not yet compiled or run; tests `fr_t15_*`, `fr_t17_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_t17_allowance_hardening.rs`):** (1) before matching, Unicode format characters (Cf, from the hand-listed ranges of `is_format_char`) are removed from the text and name delivered, fullwidth ASCII (U+FF01..U+FF5E) is folded to ASCII, and whitespace of any kind (spaces, tab, CR, LF, NBSP, U+3000) is ignored in the match, ASCII case-insensitively; the matched region is replaced by `[marker removed]` and the text is scanned again until no marker is left (bounded at 1000 passes, then every `<` and `>` becomes `?`). Ordinary text (Korean, emoji) is unchanged. (2) A channel, agent or guest name in a header or note passes `sanitize_label`: Cc, Cf, U+2028 and U+2029 dropped, whitespace runs collapsed to one space, cut to 64 characters, and the channel name is neutralised like the text; the channel create route refuses a name with such a character (400 `invalid_name`). (3) One guest message is delivered to at most `MAX_MENTION_TARGETS` = 3 distinct mentioned agents (one note: `delivered to the first 3 of N mentions`), and one guest gets at most `DELIVERIES_PER_HOUR` = 20 deliveries an hour (in memory, lost on restart; one note per message for the rest); the post itself succeeds. (4) The allowance, revocation and expiry are re-read immediately before each send (sends are sequential), so a revoke or expiry between two sends stops the later ones with one note. (5) The path "allow, clear, then allow again, then deliver" is verified by `fr_t17_reallow_after_clear_delivers` (green in CI on #418, run 42133018136). The earlier failure (#416) was a test artefact, not a product defect: in the failing commit `ed86c1b` line 414 is `assert_eq!(w.user_messages(&w.alpha).len(), 1)` ("left 0, right 1"), not the row count on line 413, so the row was written and the send went out. `Sessions::send` puts the `UserMessage` into the agent's event channel (`live.events.send`), and the event pump stores it on its own task, so the event is not yet in the store when delivery returns; the old test read it at once, every other test waits with `wait_user_messages`. The code shows no state left by the no-allowance post (one note; `delivery_slot` is consulted only under an allowance), and clear sets `allow_until` to NULL while set writes `now + hours`. Not built: a spend limit per allowance beyond these caps (the guest's 10 posts a minute and 500 messages per channel still apply), the client control for the allowance, and a guest listener (PR 5). **Client UI:** the owner sheet is written (see the `FR-T12–T15, FR-T17` status row), not yet compiled or run; it needs the guest door (#417, 5b) to be usable end to end. |

### §T status: teams and mentions (FR-T6, FR-T7, issue #55)

- **Teams** *[provisional]* (store migration 11: `teams`, `team_members`, `team_tasks`,
  `team_mail`). The first `ember-a2a team spawn` makes the calling session the leader of a new
  team. A teammate is a new session in the leader's project and directory with the agent,
  account and computer the leader chose (defaults: the leader's agent, the account router, the
  main server) and nothing else: no model, no transcript, no approvals. Each teammate answers its
  own approvals in its own session. Its first prompt arrives as an A2A message from the leader
  (FR-T3), and its instructions name its role. At most 8 active teammates; spawns count against
  the per-session limit of the window. A session belongs to at most one team, as leader or
  teammate, for good; no nested teams. Ending a teammate (leader, or the user) stops its agent
  and removes it from the team; the session and transcript stay.
- **Permissions** *[provisional]*: only the leader spawns and ends. Any member adds tasks; the
  leader assigns and updates any task; a teammate updates only tasks that are unassigned or its
  own and assigns only to itself. An assignment to someone else, and a teammate's status change
  (to the leader), are announced through the A2A queue under loop protection, best effort.
- **Mailbox**: `mail send <name>|--all` stores the mail and delivers it through the A2A queue
  (wakes the recipient, survives a restart, FR-T4); loop protection counts one message per
  recipient (FR-T5), all or nothing. The leader reads all team mail; a teammate reads team-wide
  mail and its own.
- **Messaging rule** *[provisional]*: an active teammate is reachable only from its own team
  (leader and teammates) and reaches only its own team; it is left out of everyone else's
  `ember-a2a list` and mention candidates. Leaders and ended teammates are ordinary sessions.
- **Mentions** *[provisional]*: the composer writes `@@<id>`, `@@<title>` or `@@"<title>"`. The
  user's message goes to the session it was typed in, unchanged; each mentioned session gets a
  copy as an A2A message from that session, so it can answer the origin with one call. Chosen
  over redirecting the message because the user typed in this conversation and expects it to
  continue here, and an unresolved mention then loses nothing. Mentions follow the switches and
  the messaging rule but not the rate limits (a person sent them); they are stored, so they
  count toward the window for later agent sends. Each outcome is a notice in the origin session.
- **APIs**: agent routes under `/api/a2a/team` (members, tasks, mail; runtime token = the
  caller); user routes `GET /api/sessions/{id}/team`, `GET /api/teams/{id}`,
  `GET /api/teams/{id}/mail`, `POST /api/teams/{id}/members/{session}/end`,
  `GET /api/sessions/{id}/mentions?q=`. Every team change is pushed as `team_updated` with the
  whole team (additive). Details: `ember/server/src/a2a/api.rs`.
- **Not yet verified**: a team of real Claude Code and Codex sessions on different computers and
  accounts (needs real agents and hardware); the composer suggestion list on screen.

## §R: Remote browser and agent browser use (D6)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-R1** | A browser session can be opened whose network egress is a chosen computer. The egress path is a SOCKS5 exit in that computer's ember node, carried over a transport stream (`FR-N5`), which the main server's browser uses as its proxy. | A what-is-my-IP page shows the chosen computer's public IP; a page on that computer's LAN or `localhost` is reachable. |
| **FR-R2** | Browser profile data (cookies, storage, logins, history) is stored on the main server and reused whichever computer is the egress. | Log in to a site with egress A, switch egress to B, reload: still logged in. |
| **FR-R3** | Agents can drive the same browser through a browser-automation tool (DevTools-protocol based), and the user can see and take over the page the agent is driving. | An agent fills a form while the user watches; the user takes over to complete a login; the agent continues afterwards. An "agent is active" indicator is shown. |
| **FR-R4** | Browser data can be cleared per project. | Clearing removes cookies and storage for that profile only. |
| **FR-R5** | The remote browser is available from every client, including mobile and web, not only from a desktop app. | Verified from the phone client. |

> How the browser is rendered to the client without breaking E1 (pixel streaming into a native
> surface, or confining it to a separate window that is allowed a webview) is open; it must not put a
> webview in the launcher or conversation screens.

## §U: Accounts and usage (D7)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-U1** | The main server holds several accounts per agent (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity), each with isolated credentials and configuration. *[user, `INTENT.md` D18]* A team member is defined by a harness plus either one fixed account or an automatic account-switching pipeline (`FR-U3`). **Changed by D30 (2026-10-25):** the fixed account or pipeline belongs to the harness's profile (`FR-L17`), not to the member; the member chooses only the harness. | Two Claude Code accounts run sessions simultaneously without sharing credentials or settings. A member created with a fixed account always uses it; one created with the pipeline switches accounts by policy. Member definition built, awaiting CI (#129, `tests/members.rs`). |
| **FR-U2** | A new session is started under a chosen account. *[user, `INTENT.md` D30]* The account is chosen by the harness's profile (`FR-L17`), or the project's override. | The session's account is shown in the conversation view and cannot silently change. |
| **FR-U3** | Usage per account is recorded and visible; usage routing can choose the account for a new session by policy (e.g. least used, failover when one is rate-limited). | A rate-limited account is skipped by the router and the reason is shown. |
| **FR-U4** | Sign in with OpenAI ("Sign in with ChatGPT", OAuth 2.0 / OIDC with PKCE and a loopback redirect), with a page that lets ChatGPT plan usage be consumed in addition to Codex token usage. [user] | Works for a **self-hosted** ember server only: OpenAI permits open-source, locally hosted apps to call the Responses API on the user's ChatGPT Plus/Pro plan, with a per-app weekly cap, `store:false` and `stream:true` required, and no image generation, file search, code interpreter or hosted MCP. It is never offered through `darkpyonix.dev` (remote hosting needs OpenAI's approval) [user, 2026-10-03: "OpenAI 로그인은 엠버 서버에서 사용자가 자체적으로 하는걸로 하고 허브는 깃허브 로그인으로 하자"]. Source: developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source, per the darkpyonix leader's research, 2026-10-03; endpoints, dynamic client registration (`dynamic_agent_client`) and limits verified against the sub-pages the same day (see `docs/design/CHATGPT-SIGNIN.md`). Off when `EMBER_HOSTED=1`. |
| **FR-U5** | API-key providers (any OpenAI-compatible or vendor API) can be added with keys encrypted at rest. | Keys never appear in transcripts, logs or exports. |
| **FR-U6** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D18; changed by D30: the proxy is set on the profile (`FR-L17`), not the member]* A member can optionally run through a model proxy path (opencodex-like), so a harness uses a model from another source, e.g. Claude Code with `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` set to the proxy to run an API model or a Codex model. | A Claude Code member with the proxy path completes a turn on an API model and on a Codex model; without it, the harness uses its own provider. Keys stay out of transcripts (`FR-U5`). Partly built, awaiting CI (#129): the spawn env for Claude Code (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `fr_u6_*`); other harnesses are rejected with an error until their proxy setting is verified; the proxy server and the real API/Codex turns are not built or verified. *[user, 2026-10-25, "K1/K2 -- 우리 웹 소켓으로 하나?, K5 -- 실제 구현까지 보류 | 외 전부 추천대로"]* Codex and agy get the proxy env one at a time, each after an integration test verifies the CLI's real proxy or base-URL setting (issues filed). **Codex (#145)**: Codex ignores `OPENAI_BASE_URL` (verified on codex-cli 0.164.0 against a fake upstream); it honours `-c openai_base_url=...` and a custom `model_providers.<id>.base_url`, so the adapter sets a custom provider by `-c` (`agents::codex::proxy_config_args`) from spawn-env markers that `members::proxy_env` returns. Tests `fr_u6_codex_turn_goes_to_the_member_proxy_endpoint` and `fr_u6_codex_adapter_app_server_routes_turn_through_member_proxy` in `ember/server/tests/fr_u6_codex_proxy.rs` verify both `codex exec` and `CodexAdapter` (`codex app-server`) on codex-cli 0.164.0 against a local Responses API proxy, completing a full streamed turn (`AssistantDelta` + `TurnEnded::Completed`) with the member's `Bearer` key and zero credits used. Live upstream model turns require the owner's OpenAI/proxy API key (`[OWNER ACTION REQUIRED]`). Codex also honours `HTTPS_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY` (FR-X6), so a loopback model proxy stays direct. **agy (#146)**: agy 1.5.1/1.5.2 has a base-URL setting only for its Gemini API route: with `modelProvider: "gemini"` (per-session `--settings`, nothing saved to `~/.gemini`), `GEMINI_API_KEY` and `GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL`, it POSTs `<base>/v1beta/models/<m>:streamGenerateContent?alt=sse` with the key in `x-goog-api-key` (probed against a fake loopback upstream, no credits). Without `modelProvider` both variables are ignored and agy asks for a Google login, including during `agy -p /hooks` pre-flight when `HOME` has no Google login. In addition, `GOOGLE_API_KEY` takes precedence over `GEMINI_API_KEY` in agy. Therefore `members::proxy_env` returns `GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` and `GOOGLE_API_KEY` (both set to the member's proxy key so an ambient server `GOOGLE_API_KEY` never overrides it), and `agents::antigravity` passes `proxy_args` (`--settings {"modelProvider":"gemini"}`) on both `check_hook` (`agy -p /hooks`) and the turn spawn. The proxy must speak the Gemini API wire format and only Gemini models are offered on this route (no Claude or GPT-OSS). `HTTPS_PROXY` is honoured (CONNECT to `generativelanguage.googleapis.com:443`), loopback is always direct. Tests `fr_u6_agy_member_env_names_the_gemini_base_url_and_key`, `fr_u6_agy_turn_goes_to_the_member_proxy_endpoint` and `fr_u6_agy_adapter_routes_turn_through_member_proxy_with_hooks_and_version_pin` in `ember/server/tests/fr_u6_agy_proxy.rs` verify both direct `agy -p` and `AntigravityAdapter` (including `check_version`, `check_hook` and `GOOGLE_API_KEY` precedence override) on agy 1.5.2. Live upstream Gemini-compatible proxy turns require the owner's Gemini/proxy API key (`[OWNER ACTION REQUIRED]`). |
| **FR-U7** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D22: "토큰 사용량은 진짜로 token 사용량을 말하는거야"]* Actual consumed tokens are recorded: input, output and cache (read and write) tokens per turn, taken from the harness's own usage report (the `usage` event of `FR-A3`: Claude Code `result` usage, Codex app-server token usage, ACP and Antigravity usage where they report it). Each record carries session, project, agent (harness), account, model and time. Totals are aggregated per session, project, agent, account and period (day, week, month, all time). A harness that reports no token counts shows "unknown", never an estimate presented as a count. | The sum over a period equals the sum of the turns' reported counts, checked against recorded fixtures of each harness. A session moved between accounts or computers keeps each turn under the account that ran it. Totals are pushed to clients (`PR-1`). This is separate from `FR-U3` (which accounts are used and routed) and `FR-U4` (remaining subscription allowance). |

## §N: Networking (D8)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-N1** | Main server ↔ computer and client ↔ main server connections are HTTPS carried over a peer-to-peer tunnel. **Transport: iroh 1.0, decided, conditionally** [user, 2026-10-03: "P2P를 iroh로 가는건 일단 허용하는데 그게 품질이 별로면 아예 직접 구현하는거도 고민해봐"]. In-process Rust (one app on mobile), QUIC hole punching, self-hostable relay, MIT/Apache-2.0. If it misses `NFR-N1`, our own implementation is evaluated. Lives behind `FR-N5`. | Works across two different NATs with no port forwarding configured by the user. |
| **FR-N2** | **The main server is the relay** [user, 2026-10-30, `INTENT.md` D36: "Ember에 relay가 필요하면 main server가 그 역할을 하면 됩니다. ... 각 기기는 main server와 홀펀칭으로 연결합니다. 기기끼리 직결이 안 될 때는 main server가 중계합니다."]. Each device hole-punches to the main server; when two devices cannot connect directly, the main server relays between them, end to end encrypted so it sees only ciphertext (`FR-N3`). The main server must be reachable by every device (public IP, or port forwarding, UPnP or PCP) and answers public-address discovery (STUN or QAD role). No separate relay VPS (`relay.darkpyonix.dev`) is run. **On the letify-link backend** the main server runs the relay role (`EMBER_LETIFY_RELAY_LISTEN`), admits through it only the devices its gate admits (`PeerGate::attach`), and answers address observation, a signed UDP exchange modelled on QUIC address discovery, on `EMBER_LETIFY_QAD_LISTEN` (it also connects back once to the requester's TCP port, and a device publishes the TCP address it guesses from the observed IP only after that dial-back succeeded); every other device registers the main server as its relay with `EMBER_RELAY_URL=letify://<main server peer id>@host:port` (the relay is authenticated by its node id) and asks the observer for its public address with `EMBER_LETIFY_OBSERVER`. Two devices agree on a pairing token through the **rendezvous**, carried by the hub's pkarr directory: each device's signed packet holds its address record (the `transports` field lists `letify-link/1`) and a short mailbox per recipient that the recipient reads adaptively [provisional, #783]: every 1.5 s (`poll_interval`, `EMBER_LETIFY_POLL_MS`) while a dial or signal exchange with that peer is in flight (an outgoing `connect()`, a sent or a received signal) and for 30 s after it (`fast_window`, `EMBER_LETIFY_FAST_WINDOW_MS`), and otherwise at an interval that doubles up to 25 s (`idle_poll_interval`, `EMBER_LETIFY_IDLE_POLL_MS`). An outgoing dial therefore reads the answer within 1.5 s, but a relayed dial **from** a peer that is idle on the receiving side waits until the receiver next reads that mailbox, up to 25 s, unless the hub offers the batched watch (step 2 below). **Request budget** (hub on the Cloudflare Workers free tier, 100,000 requests a day per account, core INTENT D23; target under 50%): one idle pair costs 86,400 / 25 = 3,456 reads a day, and each device republishes at least every 120 s, 720 writes a day. A typical account (one main server, three computers, one phone) where the main server watches the four devices and each device watches the main server has 8 pairs: 8 x 3,456 + 5 x 720 = 27,648 + 3,600 = 31,248 a day (31%). A dial adds about 20 fast reads per side (30 s / 1.5 s). The fixed 1.5 s poll cost 57,600 reads per pair (460,800 for the same account). Devices watch only the peers they dial, are hinted or admit, so the account stays near the star shape; a full mesh of the five (20 pairs, 72,720 a day) would exceed the target. **Step 2, the batched watch** [provisional, #783; hub side core #132, PR #137]: when the hub's `/config` has `"pkarr_watch": {"max_keys": 32, "max_wait": 25}`, a registered device holds one `GET /pkarr/watch?keys=..&since=..&wait=25` (device token in `Authorization: Bearer`, never the resolve token) for all its watched peers, one request per `max_keys` peers. `since` is the timestamp of the last packet read from each peer (`0` for none). A `200` packet is verified with the same code as `GET /pkarr/{key}` and its mailbox delivered at once; then the watch restarts, and it also restarts when the watched set changes (the old request is dropped; the hub runs a new watch beside the old one). Keys in a `404` are left out until the watched set changes; a `204` restarts the watch at once. Watch requests are at least 1 s apart. Any other answer (`400`, `401`, `429`, a network error) switches to the adaptive poll above for 30 s, doubling per failure up to 10 min, then the watch is tried again. Without `pkarr_watch` in `/config` the adaptive poll stays. One watcher costs about 86,400 / 25 = 3,456 requests a day whatever the number of peers: 5 x 3,456 + 3,600 = 20,880 a day (21%), and an inbound dial is delivered as soon as the packet changes. Tested against a fake store and `FakeHub` (`ember/transport/src/letify/pkarr.rs` `fr_n2_*`, `ember/hub/tests/pkarr_watch.rs`); the hub watch is not deployed yet, so the measurement against the real hub is not yet verified. Then both dial the relay. The device that receives the offer (a letify-link `RelayOffer`) dials only when the relay it names is its own configured main server and, where a gate is attached, the sender is an admitted device. Devices register under the user's **GitHub account** on the `darkpyonix.dev` hub [user, 2026-10-03: "허브는 깃허브 로그인으로 하자"], which keeps the address directory (pkarr rendezvous), so connections need no user configuration. **Defaults** [provisional, #229]: a main server on the letify backend turns the relay (TCP) and the address observer (UDP) on by itself, both on port 7443 (`EMBER_SERVER_RELAY_PORT`; `EMBER_SERVER_RELAY=off` turns the roles off; an explicit `EMBER_LETIFY_RELAY_LISTEN` or `EMBER_LETIFY_QAD_LISTEN` wins; tests use port 0). A node or client finds the relay URL in the main server's device record on the hub first, and `EMBER_RELAY_URL` overrides it. The hub derives no relay from its own host (`relay.darkpyonix.dev` is gone). The iroh backend and its hub relay are legacy, kept for one release (removal tracked in a follow-up issue). Implemented for TCP and relay; UDP hole punching and the real-network acceptance run remain open (#229, #10). | A new computer joins by signing in; no address or port is entered by hand. Two devices behind NATs that block a direct path reach each other through the main server with no relay service other than the main server running, and the path is reported as relayed. A device that cannot reach the main server reports that reason. |
| **FR-N3** | All connections are encrypted and authenticated per device; a device can be revoked. On the letify-link backend, `PeerGate::revoke` and `PeerGate::set_allowed` reach `PeerPolicy::revoke` (new handshakes are refused, live sessions close with the reason `revoked`) and `Relay::revoke` (waiting requests are dropped and relayed pairs close at once) through `PeerGate::attach`. | Revoking a device closes its connections within one heartbeat. On letify-link the device sees close code 401 within a second, cannot start a new session until it is allowed again, and a relayed path of the device ends at once. |
| **FR-N4** | Clients reach the IDE window over a secure context, so VS Code Web's service-worker-backed webviews work on phones and tablets. | Extension webviews render on a real phone (not only headless Chromium; see `ember/proxy/docs/CONSTRAINTS.md`). |
| **FR-N5** | All Ember code reaches the network through one transport interface (connect to a peer by its key, accept, open bidirectional streams, report path state: direct, relayed by the main server, or relayed by another node). **No iroh type and no letify-link type appears outside the transport crate.** The **letify-link backend** is the default and the **iroh backend** stays for one release [user, 2026-10-30, `INTENT.md` D36; the release of overlap is provisional]. All endpoints swap together: the handshake's version negotiation (a lowest and highest version in the header) lets a mixed fleet coexist during the rollout, and a device refuses a peer with no common version with a message naming both ranges. Node keys are ed25519, reused from the iroh NodeId so registrations and allow-lists survive. **Backend selection:** `EMBER_TRANSPORT_BACKEND=iroh|letify` (default `letify` since #229 [provisional]; the `iroh` feature stays for one release and its removal is tracked in a follow-up issue), the cargo feature `letify` of `ember-transport`; every endpoint of a fleet must use the same value until the rollout is complete, and a letify endpoint refuses a peer whose address record does not list `letify-link/1` in `transports`, naming both lists (the record's `transports` field and the session's version range are the two negotiation levels). **Mapping:** one letify-link session per peer pair carries every Ember connection; a connection is its control stream (service name, accept or refuse, close code and reason) plus data streams, so `listen`, `connect`, `close(code, reason)`, `closed()` and `path_state()` behave as on the iroh backend. `Connection::open_bi_bulk` opens a stream of the bulk class (served after interactive streams), for file transfers. **Identity:** a `PeerId` is the letify-link node id; letify-link also requires the prime order subgroup, so a `PeerId` with a torsion component is refused at dial with the reason, and a revoked key stays revoked under its sign-flipped alias (the two encodings share one X25519 key). **Lifecycle:** `Transport::set_power_state` maps to letify-link's `PowerState` and `Transport::resume_now` to `Session::resume_now` and `PathManager::invalidate_paths`. Rekeying and resuming a session on a new path are letify-link's own (Noise IK, key epochs). The letify-link backend and default selection are implemented (#229). | Replacing the transport touches only that crate: a CI check fails if `iroh` or `letify_link` is imported anywhere else, and the ember server and ember node test suites run unchanged against an in-memory fake transport. The same suite passes against both backends (`ember/transport/tests/letify_local.rs` mirrors `fake.rs` and `iroh_local.rs`; `letify_gate.rs`, `letify_relay.rs`); a server on the new backend serves a node on the old one during the rollout only if version negotiation finds a common version, and otherwise reports the two ranges (`fr_n5_a_peer_that_does_not_speak_letify_link_is_refused_naming_both_lists`). |
| **FR-N6** | Canonical device identities. Every device identity (an Ed25519 public key: transport peer id, hub `endpoint_id`, stored `peer_json`) is parsed as an Ed25519 point and accepted only if it is canonical (re-compressing the decoded point gives the input bytes, so `y >= p` aliases and `x = 0` points with the sign bit set are refused) and not weak (small order, including the identity point). It is stored, displayed and compared only in canonical compressed form, as 64 lowercase hex characters; hex input is accepted in either case. The allow list, the revocation list and every server or hub comparison of device ids compare these canonical bytes, so a re-encoded alias of an allowed key is not admitted and an alias of a revoked key cannot connect. **Modulo the sign bit:** the points A and -A convert to the same X25519 key, and the holder of the secret of A can also present -A, so wherever the transport derives its key exchange from the Ed25519 key (letify-link) the gate (`PeerGate`) and the transport's own revocation compare device ids with the sign bit of the last byte cleared, while the stored and displayed id keeps its own encoding; a revoked device cannot return under its alias. Stored values written before this rule are re-validated on load: valid ones are unchanged, invalid ones are refused and logged with the reason. Done (#235). Tests: `ember/transport/tests/key.rs` `fr_n6_*`, `ember/hub/tests/directory.rs` `fr_n6_hub_endpoint_id_json_with_a_weak_key_is_rejected`, `ember/server/src/computers/mod.rs` `fr_n6_stored_peer_json_is_revalidated_on_load`. | `PeerId::from_bytes`, `FromStr` and `Deserialize` refuse non-canonical, `x = 0` sign-bit and small-order keys; hex is case-insensitive and stored lowercase; `PeerGate::allow_list` does not admit an alias of an allowed key and `revoke` cannot be dodged by one; a hub `Device` or `peer_json` with a non-canonical key fails to load, with a log line for stored rows (a stored row with an invalid key loads with no peer address, stays listed and deletable). |
| **FR-N7** | *[user, `INTENT.md` D37, 2026-11-06, #338]* A remote device's credential is its device key: a request that arrives through the transport is authenticated by the gate's peer identity (`PeerGate`, `FR-N3`), not by the local token. A device removed from the allow-list or revoked is dropped within one heartbeat and its API calls through the transport fail from then on. | A device that is not on the allow-list cannot call any route through the transport; revoking an admitted device closes its connection within one heartbeat and later calls fail (`fr_n7_letify_localhost_route_call_and_revocation` in `ember/server/tests/fr_n7_remote_device.rs`). Tests named `fr_n7_*`. *[user, `INTENT.md` D37, 2026-11-06: "지금은 전체 권한, 범위는 나중에"]* Admission is full trust in v1 (the trust model of an SSH key; every admitted device is assumed to be the owner's): an admitted device can run an agent shell as the server user, read the server token file and use the local door, so FR-N7 protects against browsers, unadmitted peers and local processes without the token, not against an admitted device. A per-device scope is a later step (#366). |
| **FR-N8** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` D37 "Per-device scope", #366]* Every admitted device has a scope: `full` (default), `read_only` or `chat_only`. The transport door checks it per route family before the handler runs and answers a refused call with 403 `{"code": "scope_denied"}`. Only the owner sets it, with `PATCH /api/devices/{peer_id}` `{scope}` on the local listener. | Tests `fr_n7_scope_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_n7_remote_device.rs` (named under FR-N7 so the existing CI filter runs them): `read_only` is refused on state-changing routes and allowed on GETs; `chat_only` cannot create sessions, open terminals, call fs or reach the token routes, and can post to a session and a channel; `full` is unchanged; a device cannot raise its own scope. |
| **FR-N8a** | *[provisional, leader 2026-11-13, #818]* `GET /api/whoami` answers `{door, peer_id, scope}`: `door` is `local` on the local listener (behind the server token, scope `full`, no `peer_id`) and `transport` on the transport door (the caller's peer id and its stored device scope). Every scope may call it, so a client can show what it is allowed to do. It is not an owner route. | Tests `fr_n7_whoami_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_n7_remote_device.rs`: the local answer; transport answers for `full`, `read_only` and `chat_only` devices. Client `Api::whoami`, serde test `whoami_decodes_both_doors`. |
| **NFR-N1** | Transport quality bar. **If iroh misses any line after tuning, our own implementation is evaluated** (`FR-N1`); the lines apply unchanged to the letify-link backend (`INTENT.md` D36), measured with `ember/transport/tests/letify_perf.rs` on loopback and with `ember-transport-bench` on the network matrix. Bulk transfers use `Connection::open_bi_bulk`, so small messages on other streams of the same connection keep their latency. Initial targets, set before measurement; the first M5 measurement may adjust a target once, with the measured data and reason recorded here. | Measured on the real network matrix: home router ↔ school/office network, home ↔ LTE hotspot, and symmetric NAT on one side; 20 runs per pair. <br>• **Relayed counts as non-direct:** a path through the main server (`FR-N2`) is relayed, not direct, in every ratio below; the report shows the direct ratio and the main-server-relayed ratio separately. <br>• **Direct-path success:** ≥ 85% across the matrix excluding symmetric-NAT pairs; symmetric-NAT pairs must still connect via the relay 100% when both devices reach the main server. <br>• **UDP-blocked networks:** on a network that passes TCP but no UDP (a school or office pair), a connection is established over TCP, directly or through the main server, 100% of runs, with first byte ≤ 3 s p95; setup time that needs UDP to be probed first is counted. <br>• **Direct-path overhead:** RTT ≤ raw path + 5 ms (p50) and + 15 ms (p95); throughput ≥ 80% of a raw TCP transfer over the same path. <br>• **Connection setup:** first byte ≤ 1.5 s p95 (relay allowed); direct path established ≤ 5 s p95 when one exists. <br>• **Relay → direct upgrade:** ≤ 10 s p95 after a direct path becomes possible; direct → relay fallback with no stream reset. <br>• **Network change** (Wi-Fi ↔ LTE): open streams survive; stall ≤ 3 s p95. <br>• **Mobile:** an idle background connection adds ≤ 2%/hour battery drain (Android and iOS); reconnect on foreground ≤ 1 s p95. |
| **PR-1** | Main server → client push channel for session status, transcript updates, computer reachability and assignments. | Messages only gain fields; a receiver ignores unknown fields and skips unknown types (D15). |


### §N status: transport wiring (M5, issue #10, #229)

Where the acceptance criteria stand after wiring `ember-transport` into the real connections on the
default `letify-link` backend (#229) and the in-memory fake transport. The tests named below run in
CI against loopback `letify-link`, `MemNetwork` and `FakeHub`; nothing here has run on real WANs
(#10, #229) or against the real hub (#62).

| ID | What is wired | Evidence / what remains |
| -- | ------------- | ----------------------- |
| **FR-N1** | ember node serves its API on transport service `ember-node` (`EMBER_NODE_TRANSPORT=1`, or `only`); ember server dials nodes registered by peer (`POST /api/computers {name, peer, token}`) and serves its own API on `ember-server` (`EMBER_TRANSPORT=1`); the client crate reaches the server with `Api::over_transport`. One transport stream = one HTTP/1.1 connection, so every HTTP route and WebSocket (exec, events, exec-server, terminal attach, push) is unchanged. | `ember/transport/tests/letify_local.rs`, `ember/server/tests/letify_e2e_node.rs` (letify-link loopback); `ember/node/tests/transport.rs`, `ember/server/tests/transport.rs`, `ember/client/tests/transport_flow.rs` (fake transport). The two-NAT acceptance run is still to do on real networks (`NFR-N1` matrix). |
| **FR-N2** | On the default `letify-link` backend, the main server runs the relay role and QAD address observer on port 7443 by default (`ember/server/src/transport.rs`, `INTENT.md` D36, `FR-N2` above). Relay URL from the main server's hub device record or `EMBER_RELAY_URL`; peers addressed by `PeerAddr` (id + hints) or bare id. **Hub** (`ember-hub` crate, `docs/design/HUB-INTEGRATION.md`, against `hub.openapi.yaml`, unversioned paths, vendored at `ember/hub/tests/fixtures/`): ember server (`POST /api/hub/link`, token sealed with `secret.key`) and ember node (`ember-node hub register`, `<state dir>/hub.json` 0600) join the user's GitHub account through a device link (user code + verification URL, polled claim signed with the endpoint key). A registered endpoint publishes its signed address record (with `letify-link/1` in `transports`) and relay offers to the hub's `/pkarr` and resolves peers there with its token (`ember_transport::HubDirectory`). No separate relay host (`relay.darkpyonix.dev`) is used since #229 [user, 2026-10-30, D36]. Computers are added by picking a device from the account (`POST /api/hub/devices/{id}/computer`) or by one-time enrol code (`FR-C1`, `FR-C2`); the devices allow-list can sync from the account (opt-in); removal on the hub is detected (`401` on `/me`) and surfaced. | Tests run in CI: `ember/transport/tests/letify_relay.rs`, `ember/server/tests/{letify_e2e_relay,letify_server_relay}.rs` (letify-link relay + QAD); `ember/hub/tests/{link_flow,revocation,directory}.rs`, `ember/server/tests/hub.rs`, `ember/node/tests/hub.rs` against `ember_hub::fake::FakeHub`. Hub-side gaps (revocation reason, client role, self-removal…) are listed in `HUB-INTEGRATION.md` §Spec gaps. The default hub is `api.darkpyonix.dev` (#62) and the hub paths carry no version (#101). |
| **FR-N3** | Per-device allow-lists enforced at accept (`ember_transport::PeerGate`) and attached to letify-link's `PeerPolicy` and `Relay` (`PeerGate::attach`): the server admits peers in its `devices` table (store migration 6; `/api/devices`, served on TCP only), the node admits server peer ids from `EMBER_NODE_ALLOWED_PEERS` / `<state dir>/allowed-peers`. Revoking (`DELETE /api/devices/{peer}`; node: edit the file + SIGHUP) closes the peer's open sessions and relayed paths within 1 s on letify-link (within one heartbeat). Bearer tokens stay as a second factor on nodes. | `ember/transport/tests/{gate,letify_gate,letify_relay}.rs`, `ember/server/tests/{fr_n3_revocation_bound,fr_n3_revocation_streams}.rs`; revocation cases in the server, node and client suites. |
| **FR-N5** | All of the above uses `ember-transport`'s API only, with `letify` as the default backend (`ember/transport/tests/default_backend.rs`). | `.github/scripts/checks/check-transport-isolation.sh` passes; transport, server, node and client tests run against both `letify-link` and `MemNetwork`. |
| **FR-N6** | Canonical Ed25519 device identities (and sign-bit-cleared canonical comparison on letify-link so `-A` cannot bypass revocation of `A`). | `ember/transport/tests/{key,letify_gate}.rs`, `ember/hub/tests/directory.rs`, `ember/server/src/computers/mod.rs`. |
| **FR-N7** | Remote devices authenticate over the transport by their admitted `PeerId` (`PeerGate`) without the local bearer token, while the local TCP listener requires `auth::protect`. | `ember/server/tests/fr_n7_remote_device.rs` (`fr_n7_*`, including `fr_n7_letify_localhost_route_call_and_revocation`). |
