## §F: File transfer between the user's devices (D24)

Status: **Draft**, not built (issues #152, #487). First unit: `ember/transfer` state machine over the fake transport (issue #487). Rows tagged [provisional] are team proposals; the
feature itself and its AirDrop-like intent are [user, 2026-10-25] (`INTENT.md` D24). The 2026-11-06 supervisor decisions on who may offer, size, names, storage and the execute bit are [provisional, 2026-11-06 supervisor].

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-F1** | The Files menu lists the user's other connected devices, computers and mobile clients registered under the same GitHub account (`FR-N2`), with name, kind and reachability (direct, relayed, offline). [user: transfer to all connected devices, mobile included] | With three devices online, each shows the other two within one heartbeat; a device that goes offline is marked offline in the same time. |
| **FR-F2** | A file is sent by picking a device and a file, by dragging one or more files (or a folder) onto a device in the list, or by the OS share action. A folder is sent as a set of files that keeps its relative paths. [provisional] | Dragging two files onto a device creates one transfer offer naming both; a folder arrives with the same tree. |
| **FR-F3** | The receiving device shows an offer (sender, file names, total size) and the user accepts or declines. Nothing is written before acceptance. A decline, or no answer within a time limit, ends the offer and tells the sender. [provisional] | Declining leaves no file or partial file on the receiver and shows "declined" on the sender; an unanswered offer expires after the limit and both sides say so. |
| **FR-F4** | Both sides show progress (bytes, rate, time left) and can cancel. A cancel removes the partial file on the receiver. [provisional] | A cancel from either side stops the stream within 2 s and leaves no partial file on the receiver. |
| **FR-F5** | Files of any size stream in chunks without holding the whole file in memory. A transfer interrupted by a dropped connection, a path change (direct to relay) or an app restart resumes from the last verified chunk, and the finished file is checked against a hash. [provisional] | A 4 GiB file moves with the receiver's memory staying under 256 MiB; killing the connection at 50% and reconnecting resumes near 50%, not 0%; the final hash matches; a corrupted chunk is detected and re-sent. |
| **FR-F6** | File bytes are stored only on the two ends of a transfer. Between devices they travel over the `FR-N5` transport interface, on the existing authenticated, encrypted connection (`FR-N3`). A send to a computer other than the server goes through the server: the server forwards each chunk to that computer's `ember-node` over its encrypted node connection, holds it in memory only while it is forwarded, and never writes it on its own disk (`FR-F14`). The server process therefore sees the content of a relayed transfer; a relay of the transport in between still sees only ciphertext. The hub is not used for storage. *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, #773)* | A relayed transfer shows only ciphertext at the transport relay; a transfer relayed to a computer leaves no file and no partial file under the server's data folder (`fr_f14_*`); a CI check finds no iroh or letify-link code outside the transport crate (`check-transport-isolation.sh`); the suite runs against the in-memory fake transport. |
| **FR-F7** | The mobile clients take part through the OS: the iOS and Android share sheet offers "Ember" as a target and then a device picker; received files can be opened or saved to the system's Files app. [provisional] | On a real phone, sharing a photo from the Photos app to Ember sends it to a chosen computer. Not verifiable without hardware; reported as not yet verified until run. |
| **FR-F8** | Received files go to a per-device folder, changeable in settings. The target default is `Ember Received/` under the user's downloads. The first version uses a received-files folder under the device's data folder instead, because a user-chosen location needs the OS file pickers and permissions (follow-up). Both are [provisional, 2026-11-06 supervisor]. A name clash never overwrites: the new file gets a numbered suffix. The Files menu keeps a transfer history (direction, device, name, size, time, result) that the user can clear. [provisional] | Receiving `a.txt` twice gives `a.txt` and `a (1).txt` in the received-files folder; the history lists both with result "done"; clearing it removes the rows, not the files. |
| **FR-F9** | Permissions. A device may send to and receive from the user's devices only while it is registered and not revoked (`FR-N3`); revoking a device closes its transfers at once. Each device sets a receive mode: ask every time (default), auto-accept from chosen devices, or off. Auto-accept never skips the size and name checks. File names are never trusted (`#527`): only the basename is accepted, control characters (including C1 and zero-width format characters), NTFS alternate data streams (`:stream`), trailing dots, path separators (`/` and `\`) and a leading dot are removed or replaced, Windows reserved device names (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`..`COM9`, `LPT1`..`LPT9`) are prefixed with `_`, and an empty result gets a generated name. A folder offer keeps relative paths, but every component goes through the same cleaning, path depth and length are capped, duplicate cleaned paths within one offer are disambiguated, and `..` never escapes the received folder. Files are written with mode 0644: the execute bit is removed, and setuid, setgid and sticky bits are never set. On mobile, the OS storage and photos permissions are requested at first use. [provisional; name, mode and sender rules: provisional, 2026-11-06 supervisor] | A revoked device's open transfer ends within one heartbeat; with receive mode "off" every offer is refused; a file named `../x` is stored inside the received folder under a safe name; `.bashrc` arrives without the leading dot; `a/b\c` becomes one name with no separators; a name of only control characters gets a generated name; a folder entry `d/../../x` stays inside the folder; a received file that was executable on the sender has no execute bit; an offer from an account or device not registered under the same GitHub account is refused. |
| **FR-F10** | Size limit and quota. The default limit is 4 GiB per transfer and is configurable per device. The limit applies to the sum of an offer (all files of a folder together). An offer above the limit is refused at the offer, before any byte moves, and the sender is told why. Offers are only accepted from the user's own devices registered under the same GitHub account (`FR-N2`, `FR-F1`). [provisional, 2026-11-06 supervisor] | An offer of two files of 3 GiB each is refused at the offer with the limit named, and no chunk is requested; an offer of exactly the limit is accepted; changing the device limit changes the next decision; a test with the fake transport shows zero stream bytes after a refusal. |
| **FR-F11** | Transfer ids and partial files. An offer id is `tr-` plus 128 random bits in hex, so ids never repeat across server restarts. A partial receive folder records the offer id and each declared file's size and hash; an existing partial folder is adopted only when that record matches the offer exactly, otherwise it is removed and the transfer starts at 0. A freshly accepted offer never adopts a partial folder, and the server removes every partial folder under the received-files folder at start, because no transfer state survives a restart (#773). [provisional] | Two server starts over one data folder give different offer ids; a part file left by an offer before a restart is gone after the restart, and a new offer accepted after it reports 0 bytes done; a part folder with a different declared size or hash, or no record, is not adopted. |
| **FR-F12** | The received-files folder setting (`PUT /api/transfers/settings`, `received_dir`) accepts only an absolute path to a directory that exists or can be created, that is not a symlink, and that is not the server's data folder or anything inside it except `<data folder>/received`, nor a parent of the data folder. The checks run on the canonical path, so `..` or a symlinked parent cannot reach a refused place. A refused path answers 400 and leaves the setting unchanged (#773). [provisional] | A relative path, the data folder, a file inside it (the server token location), a parent of it, an existing file, and a symlink to the data folder are each refused with 400 and the old setting stays; `<data folder>/received` and an absolute folder outside the data folder are accepted. |
| **FR-F13** | Node receiver. `ember-node` receives files on `GET /transfers`, `POST /transfers/offers`, `POST /transfers/offers/{id}/accept` and `/decline`, `POST /transfers/{id}/chunks`, `GET /transfers/{id}/resume-offset/{file}`, `POST /transfers/{id}/complete`, `/pause`, `/resume`, `/cancel`, and `GET` and `DELETE /transfers/history`, behind its bearer token, with the same bodies and answers as the server's `/api/transfers*`. Files go to `<state_dir>/received` by default; `EMBER_NODE_RECEIVED_DIR` changes it, with the `FR-F12` check against the state folder (refused at start). Writing uses the shared `ember-transfer` store: each chunk's SHA-256 is checked before it is written, each file's SHA-256 at the end, files are `0644` and never overwrite. Offer ids are random (`FR-F11`). Pending and active transfers are kept in `<state_dir>/transfers/ledger.json`, so after a node restart a transfer continues from its verified offset; partial folders the ledger does not name are removed at start. Without a state folder the receiver answers 409. *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, #773)* | Node tests `fr_f13_*`: offer, chunks and complete store a `0644` file with the right hash; a chunk with a wrong hash is refused and nothing is written; a node restarted over the same state folder resumes at the verified offset and completes; a `received_dir` inside the state folder is refused at start; a stale partial folder is removed at start. |
| **FR-F14** | Server relay to a computer. The server serves `/api/computers/{id}/transfers`, `/offers`, `/{tid}/chunks`, `/{tid}/resume-offset/{file}`, `/{tid}/complete`, `/pause`, `/resume`, `/cancel` and `/history`, and forwards each call to that computer's node (`FR-F13`) with the node's status and body unchanged. Nothing is written on the server's disk (`FR-F6`). Only a computer reached over the transport is a target: one registered by plain HTTP answers 403 `InsecureTarget`, before anything reaches it. The server's own computer answers 400 (it is `/api/transfers`), an unknown computer 404, an unreachable node 502. Accepting or declining for a computer is not relayed (403, later work). The body limit is 4 MiB, enough for a 256 KiB chunk as base64. *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, #773)* | Server tests `fr_f14_*` through the in-memory fake transport against a real node: the `ember-client` upload to a computer stores the file on the node with a matching hash and nothing under the server's data folder; a plain HTTP computer is refused with 403 and the node sees no offer; an unknown computer is 404. |
| **FR-F15** | Who answers an offer to a computer. The server marks an offer `owner: true` when the owner account starts it (every caller of the owner API, `INTENT.md` D37) to one of the owner's enrolled computers, which are the only computers it relays to. The node accepts such an offer on its own; any other offer waits in its pending list until it is accepted or declined on the node. `EMBER_NODE_RECEIVE` sets the node's mode: `owner` (default, as above), `ask` (every offer waits) or `off` (every offer is refused with `ReceiveOff`). *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, #773)* | Node tests `fr_f15_*`: an owner offer is accepted at once; an offer without the owner mark is pending, nothing is written, and it streams after an accept; mode `off` refuses. |
| **FR-F16** | Free space. Before a node accepts an offer (at the offer, and again at an accept), it checks the free space of the received-files folder's file system; when it is below the bytes still to arrive plus a 64 MiB margin, it refuses with 507 `NoSpace` before any byte moves. *[provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13, #773)* | Node test `fr_f16_*`: with free space reported below the offer, the offer is refused with 507 and no partial folder exists. |

## §C: Adding a computer (D39)

Status: **Draft**, partly built (issue #491, follow-up #526). `FR-C1` (the admin side and the redeem core) is merged and CI-tested (#523). `FR-C2` step 1 (the core and the owner routes) is merged and CI-tested (#543); the follow-up (orphan cleanup, limiter key, no public `complete`, #548), the door skeleton, the two door routes (C3a, #591) and the node (`ember-node enrol`) are merged and CI-tested; `FR-C2l` (#539) is written, not compiled. `FR-C1` is [provisional, session pZ proposal, needs supervisor confirmation]. The `FR-C2` rows are [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] for confirm at join (D4, D5), the node UX (D9), the own listener and limits (D1, D2, D6), the server-minted credential (D3), the later items (D7, D8, D12) and the node's credential file (D13); the fixed-port guess (D11) is [provisional, pT/pX]. The decisions and what each rejected are in `INTENT.md` D39 "Confirm at join". The design is `docs/design/COMPUTERS.md` and the memo `.scratch/pz/enrol-door-design.md` (cited below as "memo"). Today a computer is added with `POST /api/computers {name, url|peer, token}`, which needs a long-lived node token pasted by hand (`project.rs` cites `FR-X7` for this TODO; that is the metrics requirement, the add-computer rows are `FR-C1` and `FR-C2`).

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-C1** | The server mints a one-time enrol code for a new computer. Minting is allowed only from the local admin router (token-protected, never the guest door). A code is single use, expires after at most 10 minutes, carries at least 244 bits of randomness and is stored only as a SHA-256 hash. Redeem attempts are rate-limited per source address. A used, expired or unknown code gets the same generic refusal, so the answer tells nothing about which case it was. The answer to the minting call carries the code once, plus the exact command to run on the other machine: `ember-node enrol <code> --server <door url>`, with ` --server-fingerprint <server peer id>` appended when the transport is on (the FULL 64 lowercase hex id, never the short form, which is for human screens only). `<door url>` is the enrol door's address (`EMBER_ENROL_PUBLIC_URL`, else `http://<EMBER_ENROL_LISTEN>` when that names one interface), not the owner API address, which serves no `/enrol/redeem`. The 201 answer is `{id, code, expires_ms, command, warning, door: "on", server, server_peer_id?, server_fingerprint?}` with `Cache-Control: no-store`; `server_peer_id` and `server_fingerprint` (the short form from `ember_transport::fingerprint_hex`) are omitted, never null, when the transport is off; the `warning` says the code is shown once, names the door URL and, for an `http` URL, that the door speaks plain HTTP and the code and credential can be read on the network path. When the door is off, minting answers 409 `{"error":"door_off","message":...}` and creates nothing; when the door is on but the server has no usable URL (an unspecified listen address and no `EMBER_ENROL_PUBLIC_URL`) it answers 409 `{"error":"door_url_unknown","message":...}` and creates nothing, so the owner is never handed a code that cannot be redeemed. The code cannot be read again. A redeem burns the code before the owner confirms; a failed or denied enrolment needs a new mint. [provisional, session pZ proposal, needs supervisor confirmation] | Step C3c is written, not compiled: tests `fr_c2_command_has_the_full_server_peer_id_as_the_pin`, `fr_c2_command_is_the_old_string_without_a_server_peer_id`, `fr_c2_mint_answer_names_the_door_and_the_server_id`, `fr_c2_mint_answer_omits_the_id_keys_without_a_transport`, `fr_c2_mint_with_the_door_off_is_409_and_creates_no_row`, `fr_c2_mint_with_an_unknown_door_url_is_409_and_creates_no_row` in `enrol.rs` and `fr_c2_door_url_*` in `enrol_door.rs`. Integration tests on the server (`fr_c1_*`, merged in #523, run by CI): minting through the guest door is refused; a used code is refused; a code older than 10 minutes is refused (fake clock); the code is not stored in clear text (the stored value is the SHA-256 hash, and the code string is absent from the database file); a decoded code has at least 244 bits; the refusal body of a used, expired and unknown code is identical; the Nth redeem from one address within the window is rate-limited while another address is not; a second read of the minting answer is not possible. |
| **FR-C2** | The other machine runs `ember-node enrol <code> --server <url>`. It redeems the code at the enrol door (`FR-C2e`), waits while the owner confirms it in the app (`FR-C2a`, `FR-C2c`), receives its own credential once (`FR-C2d`), and the computer appears online in the registry. The project screen polls the admin status route. A revoked computer needs a new code. The existing hub device-link route `GET /api/hub/devices` needs no new server code. The 컴퓨터 추가 sheet of the Project screen (#490) is built as a read-only list of the account's hub devices (`Api::hub_devices`): name, kind, a state pill (이 서버, 컴퓨터로 등록됨, 허용됨, 등록 안 됨), the online state as the hub reports it (온라인, 오프라인, or 상태 알 수 없음 when absent), a note that a new computer joins with a one-time code and that this screen is later, and the states loading, hub off (503), empty and a fixed sentence per failure status. Names and addresses from the hub are cleaned before they are shown (control and bidi characters dropped, name capped at 64 characters, address at 80). Adding a computer by a pasted node token is not offered (D39). The one-time code UI is written, not compiled: 새 컴퓨터 추가 (name, 코드 만들기), the code with the server's `command` shown as given (never built in the app), a 2 s status poll, the confirm card (reported name, observed address, warnings that inform, 600 ms delay) and the outcome; `door_off` and `door_url_unknown` (409 at mint) show a fixed explanation and no code; `ember/app/src/ui/enrol_flow.rs`, tests `fr_c2_enrol_*`, client tests `enrol_tests` in `ember/client/src/api.rs`. Tests `fr_c2_*` in `ember/app/src/ui/add_computer.rs` and `hub_device_tests` (`ember/client/src/api.rs`); this part is written, not yet run by CI. The code flow is for machines that are not signed in to the account. A machine that already has a device key uses the device-link route; there is no enrol over the `FR-N5` transport. For a node that reported a peer id and no url (peer-only), the confirm card shows the node's fingerprint large and a field where the owner types the fingerprint from the node's own screen (never prefilled); 확인하고 추가 is enabled only when it matches (whitespace and case ignored, 16 hex), the typed text is sent as `expect_fingerprint`, and a 409 `fingerprint_mismatch` shows a fixed line; when a node reports both url and peer id and the server registers by url, the card says the peer id is not used (tests `fr_c2_enrol_peer_only_*`, `fr_c2_enrol_fingerprint_matches`, `fr_c2_enrol_registered_by_*`, written, not compiled). The node command (`ember-node enrol`, `FR-C2h`) is merged (#614); the sheet shows the server's `command` with the code argument displayed as `-` (the code on standard input) and the original command in a second muted line (`stdin_form`), orders its async answers in the pure reducer `reduce` (a late mint is cancelled, stale answers change nothing, every end state clears the code, confirms in flight at close are remembered in a bounded set of up to 8 detached confirms so every completion posts its notice, closing an ambiguous confirm posts a notice, and a confirm in flight is not cancelled on close), and tells the owner when a cancel on close failed (tests `fr_c2_enrol_reducer_*`, `fr_c2_enrol_stdin_form_*`, written, not compiled). [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | `fr_c2_enrolled_node_authenticates_and_is_listed_online` (`ember/server/tests/fr_c2_enrol_node.rs`: a transport-only in-process `Node` enrolled through the real `EnrolDoor` and `ComputersRegistrar` with the delivered token and `allowed-peers`, `GET /api/computers` lists it online over the transport); `fr_c2_a_revoked_computer_needs_a_new_code` (`ember/server/tests/fr_c2_enrol_node.rs`: the old credential is refused, a fresh code re-enrols). The hub device-link route stays covered by its `FakeHub` tests. |
| **FR-C2a** | Enrolment states. An enrolment is `waiting` from the mint until the code is redeemed, then `awaiting_confirmation`. The owner's confirm moves it to an internal `confirmed` state, which the admin still sees as `awaiting_confirmation`; the delivery of the credential to the node moves it to `joined`. The owner's deny moves it to `denied`. It becomes `expired` when a `waiting` code passes its 10 minutes, or when an `awaiting_confirmation` enrolment passes 10 minutes after the redeem (the confirm deadline, equal to the code lifetime). A `confirmed` enrolment also becomes `expired` when its credential is no longer in server memory (a restart) or is not taken within 10 minutes of the confirm. No other transition exists; `joined`, `denied` and `expired` are final, and an illegal move is a typed error in the core. Step 1 is merged and CI-tested (#543). When a `confirmed` enrolment expires (not taken in time, or the credential lost to a restart) the computer the confirm registered is removed from the registry, once and idempotently; a failed removal is logged at warn without any secret and does not stop the state change; a `joined` enrolment is never removed; the owner can then mint a code with the same name. The follow-up is written, not compiled. The admin status values are exactly `waiting`, `awaiting_confirmation`, `joined`, `denied`, `expired`. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | `fr_c2_status_values_waiting_awaiting_joined_denied_expired` (memo §7, fake clock, every state and transition); `fr_c2_confirm_after_the_deadline_is_refused`; `fr_c2_deny_registers_nothing_and_the_node_sees_denied`; `fr_c2_two_concurrent_redeems_one_wins`; `fr_c2_the_transition_table_is_explicit_and_illegal_moves_are_typed_errors`; `fr_c2_concurrent_confirm_and_deny_exactly_one_wins`; `fr_c2_confirmed_then_expired_removes_the_computer`; `fr_c2_a_restart_expires_the_confirmed_row_and_removes_its_computer`; `fr_c2_a_joined_enrolment_is_never_removed`. |
| **FR-C2b** | The redeem answer carries only `{enrol_id, poll_secret, expires_ms}` and never a credential. `poll_secret` is 256 random bits, stored only as a SHA-256 hash, returned once, and can do one thing: ask this enrolment's status and, after the owner confirms, receive the credential once. The request carries `{code, node_public_info}`; `node_public_info` is untrusted display data (`hostname`, `os`, `arch`, `node_version`, optional `url`, `peer_addr`, `peer_id`), length-capped, control characters refused, JSON only, 4 KiB in total. The server records what it observes (source address after the trusted-proxy rule, and the time). A used, expired, unknown and malformed code gets one identical refusal (status, headers and body). The core refuses a `node_public_info` that breaks its caps with a separate error that depends only on that information, before the code is looked at and without burning the code or spending a failure budget. Core part written, not compiled; the door part is step 3. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | `fr_c2_redeem_valid_code_answers_enrol_id_and_poll_secret_and_not_a_token`; `fr_c2_used_expired_unknown_malformed_codes_get_an_identical_response`; `fr_c2_node_info_is_length_capped_and_control_chars_refused`; `fr_c2_only_hashes_are_stored` (the database files do not contain the poll secret). |
| **FR-C2c** | Confirm at join, the owner side. Two routes on the token-protected local router only, never on the door: `POST /api/computers/enrol-codes/{id}/confirm {name?, expect_fingerprint?}` and `POST /api/computers/enrol-codes/{id}/deny`. `GET /api/computers/enrol-codes/{id}` also answers `node_info`, `observed_addr`, a short peer-id fingerprint (the first 64 bits of the id: enough for a person to compare two screens, but an attacker with enough compute can grind a key whose id starts the same, so the comparison defends against a mistake and a casual impostor, not against a determined one; the full id in the pin of the node command is the strong check), `name` and `confirm_deadline_ms`. The app shows the machine's reported host name and the observed address next to the owner-chosen name, as plain text (never HTML), and warns when they differ in an obvious way (an observed address that is not private while the door is LAN-only, or a host name mismatch; a mismatch informs and never blocks). The owner may rename at confirm. Confirm checks, in one transaction, that the state is `awaiting_confirmation` and inside the deadline, and that the name is not in the registry (checked again here, not only at mint); a clash answers 409 with a message, the enrolment stays `awaiting_confirmation`, and the same call accepts a replacement name. Confirm registers the computer (`register` or `register_peer`), records the computer on the enrolment inside `Enrol::confirm` (there is no public `complete`), and rolls the registration back if recording fails. The name check and `register` are separate steps and another writer to the registry can take the name in between; `Registrar::register` answering name-taken is the authoritative answer and gives 409 `name_taken` with the state unchanged. When the node reported no address, the server proposes the observed source address with port 8741 and the app shows it to the owner for editing; the guess is never trusted automatically [provisional, pT/pX]. Registration address rule (step C4, issue #625, written, not compiled): (1) a node `url` registers the computer by URL, and the URL wins when a peer id is sent too; (2) no `url`, a peer id, and the transport on in this server: the computer is registered by peer (`register_peer`); (3) no `url`, a peer id, and no transport in this server: the observed URL, as before, so a LAN node enrolled with `--transport` against a server without the transport still works; (4) neither: the observed URL, or the error that no address could be derived. The peer id is the node's own claim, so the owner compares its fingerprint (the status answers `fingerprint` and `node_info.peer_id` while the enrolment awaits confirmation) before confirming; a peer id that is this server's own is refused at register, and the registry is touched only at confirm, never at redeem. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] Step C5 (issue #633, written, not compiled): the confirm body really carries `expect_fingerprint`. It is compared, after dropping spaces and case, with the fingerprint the node reported; it must be 16 hex digits (4 groups of 4), else 400. A different value, or any value for a node that reported no peer id, answers 409 `fingerprint_mismatch`; nothing is registered and the enrolment stays `awaiting_confirmation`. On the peer path (the computer would be registered by its peer id) `expect_fingerprint` is mandatory: without it the answer is 409 `fingerprint_required`, state unchanged. On the url and observed paths it is optional. The status answer carries `registered_by` (`url`, `peer` or `observed`, the way a confirm would register the node; `url` even when the node also sent a peer id, which is then not used) while the enrolment awaits confirmation and a registrar is set. A second computer with an already registered peer id is refused by the registry (`POST /api/computers` with `peer` answers 400 "a computer with this peer id is already registered", and so does a confirm; the address list is not compared). [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]  The server probes the address once (`GET /health`) and reports reachability without blocking the confirm. Deny registers nothing. | **FR-C2d** | The credential. The node token is 256 random bits minted by the server at confirm time and never before; the node never chooses it. It is delivered once, to the node's poll (`GET /enrol/status/{enrol_id}` with `Authorization: Bearer <poll_secret>`), together with the confirmed name and, when known, the server's peer id (`credential.server_peer_id`). **Sealed delivery (#540, written, not compiled).** The token never crosses the network in clear. The node sends a one-time X25519 public key in `node_public_info.enrol_key` (64 lowercase hex; the private half stays in the memory of that `ember-node enrol` run). The door seals the token to it: a one-time X25519 key of its own, HKDF-SHA256 over the shared secret (bound to the enrol id and both public keys), ChaCha20-Poly1305 with the enrol id as associated data. The `joined` answer carries `credential.sealed_token` (`{v, epk, ct}`) and no `node_token` key. A reader of the traffic who holds the poll secret cannot read the token; it can fetch the sealed credential first, which opens with the node's private key and no other, and the node's run then fails. A redeem without a usable `enrol_key` (missing, malformed, or a weak point) is refused as bad node information (400, before the code is looked at, nothing burnt), and the node refuses a `joined` answer without `sealed_token`: there is no cleartext path, because no deployment needs one (rejected: answering a keyless node in clear). An active attacker on a plain-http path can still swap the node's key unless the command carries the pin (`FR-C2j`): the server's proof covers the node's key, so with the pin a swapped key is detected before any credential is asked for. [provisional] (leader task, 2026-11-13; owner confirmation pending) The `joined` answer carries the server's public transport peer id (64 lowercase hex, derived from the server's key at start, never taken from a request) when the transport is on, and omits the key when it is off (never `null`, never empty); no other poll answer carries it (the redeem answer carries the server's identity and its proof, `FR-C2j`), and the node accepts it only when it equals the identity proven at the redeem, and one function, `ember_transport::fingerprint_hex`, makes the short form the app and the node both show. The node cannot verify the server, so the id is trust on first use and a node must not trust it unchecked. Requirements for the node step (also in `INTENT.md` D39): R1 pin first: the command the mint prints carries `--server-fingerprint <full 64 hex peer id>` (the short form is for human screens only; step C3c, written, not compiled, [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]), and the node compares the id the server PROVED at the redeem with it (`FR-C2j`, #541) BEFORE polling for any credential and so before writing anything; a mismatch aborts, writes nothing and prints both fingerprints. R2 no pin means no automatic trust: the node prints the delivered id's fingerprint and asks whether it matches the server's console or app; non-interactive use needs `--server-fingerprint` or an explicit `--trust-unverified-server` (its help text and a warning say that it widens the node's reach to an unverified server), and by default the id is NOT added to `allowed-peers` (the token-only HTTP path still works as before). R3 verify, then write: `enrol.json` (0600, tmp and rename) and `allowed-peers` together or neither; the id is shape-checked (64 lowercase hex) first and only that one id is added. R4 the node prints what it will allow: commands from this server's fingerprint over the transport and from the token holder over HTTP. The `ember-node enrol` command that enforces R1 to R4 is written, not compiled (`FR-C2h`, `ember/node/src/enrol_cli.rs`); it is the only consumer of the id. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending]. First contact (trust on first use). The node cannot verify the server on first contact: nothing it holds yet links the delivered peer id to the machine at the URL. What pins it is the full id in the command the app prints (`--server-fingerprint <64 hex>`, R1). Without a pin there is no automatic trust: an interactive run asks y/N (the answer must be a deliberate y; n, Enter or Ctrl-D declines), and a non-interactive run or `--yes` trusts only with `--trust-unverified-server`; otherwise the id is not added and only the token-only HTTP path is set up. Residual risks that remain: a rogue server at the URL can still hand the node its own token and so take over the node's HTTP path (it receives the code and the node info as well), and `https` plus the pin are the mitigations. Known limits: the plain-http warning also prints for loopback and private-range doors, where it is mostly noise; `--trust-unverified-server` still asks when the run is interactive. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] Before confirm the poll answers only the status. After the first delivery the pending token is wiped (the live copy is in the registry, in clear as for every computer) and a later poll answers `joined` with no credential. The pending token is held in server memory only, never in SQLite (the database holds the SHA-256 of the poll secret and nothing of the token). A server restart between the confirm and the delivery loses it: the enrolment then reads `expired`, the computer stays registered with a token nobody holds, and the owner removes it and mints a new code. A lost delivery means the same; there is no retry path. Step 1 written, not compiled. A wrong poll secret and an unknown enrol id answer alike (404), compared in constant time. Polls are limited to 1 per 2 s per enrolment (429 with `Retry-After`). | **FR-C2e** | The enrol door. A listener of its own, `EMBER_ENROL_LISTEN` (`ip:port`), default OFF: unset or empty binds nothing. A bad value, the owner's address, the guest door's address, or a non-loopback address without `EMBER_ENROL_LISTEN_ALLOW_REMOTE=1` is a startup error; with it the server logs a warning. `X-Forwarded-For` is read only from the peers in `EMBER_ENROL_TRUSTED_PROXIES` (its own variable, not the guest one). The door serves exactly `POST /enrol/redeem`, `GET /enrol/status/{enrol_id}` and (`FR-C2m`) `DELETE /enrol/status/{enrol_id}` and answers 404 for everything else, including every owner route (after the layers above, which can answer 429, 503, 408, 403, 400, 405 and 413 first, and a wrong method on one of those two paths is, with a good Host, a 404 with an empty body (HEAD is a 405, the guard's bare answer) and, with a foreign Host or Origin, a 403; every path OUTSIDE those two is the same 404 whatever the method, with exactly the two door headers, so they cannot be told apart; the one exception, accepted by the supervisor on 2026-11-06: a wrong METHOD on one of the two registered paths also carries an `Allow` header, because axum's `RouteFuture` adds `Allow` to whatever response a request with no route for its method gets, after the fallback (itself wrapped by the guard) has answered. The two paths are public (this row, and the node calls them), so `Allow` leaks nothing new); the enrol routes are 404 on the owner listener, and `/enrol/*` and `/api/computers/enrol-codes*` are not `token_exempt`. The door is its own task, so its failure never stops the owner server. `EMBER_ENROL_PUBLIC_URL` is the URL printed in the command (the door port; `EMBER_PUBLIC_URL` is no longer used for enrol); when the door is off the mint answers 409 `door_off` and prints no command, and an unspecified listen address without `EMBER_ENROL_PUBLIC_URL` gives 409 `door_url_unknown` (`FR-C1`). Request shape: body 4 KiB (413), request timeout 10 s (408), JSON content type only, a Host and Origin guard (an absent Origin is accepted, a node CLI sends none), HEAD, TRACE and CONNECT 405 (MEASURED in CI on #591: HEAD on the GET poll route is stopped by the guard, a bare 405 with only the two door headers and no `Allow`; HEAD on the POST redeem route also gets the guard's bare 405 (the guard wraps the method-not-allowed fallback too), and `Allow: POST` is added afterwards by axum; neither runs a handler, proved by `enrol_door::tests::no_wrong_method_runs_a_handler`. The Host and Origin checks DO cover the method-not-allowed path: MEASURED on 42 requests (`fr_c2_guard_matrix_is_measured`, `GUARD_MATRIX` lines), a foreign Host, or a good Host with a foreign Origin, is a 403 for every wrong method on both registered paths and for the two real routes),  Upgrade 400, `no-store`, `nosniff`. | **FR-C2f** | Door limits (a configurable struct, like the guest `LimitConfig`). A global failed-redeem cap of 60 failures per 10 minutes across all sources: when it is empty every source is rate-limited until it refills, decided before the code is looked at, so rotating source keys (the 4096-key eviction of the per-source window) cannot reset it; a success does not spend it. The poll route verifies the poll secret first (`Enrol::verify_poll`, no side effect) and applies the one-per-2-seconds poll limit only to polls whose secret verified, so a caller without a valid secret takes no limiter slot and a rate-limited poll never consumes the credential; the limit is never applied before verification, and wrong-secret probes are bounded by the per-address limit and the global bucket. Per-address limits on `client_ip` (IPv6 keyed on the /64). Door-wide bucket 5 requests per second with burst 10, at most 8 in flight, at most 32 connections; excess is 503 with `Retry-After`. The per-address check comes FIRST and the shared budget second (the same order as the guest door, `limit_layer`): a request that its own address refuses (429) never spends the bucket, so one flooding address cannot drain it and turn every other source into a 503; a request that passes its address check and then finds the bucket empty or no in-flight permit left is 503. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | `fr_c2_per_address_redeem_limit_and_another_address_unaffected`; `fr_c2_global_failure_cap_blocks_every_source_even_after_key_rotation` (more than 4096 sources); `fr_c2_an_empty_global_bucket_or_no_in_flight_permit_is_503_after_the_address_check_passes` (it also asserts that a request refused by its own address does not spend the bucket); `fr_c2_connection_cap` (32 open; a 33rd connection is closed at once at accept, before any task is spawned for it, and a slot frees when one closes). |
| **FR-C2g** | Nothing is logged that contains the code, the poll secret or the node token. Log lines name the `enrol_id` and the source address only; a failed redeem logs at `warn` at most once per address per minute; the door's access log never records bodies or the `Authorization` header. An enrol id taken from a request is logged only through `log_id` (at most 64 characters, anything but ASCII letters, digits, `-` and `_` replaced by `_`), so no control character or escape sequence can reach a log. The new request and response types have a redacted `Debug`. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | `fr_c2_the_secrets_are_in_no_log` (tracing capture over a full redeem, poll and confirm: the code, the poll secret and the node token are absent); `fr_c2_only_hashes_are_stored`. |
| **FR-C2h** | `ember-node enrol <code> --server <url>` (flags `--url`, `--transport`, `--state-dir`, `--yes`, `--allow-http`, `--server-fingerprint`, `--trust-unverified-server`, `--replace`; the name is the owner's choice at mint, not a flag). With `--transport` it sends its peer id, and without `--url` the server registers it by that peer id when its own transport is on (`FR-C2c`, step C4). It validates the server URL (http or https, no userinfo, no path, no control characters), prints the server URL, whether TLS is used, and "This node will accept commands from this server.", and asks "Continue? [y/N]" before it redeems unless `--yes`. Plain http to a loopback or private-range address (RFC 1918, link-local) is allowed; plain http to a public address needs `--allow-http`. A refused code prints "this enrol code cannot be used" and exits non-zero without distinguishing cases. After redeem it prints that it waits for the owner and polls every 2 s until `joined`, `denied`, `expired` or the confirm deadline. `--server-fingerprint` takes the full 64-hex server peer id (preferred) or the short form, which is accepted with a notice that it is a 64-bit pin; a mismatch aborts before anything is written. The server peer id is appended to `allowed-peers` only when the pin matched, the owner confirmed it at the prompt, or `--trust-unverified-server` was given. On delivery it writes `enrol.json` (`{server, node_token, server_peer_id?, name, computer_id, enrolled_ms}`) in the state dir with mode 0600, staging both files first and renaming `enrol.json` before `allowed-peers`, with the previous `enrol.json` read into memory first and put back when the second rename fails (replacing `enrol.json` is one atomic rename, so the file never goes missing, and no backup file is made); the state directory is locked with `.enrol.lock` (`flock`) for the whole run, so two enrols cannot interleave; reads of `enrol.json` and `allowed-peers` refuse a symlink, a non-regular file and a file owned by another user; an `allowed-peers` that is a symlink is never replaced. ember-node is unix-only: the credential file is protected by mode 0600 and the owner check only (no Windows ACL handling). It never prints the token. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] Status: N1 (`ember/node/src/enrol.rs`: reading, validating and atomically writing the files, `append_allowed_peer`, and `config::token_from`) is merged (#609, CI compiled and ran its tests); the hardening of N1 (N1b: state-dir lock, crash-safe replace without a backup file, hardened reads, `config::token_with_source` and the token source log) is merged (#613); N2 (the `ember-node enrol` command in `ember/node/src/enrol_cli.rs`, with the arm in `main.rs`) is merged (#614, CI compiled and ran its tests); the follow-up N2b is written, not yet compiled: a preflight (`enrol::preflight`, run after the lock and before the redeem) refuses what the final write would refuse later (an unreadable `enrol.json` when replacing; an `allowed-peers` that is a symlink, not regular, foreign, over 64 KiB, not UTF-8 or unparseable; a state dir that cannot be written), because a refusal after the delivery loses the token; Ctrl-C (SIGINT), SIGTERM and SIGHUP (the terminal closed, the ssh link dropped) are listened for from BEFORE the code is redeemed; a signal during the redeem request or while waiting for the owner exits with "interrupted; nothing was written on this node. The code may already have been redeemed: ask the owner to cancel the pending computer in Ember, or let it expire." (the server has no cancel route, issue #617), and from the delivery on no signal can kill the process in the middle of the write, which would lose the token; the preflight also refuses an `allowed-peers` that cannot be parsed even when the server later turns out untrusted and the file would not be touched, on purpose: the id is only known after the delivery and refusing early is the safe side; the help text says a code given as an argument shows in `ps` and in shell history, recommends `-` (one line from stdin; stdin is then a pipe, so there is no "Continue?" prompt and no fingerprint question: pass `--yes` and `--server-fingerprint`);  the review follow-ups of N1b (typed write error and the user wording below, the lock file check, the 64 KiB cap on `allowed-peers`, tests `fr_c2_node_write_*` and `fr_c2_node_lock_file_*` in `enrol.rs`, two integration tests in `fr_c2_enrol_node.rs`) came after that CI run and are written, not yet compiled. Not run on two real machines. Final flags: `<code>` (or `-` for one line from stdin), `--server`, `--server-fingerprint`, `--trust-unverified-server`, `--url`, `--transport`, `--state-dir`, `--yes`, `--allow-http`, `--replace`; an unknown or repeated flag, a flag without its value and a malformed pin are errors before any network call. Trust, R1 to R4 as implemented: a pin that matches (full id, or the 16-hex short form with the notice "short pin: 64 bits") trusts the delivered id; a pin that differs from the identity the server proved, or a pin when the server proved none, stops the run at the redeem answer, before any poll (`FR-C2j`, #541, written, not compiled: both fingerprints, "This node asked the server for no credential and stored nothing.", a best-effort cancel of the pending enrolment); a transport id in the credential that is not the proven identity, and a sealed credential that does not open with this run's key (`FR-C2d`, #540), abort with nothing written, the node names the computer the server already registered and tells the owner to remove it in Ember; with no pin, an interactive run without `--yes` asks "Does this match the fingerprint your Ember server prints at start and shows in the app? (n, Enter or Ctrl-D declines) [y/N]", and a non-interactive run or `--yes` trusts only with `--trust-unverified-server` (`--yes` answers "Continue?" and never grants trust); not trusted writes `enrol.json` only and says how to add the id to `allowed-peers`. Exit rules and messages: any failure exits non-zero with `error: <text>` on stderr; a refused code is exactly `this enrol code cannot be used`; a 429 is `too many attempts, wait and try again`; any other answer is `the server did not accept the request (status N)`; `denied`, `expired` and `delivered` ("the credential was already delivered; ask the owner to remove the computer in Ember and mint a new code") stop the run; no answer within the deadline (the server's `expires_ms` plus 30 s, between 1 and 15 minutes) is `timed out waiting for the owner`; 5 network errors in a row stop it. Without a terminal and without `--yes` the run is refused; an existing `enrol.json` is replaced only after a prompt or with `--replace`. The state dir is locked before the first request and the lock is kept to the end. The HTTP client follows no redirects, uses no proxy, times out after 15 s, and reads at most 64 KiB of a response; the code, the poll secret and the node token are never printed, logged or put into an error, and no response body is quoted. The delay between polls is 2 s plus up to 250 ms and doubles up to 30 s on a 429. Not verified: HTTPS (no TLS server in the test harness), two real machines, a real terminal prompt. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] A failed final write says what happened to the token, because the server wipes the credential when it delivers it. `write_enrolment` returns `Result<WriteReport, WriteError>`: `Refused` (nothing changed: validation, an existing file without replace, a symlinked or foreign `allowed-peers`, an `allowed-peers` over 64 KiB, the lock, staging, or the first rename failing) and `RolledBack` (a later rename failed and the previous state is back) print "Nothing was written. The server already handed over this computer's credential once and will not do it again: the token is lost. Remove the computer <name> (<id>) in Ember and enrol again with a new code."; `NewKept` (the undo failed, `enrol.json` holds the NEW enrolment) prints that the token is on disk and names the path; `NotSynced` (both files in place, only the directory fsync failed; nothing is undone) prints that the token is on disk and works but may not survive a crash, then still prints the "accepts commands" lines and the SIGHUP hint when a peer was added, and exits 1; the sentence is said once (the CLI does not print the returned error again). The returned error is the same sentence, with no token and no poll secret. `Outcome.peer_added` is true only when a new line was appended to `allowed-peers`, and the SIGHUP hint is printed only then. Limits: `NodeConfig::from_env` reads `enrol.json` without the lock on purpose (replacing it is one rename, so a reader sees the old or the new complete file); `sync_all` on macOS is fsync and does not issue `F_FULLFSYNC`, so a power loss can still lose the write; `flock` on a state dir on NFS may be advisory-only or unsupported, and the lock is per host; the `.enrol.lock` file must be a regular file owned by this user. A second `enrol` over an existing `enrol.json` asks before replacing it, and a non-interactive `enrol` (`--yes`, no terminal) over an existing `enrol.json` is refused unless `--replace` is given. | **FR-C2i** | Warnings about plain http, shown in both places. The mint answer (and so the app) says: "The enrol door speaks plain HTTP. Anyone on the network path can read the code. The credential is sealed to the node, so reading the traffic does not reveal it; someone who can change the traffic is stopped only by the --server-fingerprint pin in the command. Use it on a trusted network, or put TLS in front of it and set EMBER_ENROL_PUBLIC_URL to the https address." (reworded for sealed delivery, #540, [provisional]) The node prints, for every `http://` server address, before the prompt, one of two texts. For a private address (RFC 1918, link-local, unique-local, `.local`) and any public one it is exactly this text (`PLAIN_HTTP_WARNING` in `ember/node/src/enrol_cli.rs`): "This server speaks plain HTTP. Anyone on the network path can read the enrol code and the poll secret. The credential is sealed to a key that only this run holds, so reading the traffic does not reveal it; whoever reads the poll secret can still fetch the sealed credential first and make this enrolment fail, and someone who can change the traffic is stopped only by --server-fingerprint. Use a network you trust, or an https address." (reworded for sealed delivery, #540, [provisional]) It names the poll secret and the race for the sealed credential as well, so the two texts have the same content but not the same words. For a LOOPBACK address (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `localhost`: the traffic stays inside this machine) the warning is weaker but still printed, never dropped (`LOOPBACK_HTTP_NOTE`, decided by the supervisor 2026-11-06): "This server speaks plain HTTP, but its address is on this machine: the enrol code, the poll secret and the credential travel only inside this machine. Use an https address if the server is not on this machine." The test is `fr_c2_node_cli_a_loopback_server_gets_the_weaker_plain_http_note_others_the_full_warning`. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] The mint answer prints the command only when the door is on. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | The memo lists no test for the wording; the text is fixed here and checked in the security review and in the app step. The node-side refusal for a public address without `--allow-http` is `fr_c2_node_refuses_plain_http_to_a_public_address_without_allow_http`. |
| **FR-C2j** | Residual risks and what removes them, documented in `COMPUTERS.md` and in the user docs. (1) Over plain http the code and the poll secret cross the network in clear. The token does not: it is sealed to the node's one-time key (`FR-C2d`, #540). A sniffer that wins the redeem race still redeems the code for itself (the owner sees its details and denies), and one that polls first after confirm takes the sealed credential it cannot open and makes the node's run fail. TLS in front of the door stays the owner's duty. (2) **The node verifies the server by fingerprint (#541, written, not compiled).** The server's identity is its Ed25519 transport key (`<data dir>/transport.key`), loaded for the enrol flow whether or not the transport is on; the minted command (built in `ember/server`, shown by the app as given) carries its public id as `--server-fingerprint <64 hex>`. The redeem answer carries `server_peer_id` and `server_proof`, the identity's signature over the enrol id and the node's `enrol_key`, made only after the code was accepted. The node verifies the signature and compares the proven id with the pin BEFORE it polls: a wrong fingerprint, a signature that does not verify, half an identity, or a pin against a server that proves none stops the run with no credential asked for and nothing written, prints both fingerprints, and asks the door to cancel the pending enrolment (best effort, `FR-C2m`). A rogue server that claims the real server's public id cannot sign for it, and because the signature covers the node's own key, a machine in the middle that swaps that key is detected as well. What remains: a command run WITHOUT the pin has no such check (a rogue server at the URL can then hand the node its own token; R2 of `FR-C2d` still keeps its transport id out of `allowed-peers`), and a rogue server always receives the code and the node info. Rejected: an identity only when the transport is on (the default server would print no pin), and a separate enrol identity key (a second fingerprint to compare). (3) The owner can confirm the wrong request; the app puts the details in front of the owner. Door auto-close (D12) is `FR-C2l`, on by default. [provisional] (leader task, 2026-11-13; owner confirmation pending) | `fr_c2j_node_enrol_a_wrong_fingerprint_aborts_before_any_credential` (the real door: no poll, nothing written, the enrolment cancelled); `fr_c2j_node_stub_a_rogue_server_that_claims_the_pinned_id_cannot_prove_it`; `fr_c2j_node_stub_a_server_with_another_identity_is_refused_by_the_pin`; `fr_c2j_node_stub_a_bad_proof_aborts_even_without_a_pin`; `fr_c2j_node_stub_a_proven_pinned_server_is_verified`; `fr_c2j_redeem_answer_carries_the_server_id_and_a_proof_for_this_node_key`; `fr_c2j_a_refused_redeem_carries_no_identity_and_no_signature`; `fr_c2j_proof_*` in `ember/node/src/enrol_seal.rs`. All written, not compiled. The threat table in `COMPUTERS.md` lists each residual risk. Sniffing on a real LAN and a real machine in the middle are UNVERIFIED (they need two real machines). |
| **FR-C2k** | Verification on real hardware. FR-C1 and FR-C2 are marked verified only after the owner has run the LAN enrolment between two machines (the observed address against the node's guessed `url`, name resolution, firewall), a real browser confirm in the app, a node behind NAT using the transport peer address, TLS in front of the door on a real proxy, and XFF behind a real reverse proxy. [provisional, 2026-11-06, leader approval relayed by the supervisor; owner confirmation pending] | UNVERIFIED until a person runs the checklist of memo §7 ("Needs two real machines"). |
| **FR-C2l** | Auto-closing enrol door (#539, D12), the default. `EMBER_ENROL_AUTO_CLOSE` unset or `1` turns it on; `0` opts out and the door is open from startup, as before. With it on, the door address is checked by one bind at startup and released, so a bad address still fails the start. The door is then closed while no enrolment needs it. The owner's mint opens it (`DoorGate::open`) before it answers, and the 201 answer says `door: "open"` (`"on"` when the door is always open). If the bind fails, the code just minted is cancelled and the mint answers 503 `door_unavailable`, so no unusable code is shown. An enrolment needs the door while it is `waiting`, `awaiting_confirmation` or `confirmed` (the node still has to fetch its credential); the door closes when none is left: the last code used and its credential fetched, denied, cancelled (by the owner or the node, `FR-C2m`) or expired. A watch checks once a second and at once after an owner cancel or deny; open and close take one lock and the mint writes its row first, so a close never races past a fresh code. Default on because there are no production deployments to protect. [provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13) | `tests/fr_c2l_enrol_door_auto_close.rs`: `fr_c2l_auto_close_is_the_default_and_0_opts_out`; `fr_c2l_door_is_closed_without_a_pending_code_and_opens_at_mint` (the mint answer shows `door: "open"`, the socket accepts); `fr_c2l_door_closes_after_the_last_code_expires` (the socket is released); `fr_c2l_door_closes_after_the_last_code_is_denied_or_cancelled`; `fr_c2l_the_watch_closes_the_door_after_a_deny_nudge`; `fr_c2l_with_auto_close_0_the_mint_says_door_on`. |
| **FR-C2m** | The node cancels its own enrolment (#617). The door serves a third route, `DELETE /enrol/status/{enrol_id}`, with the same `Authorization: Bearer <poll secret>` as the poll and the same order: verify the secret first (`Enrol::verify_poll`, no side effect), then the per-enrolment poll limit (shared with the poll, 429), then the change. A wrong or missing secret and an unknown id give the poll's 404, byte for byte. An `awaiting_confirmation` enrolment moves to `expired` (an existing move, so the owner's app needs no new state) and the owner can no longer confirm it (410); the answer is `200 {"status":"cancelled"}`. Once the owner has answered (`confirmed`, `joined`, `denied`, `expired`) nothing changes and the answer is `409 {"status":<state>}`. `ember-node enrol` calls it on Ctrl-C (SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP) while it waits, best effort with a 3 second timeout (a 429 from the shared poll limit is retried once after `Retry-After`, at most 2 s), and says whether the server cancelled the enrolment or the owner should deny it. [provisional] (leader, 2026-11-13) | `tests/fr_c2_enrol_redeem.rs`: `fr_c2_node_cancel_with_the_poll_secret_stops_the_owner_confirm`; `fr_c2_node_cancel_with_a_wrong_or_no_secret_or_unknown_id_is_the_poll_404_and_changes_nothing`; `fr_c2_node_cancel_after_the_owner_confirmed_is_409_and_the_credential_is_still_delivered`; `fr_c2_node_cancel_shares_the_per_enrolment_poll_limit`; the route and guard matrices (`fr_c2_enrol_door_serves_exactly_the_route_table`, `fr_c2_guard_matrix_is_measured`) treat `DELETE /enrol/status/x` as a route; the expected `Allow` is `GET,HEAD,DELETE`, to be confirmed by the measured line. Node: `fr_c2_node_cli_cancel_sends_the_poll_secret_and_reads_cancelled`, `fr_c2_node_cli_cancel_gives_up_after_the_short_timeout`, `fr_c2_node_cli_the_interrupt_message_says_whether_the_server_cancelled`. |
