## §W: IDE window (VS Code Web, wrapped)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-W1** | The IDE window serves VS Code Web for the session's project and current computer, so that only API and data traffic crosses the network on repeat loads. | Static workbench assets are cached; a cold open on a slow link renders the shell promptly and degrades gracefully. |
| **FR-W2** | The wrapping layer applies CSS/DOM overrides for a native-feeling titlebar and a responsive layout for tablet and phone widths, on an unmodified VS Code Web build. | Implemented today by `ember/proxy/` (the iframe wrapper, overlay, keyboard policy). A CI check diffs the served bundle against its pinned release. |
| **FR-W3** | Native window chrome is suppressed where the injected titlebar replaces it, without losing window controls. | Verified per platform. |
| **FR-W4** | Two runtimes: OSE (DarkPyonix-built from MIT source, Open VSX) by default, and VSC (the user's installed Microsoft build, Microsoft Marketplace) as an option. *[provisional, from `docs/design/INTEGRATION.md`]* Server relay of node files (#456, built for the read-only subset and the content writes): `POST /api/computers/{id}/fs/{op}` for `stat`, `lstat`, `read`, `list`, `readlink`, `glob` and `grep`, with the node's request and response bodies unchanged (`ember/server/src/computers/fs.rs`). The node enforces its allowed roots; the server adds no second check, caps the request body at 1 MiB, and adds no response cap (a read returns at most the node's 16 MiB). Server-side bounds (#467, *[provisional]*): the `glob` and `grep` `limit` is clamped to 1000 (`MAX_SEARCH_LIMIT`, also the node's default) before it reaches the node; at most 8 relayed operations run at once across all computers (a global semaphore, so at most about 8 x 21 MB of read responses are buffered); each operation has a 60 s deadline, permit wait included, and answers 503 when no permit came in time and 504 when the node did not answer in time (the node request is dropped). A client that disconnects drops the handler and with it the node request; the node may finish work it already started. **Node-side bound, decided not built here:** the node collects every glob match before sorting and truncating, so a huge tree still costs node memory and time below the clamp; the node should stop the walk at a match cap (for example 10 x the limit) and say `truncated`. That is node work in `ember/node/src/fs.rs` (a separate change). Path edge cases (NUL, backslash, NFC and NFD spellings, a 5000-byte name, a trailing slash) are answered by the node and relayed unchanged (status and body equal a direct node call); a NUL byte and a file path with a trailing slash are client errors. **Error text is not scrubbed** (decided): an unreachable node's 502 names its URL or address and a node 4xx body may carry absolute node paths. The routes are owner-only (D37) and the owner already knows both; scrubbing becomes required before any scoped or member access (#366). Tests `fr_w4_fs_relay_glob_and_grep_limit_is_clamped_by_the_server`, `_path_edge_cases_pass_the_nodes_answer_unchanged`, `_times_out_with_504`, `_concurrency_is_bounded_by_the_semaphore`, `_client_drop_releases_the_permit` and the unit test `fr_w4_fs_relay_limit_is_clamped`, written, not yet compiled or run. Write relay (#456, *[provisional]*, limited by `INTENT.md` D48): `write`, `pwrite` and `setattr` are relayed the same way (device gate and server token, the shared semaphore and deadline, the node's answer unchanged), because the node opens them first and checks the handle. Their request body is capped at 24 MiB (`MAX_WRITE_BODY`, 16 MiB of data in base64 plus the JSON; the node accepts 64 MiB), a larger body is 413 before the node. A path outside the roots, through a symlink out, or into a denied path (`.ember`) answers the node's status and body; a failed `expect` precondition is the node's 412 with `actual_sha256`. The namespace operations `remove`, `rename`, `mkdir` and `symlink` are not routed (404) until the node covers them with handle checks (D48). Tests `fr_w4_fs_relay_write_pwrite_setattr_reach_the_node`, `_writes_outside_the_roots_or_denied_are_refused_by_the_node`, `_write_precondition_conflict_is_the_nodes_412`, `_write_body_is_capped_at_24_mib`, `_namespace_operations_are_not_routed`, written, not yet compiled or run. Unknown computer 404, the local computer 400, an unreachable node 502, a node error keeps its status and body. The routes are owner routes behind `auth::protect`; the guest door does not serve them. *[provisional]* Tests: `ember/server/tests/fr_w4_fs_relay.rs` (`fr_w4_fs_relay_*`), `fr_t14_guest_door.rs` (not yet run). | Choosing VSC shows an install notice, a copyable install guide, and a command field to verify `code --version` before `code serve-web` is used. |
| **FR-W4a** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` D42, #457]* Per-node OSE lifecycle. Each `ember-node` manages one `dpx-ose-server` process per canonical workspace folder inside its `PathPolicy` allowed roots (outside roots -> 403 `forbidden_path`; missing folder -> 404 `not_found`; missing binary -> 503). Binary resolution: `EMBER_OSE_SERVER`, else the newest `~/.local/share/dpx-ose-*/bin/dpx-ose-server`. Data dir: `<EMBER_OSE_DATA_DIR or <state_dir>/ose-data>` (mode 0700, symlinks refused), passed as `--server-data-dir <dir> --extensions-dir <dir>/extensions`. Connection token: 64-hex random per process, written with mode 0600 (`create_new`, no trailing newline) to `<dir>/tokens/<first 16 hex of SHA-256 of canonical folder>.token`, passed via `--connection-token-file`, and deleted on drop or exit. Environment: cleared except `HOME`, `PATH`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`, `USER`. Reaped when the child exits; killed on node drop. | `fr_w4a_node_ose_spawns_per_folder_under_allowed_roots_and_reuses_live_process`; `fr_w4a_node_ose_refuses_folder_outside_allowed_roots_with_403`; `fr_w4a_node_ose_writes_0600_token_file_and_clears_env`. |
| **FR-W4b** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` D42, #457]* Node OSE routes (all behind the node's bearer token and transport peer allowlist). (1) `POST /ide/ose {folder}` starts or reuses the folder's OSE and answers 200 `{launch_id, placeholder, endpoint}` where `launch_id` is a 32-hex opaque id, `placeholder` is a 64-hex random token distinct from the real token, and `endpoint` is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`. (2) `GET /ide/ose/{launch_id}/relay` (WebSocket upgrade) connects to the launch's `127.0.0.1:<port>` (3 s timeout, 1011 on failure), relays binary frames both ways with backpressure, rejects text frames with 1003, enforces 16 concurrent relays per launch and 64 per node (429), closes on 120 s idle, and replaces every occurrence of the 64-byte ASCII `placeholder` with the real 64-hex token in the first 8 KiB of client-to-OSE bytes (including across frame boundaries) so the real token never leaves the node. (3) `POST /ide/extensions {id, version?}` validates `extension_spec` (400), enforces one install at a time on the node (409) and a 120 s process-group timeout (502 with output capped at 16 KiB), and answers 200 `{installed: true, id, output}`. | `fr_w4b_node_ide_ose_requires_node_token_and_returns_placeholder`; `fr_w4b_node_ide_relay_substitutes_placeholder_and_pumps_bytes`; `fr_w4b_node_ide_extensions_installs_and_enforces_mutex_and_timeout`. |
| **FR-W4c** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` D42, #457]* Server-to-node OSE relay. For a session or request targeting a registered computer `id != "local"`: (1) `GET /api/sessions/{sid}/ide` calls the node's `POST /ide/ose {folder: session.cwd}` over HTTP or the `FR-N5` transport and returns `Target { kind: "ember", available: true, launch: { ..., ose: { endpoint, relay: "/api/computers/{id}/ide/relay/{launch_id}", connection_token: placeholder } } }` (or `available: false, reason` when the node is unreachable or has no OSE binary); (2) `GET /api/computers/{id}/ide/relay/{launch_id}` (behind `auth::protect` on the local listener, `check_origin`, and the device gate on the transport; 404 on the guest door) upgrades and bridges binary frames to the node's `GET /ide/ose/{launch_id}/relay` with `tokio::select!` so revoking a device (`FR-N3`) drops both WebSockets within one heartbeat; (3) `POST /api/computers/{id}/ide/extensions` forwards to the node's `POST /ide/extensions` and preserves its status and body (502 when unreachable). | `fr_w4c_ide_launch_for_node_computer_calls_node_and_returns_server_relay`; `fr_w4c_server_ide_relay_bridges_to_node_and_ends_on_device_revoke`; `fr_w4c_server_ide_extensions_forwards_to_node`. |
| **FR-W5** | On Android and iOS the IDE window works without Node: through a `serve-web`-compatible Rust backend, or directly through web APIs with no backend. [user] | Opens and edits a project on a phone with no Node installed anywhere on the device. Only web-capable extensions run in this mode (`INTENT.md` D10). |
| **FR-W5a** | *[provisional, `docs/design/MOBILE-NO-NODE.md`]* On Android and iOS, "Open IDE → VS Code" opens the session's project on its **current computer**: the IDE window loads that computer's own `serve-web` (OSE or VSC) through the in-app gateway (`FR-W5b`) over the transport (`FR-N5`). Node runs only on the computer. | On a phone with no Node anywhere on the device: open a project, edit and save a file, and the change is on the computer's disk. An extension with only a `main` (Node) entry installed on that computer works. An extension webview renders on a real phone (`FR-N4`). |
| **FR-W5b** | *[provisional]* One Rust **ide-gateway** in the app process serves the IDE window from a loopback origin that the platform webview treats as a secure context, with a second secure origin for `webviewEndpoint`. It reverse-proxies HTTP and WebSocket over transport streams (mode `FR-W5a`) and serves static workbench assets and the working-copy file API (mode `FR-W5f`). | Binds loopback only and rejects requests without the per-launch token. `navigator.serviceWorker` exists in both origins on iOS and Android. No `iroh` type is imported outside the transport crate (`FR-N5`). Wi-Fi ↔ LTE switch keeps the workbench connected within `NFR-N1`'s stall bound. |
| **FR-W5c** | *[provisional]* On Android and iOS, "Open IDE → Ember IDE" opens files of the current computer in the dioxus-compose editor core through ember node file operations (`FR-X1`) over the transport. No webview, no JS engine. | Open, edit and save a remote file from a phone, over a direct path and over the relay. If the file changed on the computer since it was opened, saving reports a conflict instead of overwriting. `NFR-L2`'s webview check covers the editor core. |
| **FR-W5d** | *[provisional]* A project subtree can be made available offline as a **local working copy** in the app sandbox, recording each file's base content hash; the editor core edits it with no network. | In airplane mode: open, edit, save, create and delete files in the working copy; the app restarts with the edits intact. Excluded paths and the size limit are honoured. |
| **FR-W5e** | *[provisional]* When online, working-copy changes sync to a chosen computer of the project; a file changed on both sides is never overwritten. | Edit file X offline only on the phone and file Y on both sides: X is written to the computer; Y is shown as a conflict with a three-way diff, and neither side's content is lost. |
| **FR-W5f** | *[user: `FR-W5` "directly through web APIs with no backend"; form provisional]* Offline VS Code mode: the IDE window boots a bundled, pinned OSE web build with no `remoteAuthority`, so every extension runs in VS Code's own web-worker extension host; the working copy is exposed through a built-in `ember-fs` web extension. Configured only through the embedder API (`IWorkbenchConstructionOptions`); VS Code source is not patched (E4). | In airplane mode on Android: open the working copy, edit, save, and run a web extension (a theme, a grammar, and one language extension with a `browser` entry). The served bundle matches its pinned release (`FR-W2`'s diff check). No Node on the device. |
| **FR-W5g** | *[provisional]* In any no-Node mode, the extensions view says for each extension whether it runs on the phone (`browser` entry) or needs a computer, and extensions without a `browser` entry are not offered for local install. | Classification comes from the extension manifest (`browser`, `main`, `extensionKind`); a `main`-only extension shows "needs a computer" and a one-tap switch to `FR-W5a`. |
| **FR-W5h** | *[provisional]* On iOS, `FR-W5f` (downloaded extension code) ships only behind a flag, enabled after the App Review decision (`MOBILE-NO-NODE.md` Q-M3). `FR-W5a`–`FR-W5e` ship without it. | An iOS build with the flag off downloads and executes no extension code locally. |
| **NFR-W5a** | *[provisional: initial targets, to be adjusted once by the first measurement]* Mobile IDE responsiveness. | On a reference mid-range Android phone and a reference iPhone: `FR-W5a` warm open to editable ≤ 3 s p95 over a direct path; `FR-W5c` remote file open ≤ 1 s p95 for a 100 KB file; `FR-W5f` cold open ≤ 5 s p95; IDE-window RSS recorded per mode. |
| **FR-W6** | vscode-darkpyonix (notebook renderer) and vscode-darkpyonix-theme are installed by default. [user] | Present on first launch for both runtimes. |
| **NFR-W1** | Extensions are tested unmodified from the marketplace matching the runtime. | Regressions block release. [provisional] A fixed list of popular extensions (`ember/ose/regression/extensions.json`) is installed from Open VSX into the OSE build; each must activate without error in the extension host log, as `expected.json` records. A failure not marked `known-fail` fails the run. |

> **`FR-W5a`–`FR-W5h`, `NFR-W5a`** come from `docs/design/MOBILE-NO-NODE.md` (2026-10-03, design only;
> outside the 10-18 deadline). The phone is mainly a client of a computer's own `serve-web` (`FR-W5a`)
> or ember node (`FR-W5c`); offline it edits a local working copy (`FR-W5d`, `FR-W5e`), optionally in
> VS Code Web with web extensions only (`FR-W5f`). **Rejected:** a `serve-web`-protocol-compatible
> server on the phone: the web workbench always opens a remote extension-host connection when a
> remote exists, which E4 forbids us to answer with our own host, and with no Node every extension
> runs in the web worker anyway. Whether this reading of `FR-W5`'s wording is right is open
> (`MOBILE-NO-NODE.md` Q-M1). The build is tracked in #80.

### FR-W4 extension install and data dir (#459, provisional, not yet compiled or run)

Each computer has an OSE data dir, `<EMBER_OSE_DATA_DIR or <EMBER_DATA_DIR or ~/.ember>/ose-data>/<computer id>`
(mode 0700 when Ember creates it; an existing directory keeps its mode and a warning is logged if group or others
can access it; a symlink at the root or the computer dir is refused). With no override and no HOME the data
root does not resolve: the OSE start and the install fail with a message, never a relative path. The dir is
passed as `--server-data-dir` to every OSE the server starts and to every install.
`POST /api/computers/{id}/ide/extensions` with `{"id":"publisher.name","version":"1.2.3"}` runs
`dpx-ose-server --install-extension publisher.name[@version]` against it. The id is
`^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]*\.[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]*$` (uppercase allowed, as in `GitHub.copilot`, never a
leading `-`); the version is `1.2.3[-+suffix]`. No shell, a 120 s timeout that kills the child and its
process group, output capped at 16 KiB, one install per computer at a time (409). Answers 200, 400, 404, 409,
502, 503, 500. Local computer only.

Security boundary:

- The route is owner/admin only (D37): it sits in the token-protected router and is not reachable through the
  guest door.
- The OSE child (install and start) does not inherit the server environment: it gets `HOME`, `PATH`, `LANG`,
  `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR` and `USER` only. `ember/ose/smoke.sh` and `regress.sh` set no variable of their own.
  **Effect of the cleared environment (unverified with a real extension host):** proxy variables
  (`HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY` and their lower-case forms), `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SHELL`, `TERM`,
  `XDG_*` and `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` do not reach the OSE or its extensions, so an Open VSX download behind a proxy
  may fail and an extension that needs one of these finds it unset. **Opt-in pass-through (#471,
  [provisional]):** `EMBER_OSE_ENV_PASSTHROUGH` in the server environment (owner configuration, never a request
  field) is a comma-separated list of extra variable NAMES, default empty. Each valid name that is set in the
  server environment passes to both OSE children (`ose_env` in `ember/server/src/ose.rs`). A valid name is
  `[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*`, or one of the lower-case proxy forms `http_proxy`, `https_proxy`, `no_proxy`,
  `all_proxy`; anything else (a `=`, lower case, a dash) is ignored with a warn and never passed. The owner
  lists what their setup needs, for example `HTTPS_PROXY,https_proxy,NO_PROXY,SSL_CERT_FILE`. `SHELL`,
  `TERM`, `XDG_*` and `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` stay out of the default list until a real OSE run with an extension
  host shows they are needed (unverified); the owner can pass them through the setting. Tests
  `fr_w4_ose_env_passthrough_*` (unit tests in `ose.rs`), written, not yet compiled or run.
- **Known gap, signature verification:** Open VSX installs need `extensions.verifySignature` off in the data
  dir's settings (`ember/ose/smoke.sh` writes it for the Machine and User scopes). The route does not set it and
  does not verify any signature, so an install from the route either fails on the signature check or, with the
  setting off, runs unverified code, and installed extension code later runs in the editor's extension host.
- **The connection token is not on the command line, and never leaves the server (C2, #458, #461).** The OSE start writes the real 64-hex token to
  `<data root>/<computer id>/tokens/<first 16 hex of SHA-256 of the canonical folder>.token` and passes
  `--connection-token-file <path>`, so `ps` shows no secret. The `tokens` directory is made 0700 (a symlink is
  refused); the file is created with `create_new` and mode 0600, so it never exists with wider permissions,
  and holds the 64-hex token without a trailing newline. A stale file at the path is removed first (a
  directory there is refused). The file is deleted when the process entry goes away (child exited, manager
  dropped) and when the start fails. Each process entry also mints an opaque 32-hex `launch_id` and a 64-hex
  `placeholder` token distinct from the real token. `GET /api/sessions/{id}/ide` returns
  `relay: "/api/computers/{computer}/ide/relay/{launch_id}"` and `connection_token: <placeholder>` (never the real
  token). `GET /api/computers/{computer}/ide/relay/{launch_id}` (behind `auth::protect` on the local listener,
  `check_origin`, and the device gate on the transport; 404 on the guest door) validates `{computer}` (`local`;
  unknown computer 404, non-local registered computer 400, invalid id 400) and `launch_id` (unknown 404 before
  upgrade), enforces 16 concurrent relays per launch and 64 per server (429 before upgrade, held by RAII guards),
  upgrades to a binary WebSocket, dials `127.0.0.1:<port>` (3 s timeout, 1011 on failure), pumps binary frames both
  ways with backpressure (text frames from the client close with 1003), closes both sides after 120 s of idle in
  both directions, and in the first 8 KiB (`8192` bytes) of client-to-OSE bytes replaces every occurrence of the
  64-byte ASCII `placeholder` with the 64-byte real token (including across frame boundaries). Revoking a device
  (`FR-N3`) ends its open relay within one heartbeat and drops the TCP connection to the OSE. Tests:
  `fr_w4_ose_token_file_*` in `ember/server/tests/fr_w4_ose_data_dir.rs` and `fr_w4_relay_*`, `fr_n3_ide_relay_*`,
  `fr_t14_guest_door_does_not_serve_the_ide_relay` in `ember/server/tests/fr_w4_ose_relay.rs`. Unverified
  with the real `dpx-ose-server`: that it reads the file once at start and honours it, whether it expects
  or trims a trailing newline (`ember/ose/README.md` only names the flag), and whether `GET /version` answers
  without a token when `--connection-token-file` is set. The `tokens` dir lies inside
  `--server-data-dir`; the OSE is not known to serve or list it (unverified).
- The 200 and 502 `output` is what the OSE printed and can contain absolute paths of the server machine; only the
  owner sees it.
- Tests: `ember/server/tests/fr_w4_ose_data_dir.rs`.
- Unverified: the `@version` form, and `--list-extensions` (not documented in this repository, so there is no list
  route). How several OSE processes behave on one data dir is unknown. A server shutdown or panic does not kill
  a running install or the OSE start's grandchildren (follow-up).

### FR-W4 acceptance: the OSE runtime

OSE is built by `.github/workflows/test-ose.yml` from `ember/ose/` (Code-OSS at the tag in `ember/ose/VERSION`,
`product.json` overrides only; see `ember/ose/README.md`). A release (tag `ose-v*`) is accepted when,
for every target (linux-x64, linux-arm64, darwin-arm64, darwin-x64):

1. **No Microsoft marketplace or telemetry in `product.json`.** `ember/ose/check-product.sh` passes on
   the packaged `product.json`: `extensionsGallery` points at Open VSX
   (`serviceUrl` `https://open-vsx.org/vscode/gallery`, `itemUrl` `https://open-vsx.org/vscode/item`,
   resource/extension templates on `open-vsx.org`); `enableTelemetry` is not true and there is no
   `aiConfig`; no `marketplace.visualstudio.com`, `*.vsassets.io`, `vscode-unpkg.net`, update,
   experiments or voice endpoint appears anywhere. Remaining Microsoft hosts (the webview CDN,
   Copilot doc links) are listed in the job log and in `ember/ose/README.md`.
2. **It launches.** `ember/ose/smoke.sh` starts `bin/dpx-ose-server` with the flags `ember/proxy/dpx` uses
   (`DEFAULT_OSE_ARGS`: `--host --port --without-connection-token --accept-server-license-terms
   --server-data-dir`) and the workbench page is served.
3. **Open VSX search and install work.** `ember/ose/smoke.sh` queries `<serviceUrl>/extensionquery` and
   gets results, and the server CLI installs an extension (`redhat.vscode-yaml` by default) from
   Open VSX.
4. **Manually, once per release, on a Raspberry Pi (64-bit OS) and a Mac:** `python -m dpx.serve`
   with `DPX_OSE_SERVER` set opens the IDE window; the Extensions view searches Open VSX and
   installs an extension; Help → About shows "DarkPyonix OSE" and the Code-OSS version.

Steps 1–3 run in CI on every build; step 1 alone runs on every pull request touching `ember/ose/`.

---

## §P: Persistent work in IDE windows

[user, 2026-10-03: "엠버에서 vscode나 엠버 에디터 같은 경우 그 안에서 실행 중인 작업들은 창을 끄더라도
항상 실행되고 있어야 한다는거 잊지 마. 만약 터미널에서 뭔가를 켜놨다 하면 창 꺼도, 다른 컴퓨터에서 접속해도
그 터미널이 보여야 하는거야."] Applies to both IDE targets that Ember hosts: the VS Code window (VS Code
Web) and the Ember editor.

**Design.** The terminal process is owned by **ember node's persistent PTY session** on that computer,
not by the IDE window, VS Code's pty host, or the client. The IDE is only a client that attaches to it.
This way a window close, a VS Code server restart, or a client switching device does not end it, and
VS Code and the Ember editor see the same terminal. VS Code's own persistent terminals
(`terminal.integrated.enablePersistentSessions`) are not relied on, because a server restart or the
revive timeout ends them.
Design, attach API and VS Code settings: `docs/design/TERMINALS.md`.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-P1** | Terminals, tasks and launched processes started from an IDE window keep running after every window on every device is closed. | In a VS Code window run `sleep 600; echo done`, close the window, and wait 10 minutes. The process is still alive on the computer (visible in ember node's session list) and prints `done`. The same applies to the Ember editor. |
| **FR-P2** | Re-attaching shows the same terminal, from the same device or from any other computer or phone. Scrollback (at least 10,000 lines) and the live input state carry over: the shell prompt, a running TUI, and cursor position. | Start `htop` or a REPL in one window. Close it. Open the IDE on another computer (or phone). The same terminal appears in the terminal list with its full scrollback and the TUI redrawn, and accepts input. |
| **FR-P3** | The same terminal can be attached from several devices and from both IDE targets at once. | A terminal opened in VS Code appears in the Ember editor's terminal list on another device. Output appears on both within 100 ms of each other on a LAN. Built [provisional]: every server WebSocket upgrade (terminal attach, `/api/push`, browser view and DevTools relay) refuses a browser `Origin` that is neither the request `Host` nor loopback with 403, so a web page cannot attach cross-site; no `Origin` (native clients) is allowed (`ember/server/src/api/origin.rs`, `ember/server/tests/fr_p3_ws_origin.rs`; written, not yet run). This is not authentication: who may attach beyond the loopback trust is open with pY. A page served from another loopback port and DNS rebinding are not stopped by it. |
| **FR-P4** | Input from several attached clients: **all attached clients may type** and their keystrokes are serialized by ember node in arrival order. Any client can **take control**, after which other clients are read-only, with a visible "controlled by <device>" banner, until control is released or the controller detaches. The terminal size follows the controller, or the most recently active client when nobody has control. Others see the content at that size, scaled to fit. | Two devices type alternately: the output contains both inputs in order and neither is lost. Device A takes control: device B's keystrokes are refused with the banner shown, and are accepted again after A releases or detaches. Resizing on the controller resizes the PTY. |
| **FR-P5** | VS Code tasks (`tasks.json`) and the Ember editor's run actions run in persistent sessions as well. Debug sessions keep their debuggee process running when the window closes; re-attaching the debugger is best-effort. | Start a long build task, close the window, reopen: the task's terminal is listed with its output and exits normally. A program started under the debugger is still running after the window closes. |
| **FR-P6** | Persistent sessions are listed per computer and per project in the client, with what started them (IDE, agent, user), and can be killed from there. Sessions survive an ember server restart. Sessions survive an ember node restart only if the processes were detached from it (best-effort; documented). | Restart ember server: every terminal is still listed and attachable. The list shows the origin and allows kill. Through the server: `/api/computers/{id}/terms...` relays list, create, kill and attach to the node, including `/api/computers/local/terms...` through the lazy in-process loopback node (`ember/server/tests/terms_relay.rs`, `ember/server/tests/fr_w4_local_terms.rs`, #318, #352, #455) [provisional]. The local node's terminals survive a server restart: records in `<data dir>/local-node`, keepers run as `ember-server __keep-pty`, a restarted server re-adopts and lists them (`ember/server/tests/fr_p6_local_terms_survive_restart.rs`, #26; written, not yet run) [provisional]. |
| **FR-P7** | The node lists the TCP ports in LISTEN state held by processes that descend from a terminal (PTY) or job the node itself started (a process tree walk from the pids it tracks), so the client can show a session's web pages. `GET /ports` answers `{ports: [{port, scope, process, owner}], supported}`: `scope` is `loopback` (127.0.0.0/8 or ::1), `lan` (any other concrete address) or `any` (0.0.0.0 or ::); `process` is the executable name only; `owner` is `{kind: term\|job, id}`. Processes that are not descendants of a node-owned terminal or job (system services, other users, the node itself, ember-server) are never listed. `supported` is false where the node cannot look (platforms other than Linux and macOS), and `ports` is then empty. The route sits behind the node's auth like `/terms`. No pid, command line, environment, working directory or user name is exposed. Best effort: an unreadable `/proc` entry or a failed `lsof` gives an empty or partial list, never a 500. The server relays it as `GET /api/computers/{id}/ports` behind the same auth as the other per-computer routes. **[provisional]** (#386) | In a terminal or job run a listener on `127.0.0.1` port 0. `GET /ports` lists that port with `scope` `loopback` and the terminal or job id. A listener opened by the test process itself is absent, and the body holds no command line text. Unit tests parse `/proc/net/tcp`, `/proc/net/tcp6` and `lsof -F` samples (`ember/node/src/ports.rs`, `ember/node/tests/ports.rs`, `ember/server/tests/fr_p7_ports_relay.rs`; written, not yet run). Linux reads `/proc/<pid>/fd`; macOS runs `lsof`; other platforms answer an empty list with `supported` false (`fr_p7_unsupported_platforms_say_so`). |
| **NFR-P1** | Attach latency and overhead. | Re-attach shows the last screen in ≤ 500 ms on a LAN. Idle sessions cost ≤ 2 MB of memory each in ember node beyond the shell itself. Measured on loopback (`ember/node/tests/term_nfr.rs`, #317, macOS arm64): 50 idle sessions cost 46,530 B (45.4 KiB) of RSS each (2,326,528 B total delta); attach-to-snapshot worst of 10 on `127.0.0.1` is 662.25 µs. Two-machine LAN latency is not measured yet. |

**FR-P5 notes (debugging).** VS Code serves a debug adapter's `runInTerminal` itself and an extension
can neither answer it (trackers only observe; a `DebugAdapterDescriptorFactory` can only be
registered by the extension that defines the debug type), and a *launch* debuggee dies with its
adapter anyway (js-debug's watchdog, debugpy's launcher). So the VS Code companion rewrites a
`launch` with `"console": "integratedTerminal"` into a persistent session running the program with
the debugger listening on 127.0.0.1 plus an **attach** configuration: Node (`node`/`pwa-node`, runtime
`node`, via `--inspect-brk`) and Python (`debugpy`, via `debugpy.listen`). Other adapters and
consoles are unchanged and not persistent. A window opened later offers "Re-attach the debugger"
for this project's running debuggees. Details and the per-adapter table: `docs/design/TERMINALS.md`
§3c.

---

## §B: Native bridge (IDE window webview ↔ native shell)

Unchanged in substance from 09-22; see `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 and `INTENT.md` D11.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-B1** | Injected script detects a tab drag-out gesture without interfering with VS Code's own tab reorder. | A short drag reorders; a long drag detaches; never both. |
| **FR-B2** | On detach, the webview posts file URI, cursor, scroll, selection and source window to the native host through the platform's webview message API. | No version field; an unknown kind is logged, not dropped. |
| **FR-B3** | The native host opens a new IDE window at the same project and computer with that state. | The window appears within one frame of the gesture completing. |
| **FR-B4** | The bridge is bidirectional (native → webview pushes). | Additive fields are always compatible. |
| **NFR-B1** | New IDE window open-to-usable ≤ a plain `serve-web` page load + 100 ms. | p99 on the reference machine. |
| **NFR-B2** | One bridge message ≤ 5 ms p99 encode-to-receipt. | Regression guard. |

**Implementation notes (10-03).** Webview side: `ember/proxy/static/detach.js` (VS Code Web target;
the editor core reuses the schema later). Native side: the `ember/bridge/` crate (`ember-bridge`):
message types, the `WebviewBridge` trait and the detach → open-window step.
No platform webview implementation yet (#30). Details and field reliability: `ember/proxy/README.md`
"Tab detach".

- **FR-B1** "never both" is enforced by deciding at `dragend` only when no VS Code drop target
  accepted the drop (`dropEffect === 'none'`) and the drop is > 48 px from the tab strip or
  outside the window. VS Code's own drag-out-of-window feature
  (`workbench.editor.dragToOpenWindow`) is defaulted off by the proxy; Alt-drag stays VS Code's.
  Dirty, multi-selected and resource-less tabs are not detached. Touch is out of scope until
  a touch drag gesture exists (VS Code's tab drag is HTML5 drag-and-drop).
- **FR-B2** positions are 0-based. `fileUri` is always present; `cursor`, `selection` and
  `scroll` are `null` when the dragged editor is not visible and has no saved view state.
  Additive fields: `workspace`, `screen`, `label`, `editor`, `stateSource`, `sentAtMs`.
  The payload is a JSON string on every platform.
- **FR-B3** without a native shell (plain browser) the fallback opens the same workspace URL
  with VS Code Web's `payload=[["openFile","<uri>:<line>:<col>"],["gotoLineMode","true"]]`:
  the cursor survives, the selection range and scroll do not. The native host loads the same
  URL (`OpenWindow::vscode_web_path`); carrying selection and scroll into the new window is open.
- **NFR-B2** `sentAtMs` on `tab_detach` lets the host measure encode-to-receipt.

---

## §K: DarkPyonix kernel

The kernel, manager and hub contracts are defined in `darkpyonix-core`: `docs/PROTOCOL.md`
(kernel wire protocol), `docs/api/manager.openapi.yaml`, `docs/api/hub.openapi.yaml`,
`docs/FORMAT.md` (`.py` / `.pynb`). Ember does not restate them.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance criteria |
| -- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| **FR-K1** | Ember talks to DarkPyonix only through the `darkpyonix-core` contracts. | A kernel change that keeps the contract needs no Ember change. |
| **NFR-K1** | Ember's own code decides nothing about *what an agent does*; it covers session lifecycle, transport, execution and presentation. | Code-review check. |

---

## §E: Editor core (long-term draft, gated)

Formerly §M. Work has started (M8, #32): `ember/editor-conn` and `ember/editor` exist and their
tests run in CI; the rows stay a draft until the editor opens a project on screen. See
`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/design/EDITOR-CONNECTION.md` and `docs/design/EDITOR-SESSION.md`.

| ID | Requirement (draft) |
| -- | ------------------- |
| **FR-E1** | Compose-native text buffer and cursor/selection model, IME-correct for CJK, reusing `dioxus-compose`'s IME work. |
| **FR-E2** | Diagnostics from unmodified extensions render correctly positioned: squiggles follow edits; the Problems tab lists all markers errors-first; clicking a row opens the file and places the caret at `(line, col)`; rows are capped at `MAX_VISIBLE_PROBLEMS` (200) with an overflow load-more button; closing a file clears its markers. Extension output channels (`MainThreadOutputService`: `$register`, `$append`, `$clear`, `$reveal`, `$dispose`) and notifications (`MainThreadMessageService.$showMessage`) are decoded and surfaced in the bottom Output panel and toast notices. |
| **FR-E3** | CodeLens and hover overlays from unmodified extensions render correctly positioned. |
| **FR-E4** | Inline completion (ghost text) providers work; this is the highest priority within §E. |
| **FR-E5** | Extension webview panels still work, contained to their panel. |
| **FR-E6** | *[provisional; basis: owner D38 "the feature equals the OSE's", leader approval 2026-11-06 of a TextMate interpreter in `ember/editor` with the `onig` crate; #441 folding and semantic tokens]* **Syntax highlighting, semantic tokens and code folding in the editor.** The text is tokenized by an interpreter written for TextMate grammars, with Oniguruma as the regex engine, overlaid with semantic tokens from the extension host when a provider is registered, and the tokens and fold ranges reach the widget as `syntax_spans` and `folds` (`on_fold_change`). Acceptance: (1) the interpreter follows vscode-textmate's rules (begin/end/while, captures, include, injections, `\G`, back references substituted into `end`); (2) golden tests for six languages (TypeScript, JavaScript, Rust, Python, JSON, Markdown) compare the scopes it produces with the tokenization of the real VS Code (vscode-textmate) on the same text and grammar; (3) grammars come only from installed extensions' `contributes.grammars`, no grammar is bundled; (4) a large file is tokenized line by line and incrementally: an edit re-tokenizes from the changed line until the state at a line end equals the previous one, and the recorded time for one edit in a 10,000-line file, and for the first open, is stated in the PR; (5) the Windows, macOS and Linux CI builds link Oniguruma. Both JSON (`.tmLanguage.json`, `.json`) and XML property list (`.tmLanguage`, `.plist`) grammar files are read (with an explicit-stack parser that caps size at 4 MiB, nesting at 64, and elements at 200,000, and refuses DTD internal subsets and non-standard entities), including `source.js.regexp` (`Regular Expressions (JavaScript).tmLanguage`), while YAML grammar files remain refused with `UnsupportedFormat`. Injections are applied as in vscode-textmate: the root grammar's `injections` (a scope selector per rule) and the grammars whose `injectTo` names the root and that carry an `injectionSelector`, with the selector syntax of vscode-textmate (name prefixes, paths, `-`, `|`, `,`, grouping, `L:` and `R:` priority); a selector is input from an extension and is rejected whole over 1024 characters, 512 tokens or 16 levels of nesting, and a grammar has at most 256 injections; the golden tests include a grammar pair that uses every selector form. Session connection (`ember/editor/src/highlight.rs`): (6) when a document opens, or its text is replaced by a reload or a resync, the session reads the grammar of its language id from the catalog through the OSE file channel together with the grammars it includes, at most 40 per language and each read once per session; a read is asked for only at a path inside the contributing extension's folder, a file over 4 MiB is not read (its size is asked first and checked again on arrival), and a failed read is tried again the next time a document of that language opens; (7) tokenizing runs on a thread of its own, never on the session's task or the UI thread, jobs run in order and jobs that queued up are one batch with one answer per document; the queue is bounded, and a job that does not fit makes the document rebuild instead of being lost; (8) the answer carries the coloured runs of the visible lines plus or minus 150 (moved when the visible lines come within 50 lines of the window's edge) and the document version they were written against, and the widget moves the runs along with later typing; an answer for a closed or rebuilt document, for a version the document has not reached, or more than 500 versions behind it, is dropped; (9) colours are switched off for one document, and the editor works as without them, when its language has no grammar, the grammar cannot be read or compiled, or the document is over 8 MiB or 100,000 lines (or grows over it); a line over 20,000 bytes, one with 256 rules open, or one that uses up its search budget (5,000 regular expression searches plus 50 per byte, at most 2,000,000) is left uncoloured from where it stops; (10) a panic in the tokenizer is caught: the documents of that batch go to colours off and the worker restarts; (11) **Semantic tokens** (`ember/editor/src/semantic.rs`): `MainThreadLanguageFeatures.$registerDocumentSemanticTokensProvider` and `$emitDocumentSemanticTokensEvent` are decoded with their `SemanticTokensLegend` (`tokenTypes`, `tokenModifiers`); the session calls `ExtHostLanguageFeatures.$provideDocumentSemanticTokens(handle, uri, 0, token)` (cancellable, debounced across edits, previous result released with `$releaseDocumentSemanticTokens`), decodes the `VSBuffer` binary reply (`Reply::Buffer`, little-endian `u32` header `(id, kind, data_len)` followed by 5-integer delta tuples `(delta_line, delta_start, length, token_type, token_modifiers)`), maps standard LSP/VS Code token types to `SyntaxRole`, and overlays semantic runs onto the TextMate `DocRun`s of the visible window so semantic tokens override TextMate scopes on covered character ranges while leaving uncovered ranges intact; (12) **Code folding** (`ember/editor/src/folding.rs`): every open document computes indentation-based fold ranges (`compute_indent_folds`, matching Code-OSS `IndentRangeProvider`) immediately and also queries any registered `MainThreadLanguageFeatures.$registerFoldingRangeProvider` (`$emitFoldingRangeEvent`, `ExtHostLanguageFeatures.$provideFoldingRanges`), merging provider 1-based ranges (converted to 0-based) with indentation ranges without duplicate `start_line`s (provider wins on a shared `start_line`), capping at 5,000 fold regions, shifting collapsed lines across edits, and wiring `SessionUpdate::Folds` / `DocumentWidgetState::folds` / `WidgetEvent::FoldChanged` to `CodeEditor` props `folds` and `on_fold_change`. The colour of each role is the design system's; the scope-to-role table in `roles.rs` is a first mapping to be tuned with the owner. |
| **FR-E7** | *[provisional, #443; basis: the owner rule of FR-L31 and D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업"]* **Search across files and replace in the Ember 에디터 window.** (1) *Where.* A left-stripe island tool window 검색 (the mockup draws none, so it follows the project window's island rules and the `style.css` tokens). The keymap opens and focuses it: find in files is ⇧⌘F / Ctrl+Shift+F in both keymaps; replace in files is ⇧⌘H / Ctrl+Shift+H (VS Code) and ⇧⌘R / Ctrl+Shift+R (JetBrains), which also shows the replace field. (2) *Engine.* The search is the OSE's own: the Node extension host registers its ripgrep text search provider for the remote scheme (`MainThreadSearch.$registerTextSearchProvider`, because the init data says `remote.isRemote` with an authority), Ember calls `ExtHostSearch.$provideTextSearchResults(handle, session, query, token)` with a Code-OSS `IRawTextQuery` over the workspace folder, and the matches stream back as `MainThreadSearch.$handleTextMatch`. No server or node route is added. (3) *Query.* The pattern with 대소문자 구분, 단어 단위 and 정규식; 포함할 파일 and 제외할 파일 as comma-separated globs, each expanded as Code-OSS's query builder does (`p` becomes `**/p` and `**/p/**`, `./p` is relative to the folder); 제외 설정 및 무시 파일 사용 (on by default) applies `.gitignore` files and Code-OSS's default excludes (`**/.git`, `**/.svn`, `**/.hg`, `**/.DS_Store`, `**/Thumbs.db`, `**/node_modules`, `**/bower_components`, `**/*.code-search`). Open documents with unsaved edits are searched in their current text instead of the disk, as Code-OSS does, with the include and exclude globs applied by Ember. (4) *Results.* Grouped by file (path relative to the folder, match count), each match a row with its line number and a one-line preview with the match marked; a click opens the file in a tab and selects the match. At most 2,000 matches are asked for (`maxResults`; Code-OSS asks for 20,000, but the panel draws every row, there is no virtual list); when the provider reports `limitHit` the panel says "결과가 많아 일부만 표시합니다.". (5) *Cancel.* 중지 and a new search cancel the running one with the RPC cancel of the call (the extension host stops ripgrep); the panel then says the search was stopped and keeps what arrived. (6) *Replace.* With replacement text the rows preview the change (the match struck through, the replacement after it). 바꾸기 on a row replaces that match, on a file all its matches, and 모두 바꾸기 every shown match after a confirmation that names the counts (취소 is the default). With 정규식 the replacement expands `$&`, `$0`, `$1` to `$99`, `$$`, `\n`, `\t` and `\\`. Ember reads each file (the open buffer if it is open, else the disk), finds the matches again with the same options, and replaces only those at the position of a shown result; any other is skipped and counted, so a file that changed since the search is not damaged. A file is written through the `remoteFilesystem` channel keeping its BOM and line endings; an open document takes the new text only after the write succeeded (Code-OSS also saves after a replace). The panel reports how many were replaced and skipped and names each file that failed. (7) Every string is Korean in 합니다체 (`NFR-L4`) and every control has an accessible name. Draft. | Unit tests named `fr_e7_*`: the query JSON and glob expansion, the decoding of `$registerTextSearchProvider` and `$handleTextMatch`, the core's flow (start, stream, done with and without `limitHit`, cancel, no provider yet, results of an older session dropped, unsaved buffers searched instead of the disk), the matcher (literal, case, word, regex, UTF-16 columns), the replacement expansion, replace with re-check and skip, BOM and CRLF kept, the open document updated only after the write, and the panel model (grouping, counts, the cap note, the confirmation text, the Korean strings). Not verified: a real OSE (`ember/editor/tests/live_session.rs` has no search case yet), the rendered window. |
| **FR-E8** | *[provisional, `INTENT.md` D44, #452, owner confirmation pending; basis: D38 "기능은 oss랑 동일하고 디자인은 목업", D9a]* **Webviews, custom editors and notebook outputs without a webview.** (1) Markdown files open a native preview drawn from the document text. (2) Image files (png, jpeg, gif, webp, svg) open in a native image view instead of the `media-preview` custom editor. (3) Notebook outputs of mime `text/plain`, `application/vnd.code.notebook.stdout`, `.stderr`, `.error` (ANSI colours kept) and `image/png`, `image/jpeg` are drawn natively. (4) Any other webview panel, webview view, custom editor or notebook output shows, in its own panel, a placeholder naming the extension and a `VS Code에서 열기` button that opens the project in the VS Code target (`vscode_fallback_url`). (5) The Ember 에디터 window allocates no webview (`NFR-L2` extended to it). Korean strings in 합니다체. Draft. | Unit tests named `fr_e8_*`: the mime classification (native or placeholder), the placeholder text and fallback URL, the markdown and image view models, the window check. Not verified: a real OSE extension opening a webview, the rendered window. |

## Releases

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| **NFR-R1** | *[user, 2026-10-25: "일단 정식 서명 말고 임시 서명으로만 해줘. 클라이언트 앱 배포는 github release에 올리는거 맞고, 배포 형태는 compose-rust가 지원하는 형태 모두 다"]* A tag `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (first: `v0.1.0`, the version in every `Cargo.toml`) publishes a GitHub release with `ember-server` (with `ember-exec`, `ember-a2a`) and `ember-node` (with `ember-term`) for macOS arm64, Linux x86_64 and Linux aarch64, plus `SHA256SUMS`. Tags go only on a `main` merge commit and the owner pushes them. macOS binaries and `.app` bundles are ad hoc signed (`codesign --force --deep -s -`), with no Apple account and no notarization; Windows `.msix` stays unsigned. The client app is released in every format compose-rust's packager supports (FR-22.6 there): macOS `.app` and `.dmg` (`scripts/packager-macos/package-macos`), Windows `.zip` and `.msix` (`scripts/packager-windows/Cargo.toml`), Linux `.AppImage` (`scripts/packager-linux/appimage.sh`), and the Velopack and store formats (`Setup.exe`, `.msi`, `.pkg`, `.deb`, `.rpm`, Flatpak) once compose-rust builds them. The client job is gated by the repository variable `EMBER_CLIENT_RELEASE == 'true'` until `compose-rust` #505 (containing #114 Linux, #115 macOS, #116 Windows packagers) merges to `compose-rust` `develop`. Pushing the `v*` release tag on `main`, setting `EMBER_CLIENT_RELEASE=true`, and any official signing/notarization or external store publication require explicit owner approval (`[OWNER ACTION REQUIRED]`). | `.github/scripts/checks/check-release-workflow.sh` passes in CI (ad hoc signing step, packager paths, and every format present); `ember-node` and `ember-server` build on Windows in CI (#200); a real tag run (`[OWNER ACTION REQUIRED]`) attaches the archives and `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` passes; `codesign --verify` passes on the macOS assets. |
